Lithuania

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Lithuania through the Lithuanian Law on
VAT (in Lithuanian “Pridétinés vertés mokestio jstatymas”). VAT is called in Lithuania “Pridétinés vertés
mokestis” or “PVM”.

1. Taxable Persons

La Registration threshold & SME scheme for domestic taxable persons

A Lithuanian taxable person has the obligation to register as a VAT payer and charge VAT to his customers
starting from the month in which revenue from supplies of goods and provision of services exceeds EUR
45,000 (for the last 12 months), Where a person controls (alone or with other related persons) several legal
entities, all these entities must register for VAT purposes, if their joint turnover exceeds the EUR 45,000
threshold (even though the turnover of the individual entities does not exceed the said threshold). The rute
regarding VAT registration of related persons does not apply in case the related persons can prove that none
of their management bodies and/or individual members of their management bodies are the same person and
their actual economic activities are not of the same nature, and they do not act for each other’s benefit or
interests.

Lithuania is planning to implement the SME scheme as from | May 2025 (although the draft law has not yet
been been approved by the Parliament). According to the proposed new rules, taxable persons whose annual
turnover does not exceed EUR 45,000 in the current and in the previous calendar year will be eligible for the
scheme, No sectoral threshold will be introduced in Lithuania.

From | May 2025, Lithuania is also planning to introduce a VAT registration requirement for Lithuanian
taxable persons acquiring or supplying services that fall under Article 44 of VAT Directive 2006/112/EC from
or to other EU taxable persons, with no registration threshold applied. Currently, Lithuanian taxable persons
are required to account for VAT under the reverse charge mechanism but have no obligation to register for
VAT. It should also be noted that the new VAT registration requirement is planned to apply only in the case
of the supply and purchase of services to/from taxable persons established in other EU countries. The rules
regarding purchases from outside the EU will remain unchanged—there will be no requirement to register for
VAT, but there will still be a requirement to apply the reverse charge mechanism.

Furthermore, a Lithuanian taxable person or a non-taxable legal entity acquiring goods from another EU
Member State must also register for VAT, if none of exemptions indicated in Section 3.5 below applies.

The registration application to register with the VAT Payers’ Register must be filed with the local tax
authorities before the revenue exceeds the threshold.

VAT registration application can be submitted only online via Lithuania’s State Tax Inspectorate’s online
service system “Mano VMI”,

The decision on registration (or non-registration) is issued within 3 business days from the day on which the
competent tax authorities receive the application. The deadline can be extended in case the tax authority needs
additional information. If at the request of the tax authorities additional information is to be provided, the
decision on registration is issued within 3 business days after the receipt of the additional information.

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Currently, there is no registration threshold for foreign taxable persons. A foreign taxable person has

the obligation to register for VAT before performing the first transaction subject to Lithuanian VAT. The
obligation to register does not arise in case of the supply of the goods and services for which the reverse
charge mechanism applies, or if the supply is VAT exempt or taxed with the zero rate VAT (except export and
intra-Community supply of goods).

Lithuania | 361

--- Page 2 ---
Lithuania is planning to implement the SME scheme as from 1 May 2025 (although the draft law has not yet
been approved by the Parliament). According to the proposed new rules, taxable persons established in other
EU Member States whose annual turnover does not exceed the Lithuanian domestic threshold of EUR 45,000
in the current and the previous calendar year, and who do not exceed the EU threshold of EUR 100,000 across
all Member States in the current and the previous calendar year, will be eligible for the scheme. No sectoral
thresholds will be introduced in Lithuania,

‘Taxable persons wishing to apply the SME scheme in Lithuania should register for it in their Member State
of establishment and must hold a valid VAT identification number with the suffix “EX” at the end (“EX
number”),

Taxable persons established outside the EU will be required to register for VAT from the beginning of their
activities in Lithuania, as no VAT registration threshold will apply for them.

Furthermore, a foreign taxable person or a non-taxable legal entity acquiring goods from another EU Member
State must also register for VAT, provided that none of exemptions indicated in Section 3.5 below applies.

VAT registration application can be submitted only by electronic means (online) via Lithuania’s State Tax
Inspectorate’s online service system “Mano VMI” (My STI). It should be noted that before submitting VAT
registration application, foreign taxable persons must first register in the Register of Taxpayers by submitting
Form FRO227.

EU companies may choose in which county they wish to be registered. However, if the company has a
branch, a fixed establishment, owns real estate in Lithuania or pays social security contributions as an
employer, the company must register in the county where the branch, fixed establishment, real estate or the
employee is based.

The following documents must be submitted to the STI when registering for VAT purposes:

+ Application to register with the Taxpayers’ Register (form FRO227);

+ Application to register with the VAT Payers’ Register (online application); and

+ Commercial register extract issued by the competent authority of the country of establishment, approved
with the Apostille and translated into Lithuanian language.

‘The registration application form (FRO227) can be downloaded at the following link: hitps://inyurl.com/43)3dce7

The decision on registration (or non-registration) with the Taxpayers’ Register is issued within 5 business
days from the day on which the competent tax authorities receive the application, meanwhile the registration
with the VAT Payers’ Register takes 3 business days from the day on which the information is provided to the
STL If at the request of the tax authorities additional information needs to be provided, the decision on VAT
registration is issued within 3 business days from the receipt of the additional information.

1.3 VAT Grouping
There is no VAT grouping in Lithuania.

1.4 Fiscal Representative

A foreign taxable person established outside of the EU must register with the VAT Payers’ Register through its
Lithuanian fixed establishment, if any, or through a fiscal representative. Foreign taxable persons established
within the EU are entitled to register with the VAT Payers’ Register directly (i.e. without appointment of the
fiscal representative). The VAT Law provides for a relief from appointing a fiscal representative for foreign
taxable persons established outside of the EU as well, provided that in the foreign country, rules on mutual
assistance analogous to the rules provided in EU Council Directive 2010/24/ES and Council Regulation
904/2010 apply. However, currently only companies based in Norway are treated by the STI as complying
with the aforesaid and, thus, subject to the relief from appointing a fiscal representative. Meanwhile, all other
foreign persons established outside of the EU can only register with the VAT Payer’s Register through a fixed
establishment or a fiscal representative.

362 | Lithuania

--- Page 3 ---
A taxable person may be a fiscal representative of a foreign taxable person if the following conditions are

met:

+ the representative has been registered with the VAT Payers’ Register for no less than 3 years, This
requirement does not apply to audit firms and attorneys at law:

* the representative provides services in the following fields: law, accounting, audit or tax consulting:

+ the representative has had no tax arrears in the past 12 months;

+ the representative has not breached customs laws in the past 12 months; and

+ the representative has not committed any other financial offences.

The fiscal representative is jointly and severally liable for the payment of all taxes due, as well as default
interest and fines, together with the represented foreign taxable person.

A person can appoint only one fiscal agent to fulfil VAT obligations.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Lithuania applies the option to exempt the transfer of business as a going concern from VAT (Articles 19 and
29 of the EU VAT Directive).

If the taxable person transfers a business (or part thereof) to another taxable person as a going concer,

this transaction is not considered as a supply of goods or services and, thus, falls outside the scope of VAT,

provided that the following conditions are met:

* the activities are transferred as a complex of assets, liabilities, rights and obligations related to the said
activities;

+ the activities are transferred from one taxable person to another; and

+ _ the activities are continued by the transferee after the transfer.

Although the transfer of a business that meets these legal conditions is not taxed with VAT, there are VAT
consequences, The taxable person that takes over the business of the transferring entity takes over all rights
and obligations, claims, liabilities and responsibilities for VAT such as the remainder of the adjustment
periods for (im)movable tangible property.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Private use of goods by a VAT registered person shall be considered to have occurred when the VAT payer

has deducted VAT on the purchase or import of goods (or goods and services used in the production of goods)

and:

+ the goods are transferred free of charge, granting the recipient or a third party ownership rights similar to
that of an owner; or

+ the goods are consumed in any other manner that would not qualify for VAT deduction under the law.

‘The transfer or use of goods by a VAT-registered person is not considered private use if the goods are used as
samples for examination, testing, or analysis, provided they are part of the person’s regular business activities.
Additionally, goods given as low-value gifts for purposes like advertising, entertainment, charity, or support
are also excluded from being treated as private use.

There are different value limits depending on the situation. For example:
+ Business gifts for representation purposes: up to EUR 75 (excluding VAT); or
+ Gifts given to customers who have purchased other goods: up to EUR 15 (excluding VAT).

Other specific situations may have different limits.

Lithuania | 363

--- Page 4 ---
2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by intra~-Community supply

The importation of goods may be exempt from VAT if the importation will be followed by intra-Community
supply of the same unaltered goods within 7 days from the moment of importation. The exemption only
applies to importers that are VAT registered in Lithuania at the time of lodging the import declaration and the
importer can provide evidence that the goods will be intra-Community supplied (e.g. copies of contracts with
the customer, transport company etc.).

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Postponed accounting on importation is available in Lithuania and may be used by Lithuanian VAT taxable
persons (including persons not engaged in economic activities but VAT registered due to acquisitions of
goods and/or services from outside of Lithuania). When applying the postponed accounting on importation,
VAT dogs not have to be paid to the customs authorities at the time of importation but must be reported in the
monthly VAT return before the 25" day of the month following the month in which the goods were imported.

a Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

The transfer of goods by a VAT registered taxable person from one EU Member State to Lithuania to create a
stock of goods from which their customer can ‘call-off (use and pay for) the goods as and when they require
them, can be described as call-off stock. If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a transfer
of own goods followed by a local supply, but an intra-Community supply to his customer (at the moment
when ownership of the goods is transferred to the customer). The amendment to the Law on VAT of Lithuania
was adopted in October 2019 and introduces all the relevant changes to the VAT Directive with respect to
Quick Fixes.

‘The transfer of ownership of the goods from the call-off stock located in Lithuania is treated as an intra-

Community supply by the foreign supplier and intra-Community acquisition by the local customer rather than

transfer of own goods followed by local supply by the foreign taxable person (the supplier), provided that the

following conditions are met:
the supplier (or a third party at its order) transports the goods to Lithuania, and the contract foresees the
transfer of the right to dispose of the goods as owner to the customer within 12 months as of delivery of
the goods into Lithuania;

+ the taxable person acquiring the goods in Lithuania is a duly registered VAT payer in Lithuania;

+ the supplier is not established in Lithuania (i.e. its registered office (in case of a non-natural person)
or the permanent place of residence (in case of a natural person) is not in Lithuania or it has no fixed
establishment here; and

+ the supplier shall register the transport of the goods in an appropriate register established by the
corresponding Member State, and shall specify the identity and VAT code of the taxable person acquiring
the goods in the report on supplies of goods and / or provision of services to other Member States (e.g.,
VIES report).

As a result, the foreign taxable person is not obliged to register with the VAT Payers’ Register in Lithuania.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

When the supplied goods have to be assembled or installed by the supplier or another person acting on behalf’
of the supplier, the place of supply is in Lithuania if the assembly or installation takes place in Lithuania,
There is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly” in the VAT Law. Reverse
charge is applied to supplies of goods with installation and assembly where the supplier is a foreign taxable
person (not established in the territory of Lithuania) and the customer is VAT registered in Lithuania.

364 | Lithuania

--- Page 5 ---
3.3 Distance sales

From 2021, the rules and thresholds for distance sales, including both intra-Community distance sales and
distance sales of imported goods, have been standardized across the EU with no exceptions for Member
States. The distance selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT
registration number (e.g,, private individuats) is now EUR 10,000 for intra-Community distance sales.
‘Turnover generated from the supply of ‘electronically supplied services,” telecommunication services, and
broadcasting services are included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment
applies for the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Under the Lithuania Law on VAT, the simplified triangulation rules have mostly been implemented in line
with the EU VAT Directive. Under the VAT Law (among other conditions) the simplified triangulation rules
may be applied if the following conditions are met:

+ all three parties participating in the triangulation (the so-called ABC transaction) are registered for VAT
purposes in different EU Member States and carry out the ABC transaction using VAT identification
numbers granted in different EU Member States;

+ the transport is arranged by either A or B; and

+ the goods are transported directly from A to C.

Although Lithuanian VAT legislation does not explicitly state that an invoice should indicate that the special
triangulation scheme applies, it is highly recommendable to include this information according to the
European Court of Justice (ECI) case law (C-247/21 Luxury Trust Automobi)

35 Acquisition threshold

Acquisition threshold for acquisitions of goods from other EU Member States by non-taxable legal persons,
taxable persons engaged in exempt activities and flat-rate farmers is EUR 14,000.

If the threshold is not exceeded, businesses may opt to register for VAT voluntarily.

3.6 Effective use & enjoyment

Lithuania has exercised the option provided in Article 59a of the VAT Directive, stipulating that transport
services starting and ending outside the EU, when supplied to a taxable person established in Lithuania,

are not treated as supplied in Lithuania, This rule also extends to ancillary transport services and the hire of
means of transport.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied. For goods, this is the date when they are placed at the disposal of the acquirer.

A supply of goods involving their transportation is deemed to be carried out at the date the transport
commences. A supply of service is deemed to be carried out at the date the service is finally executed.

These principles, however, are overruled in case of the obligation to issue an invoice, VAT becomes
chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice. Even if the invoice is issued before the taxable transaction take
place.

For intra-Community acquisition of goods, the VAT becomes chargeable upon the issue of the invoice by the

supplier, latest on the 15" day of the month following the dispatch of the goods, if no invoice has been issued
by that time.

Lithuania | 365

--- Page 6 ---
For supplies subject to the reverse charge mechanism, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice,
latest on the last day of the month following the supply, if no invoice has been issued by that time. The VAT
on the purchase of a service, which falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT Directive,
is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

42 Advance payments

Where an advance payment is made before the goods or services are supplied, and provided that, under the
terms and conditions of the relevant transaction, the goods will be transferred or all the services provided

not earlier than after 12 months from the date of the transaction, VAT becomes chargeable on receipt of the
payment, Thus, VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the
service is completed, This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods
are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied.

43 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or
transported from Lithuania to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred VAT exempt,
shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to
an end.

Services ongoing over a period of more than 1 year, that do not give rise to statements of account or payments
in that period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of Article 196 EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each full calendar year up to the time the provision
of the services ceases.

4.4 Cash accounting

The Lithuanian VAT Law generally does not provide an option of cash accounting for VAT purposes. In this,
regard only one specific case is allowed, where an obligation to account for VAT arises on receipt of payment
for the goods or services supplied. In particular, the so-called ‘cash accounting scheme” may only be applied
to the supplies of agricultural products.

In particular, only a VAT registered person (farmer) who supplies agricultural products and the supply

of which must be formalised under a VAT invoice, has a right to opt for the cash accounting principle by
submitting an application to the State Tax Inspectorate allowing them to calculate and pay VAT to the budget
only when the payment for their supplied agricultural goods is made, i.e. instead ofa general rule - when the
VAT invoice is issued. The cash accounting principle may start to be applied as regards the calculation of VAT
only from the next tax period following the date of the application. It is also noteworthy that the issued VAT
invoice must have an explicit reference “Cash accounting scheme”.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad Debt Relief

The amount of payable output VAT may be reduced by VAT attributable to bad debts. A consideration is
treated as a bad debt if it is not received from the purchaser of goods or services for a period exceeding one
year afier the supply, provided that the output VAT was reported in VAT return and supplier can prove the
efforts to recover the debt.

5.2 Cash DiscountsAssume that the supplier sends the following invoice:
“Supply of goods BUR 1,000
21% VAT EUR 210
Total to be paid EUR 1,210

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

366 | Lithuania

--- Page 7 ---
There are no special explanations of the tax authorities on cash discounts and thus the general rules relating
to granting of discounts after the supply has taken place and VAT is reported have to be followed. Based on
the said rules, the supplier shall issue a credit note. Furthermore, if the discount is granted in another taxable
period, both the supplier and the customer must respectively report the credit note in the VAT returns of the
month in which the discount was granted (i.e. the supplier needs to reduce the taxable amount and amount of
output VAT and the customer needs to reduce the deductible input VAT).

5.3 Open Market Value

In cases where the tax administrator has a reasonable suspicion that the taxable amount of goods or services
supplied has been artificially reduced or increased, the tax administrator has the right to calculate the taxable
amount. The taxable amount of goods or services supplied may be considered artificially reduced or increased
in the case where upon giving due consideration to all terms and conditions of the transaction, the taxable
amount does not correspond to the market value of the goods or services.

lt should be noted that the tax administration has the right to (re)calculate the taxable amount only if the 2
following conditions are met:
* the taxable amount is:
* much lower than the open market price, and the customer does not have right to VAT deduction or has
the right only to partial VAT deduction;
+ much higher than the open market price, and the supplier does not have right to VAT deduction or has
the right only to partial VAT deduction;
+ there is reason to believe that the taxable value of goods and/or services has been artificially reduced
or increased for the purpose of tax benefits, and the transaction, the taxable value of which has been
artificially reduced or increased, was concluded between related persons.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

The value of packaging in which the goods are supplied (bottles, crates, containers or other packaging
indispensable for holding the goods) shall be excluded from the taxable amount of the supplied good only

in the case where there is an agreement between the seller and purchaser to return the packing to the seller.
However, if the packing is not returned within 12 months, the taxable amount calculated for the goods by the
seller must be increased by the amount equal to the value of such packaging not returned.

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Lithuania is 21%

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced rate of 9% VAT is applicable to:

+ heating energy supplied for the heating of residential premises (including heating energy transmitted via
hot water supply systems), hot water supplied to residential premises or cold water supplied to residential
premises for preparation of cold water, and heat energy consumed for the heating up of such water;

+ accommodation services supplied in accordance with the laws governing tourism activities;

+ printed and electronical books, non-periodical information publications (including textbooks, workbooks,
encyclopaedias, dictionaries, manuals, information leaflets, photo and reproduction albums, children’s
picture books, drawing and colouring books, printed or manuscript sheet music, maps, layouts and
graphics, but excluding calendars, memo books and similar publications);

+ firewood and wood products intended for burning and purchased by household energy consumers as
defined in the Law on Energy of the Republic of Lithuania; and

+ transportation of passengers and their luggage by regular routes.

The reduced rate of 5% VAT is applicable to:
+ print and electronical newspapers, magazines and other periodical publications excluding:

Lithuania | 367

--- Page 8 ---
+ publications of erotic and/or violent nature or publications failing to comply with professional ethics,
recognised as such by an institution authorised under the law;
+ publications in which paid advertising accounts for more than 4/5 of total area of the publication.

+ supply of medicines and medical aid equipment when such supply is made to persons to whom the
acquisition costs are compensated in whole or in partial pursuant to the Law on Health Insurance as well
as to prescription drugs where the costs of acquisition are not compensated pursuant to the Law on Health
Insurance.; and

+ supply of technical aid for disabled people and maintenance thereof.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rates

There are no super reduced and zero rates other than the ones mentioned in paragraph 7.1 in Lithuania.

7. Exemptions

TA Exemptions with the right of deduction

In Lithuania 0 % VAT rate is applicable to:

+ export of goods;

+ intra-Community supply of goods;

* tax free shopping (goods the value of which does not exceed a certain amount set by the Lithuanian
Government exported by persons who have their permanent residence outside the EU;

+ supply, maintenance, repairs, modification, modernisation and hiring out of seagoing vessels used for
transportation of passengers or goods on international routes or for other services for consideration, of
seagoing fishing vessels and of vessels used for rescue or assistance at sea;

+ supply and hiring out of aircraft to taxable persons who receive more than 50% of their annual income
from transporting passengers and (or) cargo on international routes and related services;

+ equipment supplied to vessels and aircrafts mentioned above, as well services for direct maintenance and
needs of the ships and aircrafts mentioned above;

+ fuelling and provisioning of the ships and aircrafts mentioned above;

+ transportation and other services directly related to export and import of goods;

* — transportation of passengers and their luggage on international routes;

* insurance and financial services directly linked to the exportation of goods;

* goods and services intended for use of diplomatic missions, consular institutions, EU institutions and
international organisations (some exceptions apply);

+ goods and services for the units of the armed forces of members of NATO (some exceptions apply);

* — supply of goods and services to European Commission or agencies/institutions established by EU
institutions that relate to the management of COVID-19;

+ gold destined for the System of the European Central Banks and the European Central Bank;

«the supply of COVID-19 vaccines and COVID-19 in vitro diagnostic medical devices (unti] December 31
2022);

+ goods supplied to Lithuanian recipients of sponsorship who transport them as sponsorship outside the EU;

+ servicing and processing of movable property that will be exported outside the EU (some exceptions apply);

+ goods and services for disaster victims (some exceptions apply); and

+ goods and services under customs control (e.g. goods are placed in a temporary storage, goods for which
inward processing customs procedure applies and ete. .

In all the cases the supplier must hold evidence and supporting documentation that prove the 0 % rate.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

In order to apply 0 % VAT rate for of intra-Community supplies the following conditions should be met:

* the power to dispose of the goods as an owner is passed to the customer;

= the customer has provided a valid VAT identification number of a EU country other than a VAT number of
the country of departure;

+ the goods are physically dispatched from one Member state to another and there is a proof of
transportation;

368 | Lithuania

--- Page 9 ---
+ the goods dispatched/transported by or on behaif of the supplier or the person acquiring the goods; and
+ the supplier has reported the supply in the country of departure (VIES declaration).

If any of these conditions are not met, the tax authorities may remove the exemption and make the supply
subject to VAT.

It is recommended that the supplier has the following evidence:

+ original VAT invoice;

+ validated VAT identification number of the customer;

* transport documentation (bill of lading / CMR and etc.) signed by the customer on receipt of the goods;
+ invoices from the provider of the transport service;

+ proof of payment; and

+ correspondence with the customer.

It should also be noted that Lithuanian tax authority allows to apply 0% VAT rate to intra-Community
supplies and export of goods only in case the transport was arranged by the supplier or the first customer,
therefore it is also recommendable to have evidence that allows to identify the person who was responsible
for the transport arrangements.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to apply 0 % VAT rate for export transactions, the following conditions should be met:

+ the goods are physically dispatched from one Member state to a destination outside the EU and there is a
proof of transportation;

+ the goods are dispatched/transported by or on behalf of the supplier or the person acquiring the goods;
and

+ in case the goods are dispatched/transported by or on behalf of the customer, the customer must not be
established/must not have a fixed establishment in Lithuania.

It is recommended that the supplier has the following evidence:

+ original VAT invoice;

+ transport documentation (bill of lading / CMR and etc.) signed by the customer on receipt of the goods:

+ copy of export documentation (e.g. [E599 form or analogous document confirming that the goods have
left the territory of the EU);

» invoice from the provider of the transport service;

+ proof of payment; and

+ correspondence with the customer.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.13.1 VAT warehouse

It should be noted that the concept of a VAT warehouse under Lithuanian VAT law differs from its

interpretation in the VAT Directive. According to the Lithuanian VAT law, there is a possibility to store goods

in a VAT warehouse provided that the goods supplied, imported or acquired from another EU Member State

are intended for:

+ selling in a special shopping area of an international seaport or airport for the passengers who are
departing to third territories or third countries; or

* supplying to the passengers travelling by air or sea to a destination outside the territory of the European
Union in the course of the transportation.

Payment of VAT for the goods held in the VAT warehouse is suspended.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Excise goods under suspension of excise duties can be stored in an excise warehouse. Lithuania does not
provide any special rules for excise goods in excise warehouses; therefore they are taxed in the usual manner.

A

IL is possible to store goods under a bonded warehouse regime, The supply of goods, when the goods are put
into the customs warehouse, is subject to zero rate VAT in Lithuania. General conditions stipulated in the

Lithuania | 369

3 Bonded warehouse

--- Page 10 ---
EU Customs code and governing application of the respective customs procedure apply to putting/storing
the goods into a bonded (customs) warehouse. The import VAT becomes due when the goods are taken out
of the customs (bonded) warehouse and released for free circulation. The person who initiates the release is
generally responsible for VAT.

Supplies of goods that are stored in the customs warehouse are subject to VAT under the general rules.
However, a zero rate applies to supplies of goods that are stored in the customs warehouse and due to the
supply, the goods are released for free circulation. In the latter case import VAT becomes due upon release of
the goods.

72 Exemptions without the right to deduction

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

The following supplies are exempt without the right of deduction:

+ supply of goods and services related to healthcare;

+ supply of social services and related goods;

+ supply of education and professional training services;

+ supply of cultural and sport services;

+ supply of postal services;

+ supply of information to the public via radio and television;

+ supply of services by religious communities, associations and centres for the benefit of their members;

+ services supplied to their member by political parties, trade unions and other non-profit making legal
persons set up and operating on the membership basis;

+ certain supply of staff by religious communities, associations and centres;

+ supply of services and of goods closely linked to the protection of children and young persons; and

+ supplies of goods and/or services by non-profit making legal persons during charitable and sponsorship
events organised by the said persons (including sale of tickets to the events). Special conditions apply.

7.2.2. Other exemptions

The following other supplies are exempt without the right of deduction:

+ supply of insurance services,

+ supply of financial services;

+ postal prepayment impressions and other special marks;

+ betting, gambling and lotteries;

+ supply of services by independent groups of persons, whose members are persons carrying out an activity
referred to in chapter 2 of Title IX of the EU VAT Directive, to their members; and

+ letting, sale and other transfers of immovable property.

7.3 Option for taxation

There is a possibility under certain conditions to opt to tax supplies of certain financial services, as well as
lease and sale of immovable property.

The option to tax is only valid if the customer is a taxable person registered for VAT purposes. The option
is compulsory for all analogous supplies to be carried out within 24 months {rom the date the option is first
exercised.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In Lithuania there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Lithuanian VAT number
established in Lithuania, This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44
and 196 BU VAT Directive.

370 | Lithuania

--- Page 11 ---
In addition, there is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-
established foreign suppliers to ‘taxable dealers’ in Lithuania,

8.1.1 General reverse charge ~ non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

When services fall under the main rule on the place of supply as defined in Article 44 of VAT Directive
2006/112/EC, the reverse charge mechanism applies if these services are provided by a non-established
taxable person to a taxable person resident or established in Lithuania. In such cases, the Lithuanian recipient
of the service is responsible for calculating and paying VAT on the reverse charge basis. This obligation
applies regardless of whether the Lithuanian taxable person is VAT-registered or not.

‘The reverse charge mechanism also applies to supplies of goods that are installed or assembled in Lithuania
by a non-established foreign taxable person and supply of natural gas, electricity, heating or cooling energy.
In these cases reverse charge applies if the customer is registered for VAT in Lithuania.

The reverse charge rule also applies when a non-established taxable person supplies goods or services in
Lithuania and was required to register for VAT (e.g., services related to real estate) but failed to do so. In such
cases, if the customer is a taxable person registered for VAT purposes in Lithuania, he becomes responsible
for paying the VAT on the reverse charge basis. However, this rule only ensures that VAT is paid in Lithuania
and does not remove the supplier’s obligation to register for VAT in Lithuania. In practice, the tax authority
does not require the non-established supplier to pay additional VAT, as it has already been accounted for by
the Lithuanian taxable person under the reverse charge mechanism. Nevertheless, the requirement to register
for VAT in Lithuania remains in place.

The invoice should contain a reference to application of the reverse charge mechanism (either a statement
“AtvirkStinis PVM / Reverse charge’ or a reference to Article 95 of the VAT Law or Article 44/196 of the EU
VAT Directive 2006/112/EC).

8.1.2 Specific domestic rules (Article 199 Directive)
The application of the reverse charge mechanism varies depending on the circumstances.

In some cases, the reverse charge applies if the customer is VAT-registered, even if they are not engaged in
economic activities. For instance, a state institution that is VAT-registered solely due to intra-community
acquisitions but does not conduct any economic activities may be required to apply the reverse charge.

However, in other situations, mere VAT registration is not sufficient—the customer must also be engaged
in economic activities. This means that if'a state institution is VAT-registered but does not carry out any
economic activities, it will not be required to apply the reverse charge.

A VAT payer registered in Lithuania must account for VAT on a reverse charge basis for the following

supplies made to him:

+ for assets received as a contribution in kind or through a reorganization: This applies when a VAT-
registered person takes over assets either as a contribution in kind or as part of a business reorganization
from another VAT-registered person; or

» the return of materially improved building or structure, which was used on a basis other than ownership,
to its owner before the end of the VAT adjustment period.

A taxable person registered in Lithuania must account for VAT on a reverse charge basis for the following

supplies made to him:

* construction works;

+ ferrous or non-ferrous metals scrap or materials resulting from recycling thereof; and

* mobile phones, tablets, laptops (current legislation indicates that this rule applies until 31 December
2026).

The supplier must refer to the reverse charge rule in his issued invoice. The following text must be mentioned
on the invoice: *AtvirkStinis PVM / Reverse charge’ or a reference to Article 96 of the VAT Law’.

Lithuania | 371

--- Page 12 ---
8.2 Joint and several liability

Although the legal acts regulating VAT do not directly provide for any provisions on joint and several liability,
there have been cases in court practice where the tax liability was transferred to the shareholder of the company.

O; Recovery of input VAT

9.1 Non-recoverable input VAT

Input tax may not be recovered on the purchase of goods and services that are not used for VAT taxable
economic activities (for example goods purchased for private use by the taxable person) or for activities for
which an exemption without the right to deduction applies.

In addition, input VAT cannot be recovered if it is:

+ — input or import VAT on goods and services intended for representation, where according to the legal acts
on taxation of profit (income) the purchase costs are not classified as representation costs as well as 50
percent of input and/or import VAT on goods and services intended for representation where according to
legal acts on taxation of profit (income) the purchase costs are classified as representation costs;

* — input or import VAT on a passenger car designed for the transportation of no more than 8 persons
(excluding the driver), or a motor vehicle of the said class attributed to the category of cross-country
vehicles, provided that the car will not be supplied or leased, or it is not used to provide services of
passenger transport for consideration. The same restrictions shall apply to the deduction of input VAT
on the lease of cars of the specified categories. These restrictions do not apply to class MI electric cars,
if the value of the class M1 electric car does not exceed EUR 50,000 (including VAT), and to cars of
the specified categories, if they are classified as special purpose vehicles according to the legislation
governing the classification and coding of vehicles; and

+ input VAT on the service of passenger transport by motor vehicles specified above, provided that the VAT
payer does not take part in the transaction for the supply of this service as an undisclosed agent.

9.2 Excess input VAT

As a general rule, input VAT may be deducted from the payable output VAT. If the amount of input VAT
exceeds the amount of output VAT, the amount of excess input VAT may be carried forward to the following
tax periods or be recovered.

The request to recover input VAT must be accompanied with valid VAT invoices confirming the input VAT
paid. The excess VAT is repaid by the tax authorities within 30 days after submission of the application or, ifa
tax audit is carried out, within 20 days after the completion of the audit.

9.3 Adjustment periods

Input VAT deductibility adjustment periods are the following:
+ for immovable property - 10 years after the taxable period in which input VAT was deducted;
+ for other fixed tangible assets - 5 years after the taxable period in which input VAT was deducted

The adjustment for capital goods/immovable property is not applicable to services.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

A foreign taxable person established in another EU Member State is entitled to apply for a VAT refund if the

following conditions are met:

+ during the period for which the VAT refund is requested, the claimant did not have a fixed establishment
in Lithuania from which economic activities were effected, or, in case of an individual, its main place of
residence was not in Lithuania; and

+ the claimant did not carry out any activities that would be subject to VAT in Lithuania, except for the
supply of transportation services and ancillary services that would be subject to zero rate VAT or the
supply of certain goods or services for which the reverse charge mechanism applies.

372 | Lithuania

--- Page 13 ---
When the application relates to a period of less than one calendar year but not less than 3 months, the amount
for which application is made should not be less than EUR 400. However, when the application relates to a
period of a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year, the amount may not be less than EUR 50.

‘The application must cover a period of not less than 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year
and not more than one calendar year, unless the period represents the remainder ofa calendar year. The
application must be submitted to the Lithuanian tax authorities by 30 September of the following year. The
deadline cannot be extended.

The application form for VAT refund must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities
in the country in which the taxable person is established. If a representative is to submit the application, the
relevant power of attorney should be submitted electronically with the VAT refund claim. If the power of
attomey is not in Lithuanian or English, it must be translated into Lithuanian. The claimant or its authorised
representative should also provide the following information in the application form:

+ general information relating to the claimant;

* economic activities the claimant is engaged in and for which VAT was incurred;

* information relating to the authorised person. if any;

+ details of the bank account to which the refundable VAT is to be paid;

«editable list of invoices and import documents; and

+ scanned invoices or import documents should also be attached to the application.

An electronic copy of invoices should be submitted only if the amount of the invoice or import document is
EUR 1,000 ot more (EUR 250 for invoices relating to fuel costs). The Litbuanian authorities are allowed to
request additional documents/information,

The decision on the VAT refund is issued within 4 months after receipt of the application. The term may be
extended to 6 months where additional information is requested or 8 months where the authorities request
additional information after the first request.

If the refund is granted, it will be paid within 10 business days afier the end of the deadline to refund the VAT,
to the bank account number provided to the authorities. However, if the refund is not granted, an appeal can
be made to the Lithuanian tax authorities within 20 days after the date of receipt of the decision. The said time
limit can be extended upon request. If this appeal is unsuccessful, the claimant can initiate proceedings before
the administrative court.

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

Lithuania will refund the VAT incurred by a taxable person established in a non-EU country provided a
Lithuanian taxable person is entitled to recover the VAT (or any equivalent tax) in that country (the principle
of reciprocity).

According to the information published on the tax administrator's website, taxable persons established in
Armenia, Iceland, the United Kingdom (with certain restrictions), Canada, Norway, Turkey, and Switzerland
may apply for a VAT refund.

The list of non-EU countries where taxable persons can apply for a VAT refund in Lithuania is not exhaustive
and may be adjusted based on the VAT refuund practices applied by other countries to Lithuanian taxable
persons. It is recommended to regularly check the tax administrator’s website for the most up-to-date
information.

However, VAT may be refined to non-EU taxable persons that supply e-services in the EU and, by using
a special registration procedure, are registered for VAT in one of the EU Member States. In this case the
reciprocity principle is not applicable.

‘When the application relates to a period of less than one calendar year but not less than 3 months, the amount

for which application is made must not be less than EUR 400, however, when the application relates to a
period of a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year, the amount may not be less than EUR 50.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A full invoice must contain the following data:

+ date of issue:

+ invoice serial number;

+ supplier’s VAT payer code or VAT identification number, name and address;

customer's VAT payer code or VAT identification number, name and address;

+ description of the goods and services and quantity thereof

«date of the supply if it does not coincide with the date of issue. Where the VAT invoice documents receipt
of advance payment, the date of receipt of advance payment shall be specified if it is different from the
date of issue of the VAT invoice;

+ unit price (excluding VAT) and discounts not included in the price;

+ taxable amount of the goods/services supplied that are taxed at the same rate;

+ applicable VAT rate/rates (except cases when invoice is issued by a taxable person who applies the SME
scheme);

* amount of VAT in national currency (EUR) (except cases when invoice is issued by a taxable person who
applies the SME scheme);

+ VAT registration number, name and address of a fiscal agent ~ where the obligation to catculate VAT
under this Law lies with a fiscal agent appointed by a foreign taxable person;

+ where the VAT invoice documents supply of goods/services that are exempt from VAT or taxed at a 0%
VAT rate, reference to the relevant provision of this Law or Directive 2006/112/EC, or any other reference
to such exemption/0% VAT rate;

+ where special VAT margin scheme applies, the reference to the margin scheme:

10.1.2 Time limits

The invoice should be issued immediately after the supply of goods or services takes place. However, in case
of continuous supplies of services and permanent supply of electricity, gas, heating and cooling energy, the
invoice may be issued for the services or goods supplied during the period of one month, In the latter case the
invoice should be issued by the 10 day of the following month.

For the supplies of goods subject to VAT at a zero rate, as well as for supplies of services subject to the
reverse charge mechanism, an invoice should be issued by the 15" day of the next calendar month, When
goods and services referred to herein are supplied continuously for a period longer than | month, the invoice
can be issued on a monthly basis by the 15" day of the next calendar month.

10.1.3 Currency
IL is allowed to issue invoices in a foreign currency, but the VAT amount must be indicated in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

When computing the amount in Euros, the conversion rate published by the Bank of Lithuania on the day
when the VAT becomes due must be used.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under the VAT Law, the contractors may agree up front, either verbally or in a written form, that the customer
will issue the invoice. There are currently no additional requirements or restrictions related to such invoicing.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ invoice number (no serial number is needed);

+ supplier’s VAT payer code or VAT identification number, name;

= customer’s VAT payer code or VAT identification number, name;

+ description and quantity of the goods and services;

* taxable amount of goods/services supplied that are taxed at the same rate;

+ applicable VAT rate/rates (except cases when invoice is issued by a taxable person who applies the SME
scheme); and

* amount of VAT in national currency (EUR) (except cases when invoice is issued by a taxable person who
applies the SME scheme).

Simplified invoice can be issued in these cases:
when the total value of supplied goods/services does not exceed EUR 100 (including VAT);

+ when the invoice is issued by a Lithuanian VAT payer applying the SME scheme in Lithuania; and

+ when the invoice is issued by a taxable person established in another EU member state applying the SME
scheme in Lithuania

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

‘The minimum term of storage of invoices is 10 years. Taxable persons must store documents in the original
form in which they were issued and sent, whether paper or electronic, and must ensure that the authenticity of
the origin, integrity of the content and readability is guaranteed throughout the storage period

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoices may be used if the origin, authenticity and content integrity are assured, The supplier must
have the consent of the customer to use electronic invoicing.

‘An invoice sent to the customer by electronic mail or by other means (in pdf or other format) is also
considered to be an electronic invoice. Such invoice may not be kept in a printed form.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period
The standard VAT return period for taxable persons is one month.

However, if the annual income from economic activities of a taxable person (except for a natural person) did
not exceed EUR 300,000 in the previous calendar year, the VAT retum period may be changed to a calendar
quarter at the request of the taxable person. Furthermore, the VAT return period may be changed to another
period not longer than 60 days, provided that this is necessary for the taxable person because of the foreign
parent company’s taxable period or deadlines for financial reporting and the beginning of the first tax period
of the financial year and the end of the last tax period of the taxable person coincides with the beginning and
end of the relevant calendar year.

The standard VAT return period for taxable natural persons is 6 months, which may be changed to one month
at the request of the taxable person.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

Ifa tax period is a calendar month, the VAT retum for the tax period must be submitted not later than by the
25" day of the next month.

Ifa tax period is a calendar quarter, the VAT return for the tax period must be submitted not later than by the
25" day of the first month of the next quarter.

Ha tax period is a calendar half-year, the VAT return shall be submitted not later than by the 25" day of the
first month of the next half-year.

The duty to make advance VAT payments is not applied in Lithuania,

must be submitted through i.SAF system, which is a part of a smart tax administration (i. MAS) system. The
deadlines for submission of the Registers are as follows:

+ ifthe taxable period is a calendar month — by the 20" day of the subsequent month;

+ ifthe taxable period is a calendar quarter— by the 20" day of the first month of the subsequent quarter;
+ in case of any other taxable period — not later than 20 days after the end of such period.

ing
‘The European sales listing must be submitted on a monthly basis before the 25" day of the next month.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

With certain exceptions, the general tax assessment period in Lithuania is the current and the previous 3
calendar years. Therefore, in the year 2025, the last open year for assessment is the year 2022,

However, in some cases this period may be be extended (for e.g,, if the tax payer does not comply with the
established minimum reliable tax payer’s criteria or it deducted VAT related to long term assets ete.).

10.3 Penalties

376 | Lithuania

--- Page 17 ---
10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

For failing to register for VAT purposes or late registration a fine of EUR 200 to EUR 390 may be imposed on
a tax payer and a fine of EUR 390 to EUR 1,100 may be imposed on a manager of the tax payer.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or European sales listing on time

For failing to file within the required time or filing incomplete documents, a fine from EUR 200 up to EUR
6,000 can be imposed on the taxable person, accountant or other person responsible for filling of the reports.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities
Under a general rule, default interest is calculated from the outstanding tax amount for each day of delay

(since | February 2025 the default interest rate is 0,027% per day). This rule also applies for late payment of
the VAT.

Information regarding applicable default interest can be found on the webpage of the Ministry of Finance:
Attps://tinyurl.com/f 3 yn2

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

In addition to late payment interest, a fine of 20% to 100% of the amount of underpaid VAT may be imposed
when non-compliance with the VAT Law is established by the tax authorities in the course of a tax audit.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

Criminal proceedings may be started in case of negligent accounting or fraudulent accounting, Furthermore,
in case of intentional acts and non-collaboration with the tax authorities in the course of the tax audit,

a maximum fine indicated above may be imposed (normally a 30% penalty is imposed unless there are
palliative or aggravating circumstances).

10.4 Tntrastat

Intrastat reports may be submitted only electronically, using the Intrastat report collection and processing
system IDAIS (available through an Intemet link: ttps://intrastat. lrmuitine.It.)

Reports are submitted after the end of the reporting month until the 10° working day of the next calendar
month, The report submission schedule for the year is published on the Official Statistics portal osp stat. gov. lt
and on the website of the Customs Department at www.lrmuitine.It,

Reports must also be completed in the event that the VAT payer did not dispatch the goods from Lithuania to other
EU Member States and/or did not receive the goods from other EU Member States during the reporting month.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
For 2025, the annual threshold for reporting is EUR 570,000 for arrivals and EUR 400,000 for dispatches.
It should be noted, though, that the thresholds are reviewed and re-approved annually. Therefore, the later
amounts are valid until 31 December 2025 only

+ reporting period;

* code of the good under the EU Combined Nomenclature;
+ description of the good:

* contract;

+ delivery conditions;

* means of transport;

+ country of consignor;

* country of consignee;

+ country of origin;

* partner identification code;

+ net weight in kilograms;
