Luxembourg

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive was implemented in Luxembourg through the “Loi du 12 février
199 ¢ oncernant la Taxe sur la Valeur Ajoutée” (hereafter Luxembourg VAT law). VAT is referred to in
Luxembourg as “Taxe sur la Valeur Ajoutée™ or TVA.

1. Taxable persons

11 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Anyone performing an economic activity in Luxembourg as a taxable person must register for VAT purposes
in Luxembourg. In this respect, a VAT registration filing must be submitted with the Luxembourg VAT
authorities within 15 days from the start of the business in Luxembourg. This VAT registration file has to
contain various documents such as:

+ a copy of the by-laws of the taxable person;

*  acopy of renting agreement;

+ a copy of business licence if applicable;

+ a bank account number from Luxembourg or foreign bank; and

+ a copy of the passport of each of the managers of the taxable person.

Additional information can be downloaded at: www.pfi.public.lu

Ona case by case basis, the VAT authorities may require additional information/documents in order to issue
a VAT number to the taxable person. The VAT number is generally granted within a 4-week period. However,
there is no VAT registration obligation in the following cases:

* the taxable person has no fixed establishment in Luxembourg and performs his activities outside
Luxembourg; or

+ the taxable person performs only an exempt activity which does not grant him the right to deduct input
VAT incurred and does not receive services from abroad; and/or

+ he does not acquire goods from another EU Member State for an amount of more than EUR 10,000.

Small businesses may apply an exemption if their annual turnover does not exceed the threshold of EUR
50,000. There is 2 10% tolerance. This generally means that the exemption may apply in a calendar year if the
annual turnover does not exceed EUR 50,000 in the previous year. If the EUR 50,000 threshold is exceeded
in a calendar year, the exemption may continue to apply until the end of the year if the EUR 55,000 is not
exceeded. If the EUR 55,000 threshold is exceeded, the exemption ceases to apply from the following day.

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no specific threshold for foreign taxable persons in Luxembourg, The applicable rules are the same
as those applicable to domestic taxable persons (see also paragraph 1.1). Every foreign taxable person
performing an economic activity, for which the place of taxation is deemed to be in Luxembourg and for
which the taxable person is liable for the payment of the VAT, must register for VAT purposes in Luxembourg.
In certain cases, a business license is also required.

‘Taxable persons not established in Luxembourg must deliver the VAT registration application with the
accompanying documents to the following address:

AED - Bureau d’Imposition Luxembourg 10
1-3, Avenue Guillaume,L 4651 Luxembourg

Luxembourg, | 379

--- Page 20 ---
Postal address:
BP.31
£-2010 Luxembourg

They can also submit the request electronically through www.myguichet.lu

Small businesses from other EU Member States who apply the scheme for small businesses in the country of
establishment:

+ with an annual turnover in the LU of less than EUR 100,000; and

+ an annual turnover of less than EUR 50,000 in Luxembourg

may also choose to apply the exemption in Luxembourg as of 2025.

13 VAT grouping

VAT grouping has been implemented in Luxembourg in 2018. Legal persons established in Luxembourg and
closely related to each other in financial, economic and organisational may opt to be considered as a single
taxable person, i.e, “VAT group”.

All transactions, services and supplies of goods between members of a VAT group are in principle not subject
to VAT. These types of transactions are considered as internal operations carried out within the same legal
entity. By reference to the EU court cases, the VAT group can include taxable persons and non VAT payers
(non-identified persons), which allows pure holding companies to set up a VAT group.

The notification of incorporation of a VAT group must be submitted by the group representative to the VAT
authorities.

1.4 Fiscal representative

The possibility to opt for a fiscal representative was re-implemented in Luxembourg in 2008.

Fiscal representation can be used in Luxembourg by a taxable person who is not established nor registered
for VAT purposes in Luxembourg if he performs an importation of tangible movable goods, whatever the
subsequent transaction is. ’he fiscal representative must be duly authorised by the VAT authorities.

In order to be valid, the appointment and the acceptance of the fiscal representative have to be made prior to
the importation of the goods. The fiscal representative is the importer of record of the goods and should have
effective control over the imported goods.

The represented taxable person remains jointly liable for the payment of the VAT, interest and penalties. The
following conditions have to be met in order to be granted the status of fiscal representative:

+ beable to act, via a Power of Attorney, for the fiscal representative;

+ be established in Luxembourg;

+ be sufficiently solvent;

+ have constantly performed all fiscal and para-fiscal obligations with the best diligence; and

+ have human, technical and material resources to accomplish the obligation in relation with its activity.

The validity of the agreement with the VAT authorities stops if any one of these conditions is not fulfilled.

In addition, the fiscal representative must produce a guarantee to ensure the payment of the VAT, interest and
penalties potentially due to the VAT authorities in case of failure to comply with VAT obligations. The amount
of the guarantee must be continuously maintained at a minimum level equal to 50% of the excess of VAT due
for the past 3 monthly VAT returns for which the obligation of filing and payment has expired. The guarantee
must amount to at least EUR 10,000.

The fiscal representative must comply with specific administrative obligations (VAT returns, invoicing, ete.)..

380 | Luxembourg

--- Page 21 ---
2. Taxable event

2.1 Transfer of business
The transfer of business is out of the scope of VAT if this transfer can be considered as a transfer of an
ongoing concern.

This provision applies if the transferee continues with the economic activity of the transferor.

2.2 Supplies free of charge
Luxembourg generally applies the principles laid down in Article 26 of the EU VAT Directive. There is no
specific amount mentioned in the law.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

It is possible to apply for an exemption on the importation of goods in Luxembourg if it is followed by an
intra-Community supply,

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Luxembourg has implemented the postponed accounting on imports provided by the Article 211(2) of the EU
VAT Directive (deferral of import VAT).

The VAT due on imported goods must be paid and declared through the VAT return of the liable person
registered for VAT purposes in Luxembourg. If the importer is not registered for VAT purposes in
Luxembourg, the VAT is levied by the Customs and Excise Administration.

No license is required by the Luxembourg VAT authorities in order to apply the postponed accounting on
importation VAT.

3. Place of supply

31 Consignment and call-off stock

Luxembourg implemented the relevant rules in line with the EU VAT Directive.

3.2. Installation supplies
A supply of goods with installation or assembly is deemed to take place where it has been assembled or
installed, In Luxembourg, there is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly”.

No domestic reverse charge is applicable on the supply with installation in Luxerabourg.

3.3 Distance sales

The regime has been modified as from 1 July 2021. All distance sales taking place in Luxembourg in a B2C
transaction are deemed to be subject to Luxembourg. No threshold is applicable. In order to facilitate the
perception of the VAT, Luxembourg implemented the OSS (One Stop Shop). This measure is facultative and
not compulsory.

Luxembourg implemented also specific rutes for intra-Community distance selling through platforms
exploited from outside BU and for distance selling of imports.

Luxembourg | 381

--- Page 22 ---
34 Simplified triangulation

The simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in Luxembourg in line with the EU VAT Directive.
The following conditions must be met to apply the simplified triangulation rules:

+ the intra-Community acquisition of goods must be made by a taxable person (B) registered in another EU
Member State but neither established nor registered for VAT purposes in Luxembourg;

+ the intra-Community acquisition must be made for the purpose of a subsequent supply in Luxembourg;

+ the goods must be dispatched or transported by the supplier A or middleman B from an EU Member State
other than that in which B is identified for VAT purposes to the person for whom he is to carry out the
subsequent supply;

+ the final customer (C) must be registered for VAT purposes in Luxembourg; and

+ the final customer (C) must be designated as liable for payment of the VAT due on the supply of goods.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

‘The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons performing

exempt activities, taxable persons benefiting from the special regime applicable to small- and medium- size
companies and taxable persons benefiting from flat-rate scheme applicable to agriculture and forestry from
suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000 for Luxembourg. These persons can,
however, opt to submit their intra~Community acquisitions of goods to Luxembourg VAT even if the threshold
is not met.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

‘The use and enjoyment rules are only implemented for radio and television broadcasting services and
telecommunications services.

Indced, the place of supply of telecommunication and radio and television broadcasting services which would
be situated in Luxembourg pursuant to the general rules is deemed to be outside the EU if the actual use or
enjoyment of these services takes place outside the EU.

Next to the above, Luxembourg has implemented effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59(a),
sub a of the EU VAT Directive for B2B services in relation to the transport of goods and related services
thereto, Consequently, even if the place of supply is Luxembourg according to the B2B main rule of Article
44 of the EU VAT Direetive, they are not subject to Luxembourg VAT if the effective use and enjoyment of
those services takes place outside the EU, ice. if the transport of goods takes place outside the EU

4, Chargeable event and chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods are put at the
disposal of the acquirer of the goods. The VAT due on services, in principle, becomes chargeable at the time
the services are supplied. The supply of goods, other than the hire of goods for a certain period or the sale

of goods on deferred terms, that involves successive statements of account or successive payments, shall be
regarded as being completed on expiry of the periods to which such statements of account or payments relate.

These principles, however, are overruled: in case of the obligation to issue an invoice, VAT becomes
chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice. In case the invoice has not been issued timely, VAT shall
become chargeable on the 15" day of the month following that during which the chargeable event occurs. The
general rule as to the time when the VAT becomes payable provides that a taxable person must, by the 15" of
the month following the end of each tax period, file a VAT return indicating the amount of VAT which became
due by him during the tax period concerned.

382 | Luxembourg,

--- Page 23 ---
42 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or
transported from Luxembourg to another EU Member State and which are supplied VAT exempt or which are
transferred VAT exempt, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such
time as the supply comes to an end.

For services ongoing over a period of more than | year that do not give rise to statements of account or
payments in that period and on which the (ax is incurred by the customer, the services are regarded as being
supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of the services ceases.

44 Cash accounting

Under some conditions, VAT can be paid over to the Luxembourg state on the basis of cash received during
the respective VAT period as opposed to making payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during
the period. The cash accounting regime is available to persons engaged in the supply of taxable services or
goods when their annual turnover is less than EUR 500,000.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

When it can be reasonably admitted that the customer, taxable person or not, will not pay or will not fully pay
the amount invoiced for a supply of goods or services, the supplier is allowed to recover the output VAT.

Ifa payment is done at a later stage, a new declaration is required for the input VAT deducted and the output
VAT due,

No specific procedure is foreseen by the LTVA regarding the evidences to be provided to the VAT authorities
in order to recover output VAT relating to unsettled sales invoices. The taxable person must nevertheless
demonstrate that the debt is irrecoverable, i.e. to prove to the Luxembourg VAT authorities that it has
undertaken all necessary actions to claim payment from the debtor (reminders sent to customers, legal actions
to obtain payment, etc.). The VAT on bad debt can be deducted / recovered directly through the Luxembourg
VAT returns.

5.2 Cash discount

Assuming that the supplier sends the following invoice:

Supply of goods EUR 1,000
1% VAT EUR_10
Total to be paid EUR 1,16

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier has to pay EUR 101 n its VAT return.
The customer can deduct EUR 103i n its VAT return. Therefore the supplier paid too much VAT and the
customer deducted too much. The taxable basis has to be regularized by the supplier and by the client. No
credit note is in principle required unless the discount applies to supplies covering a determined period and
not only one supply. In this case, a detailed document has to be issued by the supplier.

Luxembourg | 383

--- Page 24 ---
5.3 Open market value

The taxable amount to be taken into account is the “open market” value where any of the following applies

+ The consideration is lower than the “open market” value and the recipient does not have a full right of
deduction;

+ The consideration is lower than the “open market” value and the supplier does not have a [ull right of
deduction and the supply is subject to an exemption; or

+ The consideration is higher than the “open market” value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction.

The “open market” value is the full amount that a customer would have to pay under conditions of fair
competition to an independent supplier for the provision of the goods or services in the country.

54 Returnable packaging

Article 92 of the EU VAT Directive regarding the cost of returnable packing material is not implemented in
Luxembourg.

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rats

The standard rate in Luxembourg is 1% .

6.2 Reduced rate

An intermediary rate in Luxembourg is 14% and applies for instance to:

+ _ safekeeping and management of shares;

* management of credit and of credit guarantees by a person or a company other than the one who has
granted the credit;

+ wines from fresh grapes, with an alcohol percentage of 13% or less, with the exception of wines enhanced
with alcohol, sparkling wine and liquors; and

+ prepared detergents and prepared cleansers.

‘The reduced rate in Luxembourg is 8% and applies for instance to:

+ gas, liquid or gaseous, suitable for heating, lighting and motor fuel;
+ electric power; and
+ flowers and other horticultural plants.

63 Super-reduced rate

The super-reduced rate in Luxembourg is 3% and applies for instance to;

+ foodstuffs for human consumption excluding alcoholic beverages;
* foodstuffs for animal consumption;

+ therapeutic instruments and medical equipment for disabled people;
+ farm inputs excluding flowers and other horticultural plants;

* books, including brochures, maps;

* some children’s clothes; and

* copyrights.

384 | Luxembourg

--- Page 25 ---
7. Exemptions

7.1 Exemption with the right of deduction

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies
See 71.2. be low.

7.1.2 Export supplies
Luxembourg applies a zero rate to the following supplies of goods and services:

+ exports and intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ intra-Community transfer of own goods;

* new means of transport transported to acquirers outside Luxembourg but within the EU;

+ the supply of services, including transport and incidental services (loading, handling, etc.)
to an export of goods which are placed under a customs regime;

+ the chartering and leasing of seagoing vessels (for the purposes of high sea navigation)

jirectly related

The proof of these operations is not mentioned in the law for the application of the zero rate. However, in
practice, the VAT authorities may require the proof that the goods left the country, e.g. in the case of an
intra~-Community supply or an export. This could be proved by the way of a transport document or a customs
document,

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
Luxembourg has introduced the concept of a VAT-free zone.

The free zone regime is essentially a suspension regime allowing the storage, sale and work on goods with
limited tax obligations in terms of VAT, customs and excise duties.

Certain services may also benefit from exemptions such as the storage or valorisation proceeds on goods
performed within the free zone. Such a regime is particularly attractive for transactions on the commodity
exchange, where goods may change owners within a short period of time.

This regime is deemed to accompany the suspension regimes existing for customs and excise duties.

‘The payment of VAT will have to be performed once the goods exit the free zone regime. Regularization of
the temporary exemption will need to be performed in specific cases.

‘The regime can be applied by pre-authorized importers or by a fiscal representative if the importer is not
registered for VAT purposes in Luxembourg.
7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Excise goods (alcohol, tobacco, energy products) coming from another EU country may be placed in an
excise warehouse with suspension of excise duties and VAT.

7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Goods coming from a third country may be placed in a bonded warehouse with suspension of customs duties
and VAT.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

Examples of supplies that are exempt without the right to deduction are:

+ the granting and the negotiation of credit and the management of credit by the person granting it;
+ the management of special investment funds located in Luxembourg and similar funds located in the EU

Luxembourg | 385

--- Page 26 ---
as well as the securitization vehicles and the altemative investment funds (as defined by the Alternative
Investment Fund Managers Directive); insurance and reinsurance transactions, including related services
performed by insurance brokers and insurance agents; and

+ supplies of buildings or parts thereof, and of the land on which they stand.

Notably, insurance transactions and the granting of interest-bearing loans may give Lo the taxable person the
right to deduct related input VAT incurred if the client is located outside the EU.
13 Option for taxation

‘No option for taxation has been implemented in Luxembourg except for the supply of buildings and rent of
immovable property. With respect to these specific transactions, various formal conditions and formalities
must be met in order to apply Luxembourg VAT.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism ~ non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

With the exception of the reverse charge mechanism for emission allowances and similar units, Luxembourg
has not implemented a domestic reverse charge for non-established suppliers on supplies of goods or services
(option of Article 194 of the EU VAT Directive not exercised),

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

The taxable person, registered in Luxembourg for VAT purposes, to which the transfer of allowances to emit
greenhouse gases is made, is liable for payment of VAT under the reverse charge mechanism in Luxembourg.

The fact that the supplier is a taxable person established and registered in Luxembourg or in another country
is not relevant for the application of this provision.
82 Joint and several liability

Under Luxembourg VAT law, administrators and directors of a company are jointly and severally liable
for the unpaid taxes of the company. They have to ensure the company’s compliance with the law and the
financial resources availability.

9. Recovery of input VAT

9.1 Non-recoverable input VAT

There is no specific good or service for which VAT is not deductible if itis related to the taxable activity of a
taxable person.

92 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is refunded by the VAT authorities once the annual VAT return has been assessed. This can
take several years in practice. A special refund process has been introduced in the law in order to accelerate
the process. The request has to be introduced by letter, duly motivated, which allows the VAT Authorities to
make deeper checks before to proceed the refund.

9.3 Adjustment periods

+ Immovable property - 10 years
+ Movable property - 5 years

386 | Luxembourg

--- Page 27 ---
In principle, Luxembourg does not have an adjustment period for services. However, some work on
immovable property considered as investment expenses (as per the direct tax definition) can be subject to the
adjustment period.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

A VAT refund can be claimed by a foreign business for any VAT charged in respect of goods or services
supplied to it by other taxable persons in Luxembourg or in respect of the importation of goods into
Luxembourg, insofar as such goods and services are used for the purposes of the following transactions:

+ the supply of transport services and ancillary services;
+ the supply of goods and services to a taxable person which is liable for payment of VAT; and
+ the transactions falling into the scope of VAT carried out abroad for which VAT is deductible

The minimum amount to be claimed is EUR 400 (for a reimbursement period of 3 months) and EUR 50
(for a reimbursement period of one year). The minimum period for making a claim is 3 months whereas the
maximum period is one year. The deadline for making a claim is September 30 of the year following the
reimbursement period. It is not possible to submit the claim after the deadline.

The original invoices or the importation documents (invoice amounts exceeding EUR 1,000) are required. In
addition, the taxable person must have VAT refund access on the VAT refund portal.

‘The time limit for the VAT authorities to take a decision is 4 months following the VAT refund request. The
VAT authorities must make the VAT refund within a reasonable period. The time limit for appeals is 3 months.
VAT amounts wrongly charged on invoices are in principle not refundable. For additional information please
refer to:. https://pfi,public. u/fi:html

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

Even if this is not really clearly stated in the VAT Act, Luxembourg does not require reciprocity for the
recovery of VAT by non-EU taxable persons. So in principle, all foreign taxable persons, non-established in
Luxembourg, should be able to claim back the VAT they paid in Luxembourg provided that (1) they were not
obliged to be VAT registered, (2) an invoice has been issued, (3) the VAT relates to activities for which the
VAT can be deducted and (4) there is no limitation by the law of the deductibility.

The claim should be done electronically at: htips://tinyurl.com/y6ckz3j2

In order to submit a claim, a Luxtrust certificate access should be delivered. Once the Luxtrust certificate is
obtained, the VAT refund portal will be accessible. A VAT refund request can then be made.

The minimum amount to be claimed is EUR 200 (for a reimbursement period exceeding 3 months) and
EUR 250 (for a reimbursement period of one year). The minimum period for making a claim is 3 months
and the maximum period is one year, The deadtine for making this claim is June 30 of the year following the
reimbursement period. It is not possible to submit the claim after the deadline.

‘The required documents include: the original invoices or the importation documents, a certificate (with the
VAT registration number of the non-resident taxable person or a document certifying that the non-resident EU
taxable person is a taxable person for VAT purposes), and an affidavit in which the non-resident EU taxable
person is bound to reimburse to the VAT authorities any VAT amount unduly reimbursed.

The time limit for the VAT authorities to take a decision and for making the refund is 6 months following the
VAT refund request. The time limit for appeals is 3 months.

VAT amounts wrongly charged on invoices are in principle not refundable (https://pfi.public.lu/fi:htm! ).

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content
A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ date on which the invoice is issued;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ VAT identification number under which the supply of goods or services is performed;

+ name and address of the supplier and of the customer;

+ VAT identification number of the customer in case of an intra-Communily supply or the customer is liable
for the payment of the VAT;

* quantity and nature of the goods delivered or a description of the services rendered;

+ the date on which the supply of goods or services took place or was achieved or in the case of an advance
payment, the date of the advance payment if either of these dates is determined and different from the
date of the invoice;

+ the taxable amount for each VAT rate and exemption, the unit price without VAT as well as any discount if
it is not included in the unit price;

+ the VAT rate applied;

+ the VAT amount due unless a specific regime excludes this mention;

+ if VAT exemption applies or if VAT is due by the customer, a reference to the LTVA or to the EU VAT
Directive or any other reference stating that the transaction is VAT exempt or that payment of VAT is due
by the customer under the reverse charge mechanism;

+ where the VAT becomes chargeable at the time when the payment is received and the right of deduction
arises at the time the deductible tax becomes chargeable, the mention “Cash accounting” (“Comptabilité
de caisse”) should appear on the invoice;

+ where the customer receiving a supply issues the invoice instead of the supplier, the mention “Self-
billing” (“Autofacturation”) is required;

+ the indication for the application of an exemption, zero rate or reverse charge, for instance:

+ intra-Community supply: “Livraison intracommunautaire”;
+ export supply: “Exportation”;
+ B2B service: “Autoliquidation”;

+ the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport, (i.e. number of
kilometres, date since when the means of transport has been put into service, etc.); and

+ an indication of the fact that a margin scheme has been applied as well as an indication why the margin
scheme applies, e.;
+ margin scheme - second-hand goods: “Régime particulier - Biens d’occasion”;

+ margin scheme - works of art: “Régime particulier - Objets darts”;
= margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques: “Régime particulier - Objets de collection et d’antiquité”;
+ tour operator margin scheme: “Régime particulier - Agences de voyages”.

10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice must be issued before the 15 day of the month following the month in which the supply of goods
or services is rendered. In case of advance payment for a supply of goods or services not yet executed or
partially executed, an invoice must be issued at the latest upon receipt of the advance payment.

10.1.3 Currency

The amounts which appear on the invoice may be expressed in any currency, provided that the amount of
VAT payable or to be adjusted is expressed in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

The exchange rate which must be used for the conversion is the rate published by the Luxembourg Central
Bank or by an accredited banking institution according to the exchange rate published by the European
Central Bank.

388 | Luxembourg

--- Page 29 ---
10.1.5  Self-billing
Under LTVA, self-billing is allowed if'a specific written agreement exists between the two parties.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

When an invoice does not concern transactions with another EU Member State and its total amount (VAT
included) is not above EUR 100, it suffices to include only the following information:

+ date of issue;
+ name and address of the supplier;

+ quantity and nature of the delivered goods or description of the services; and
+ VAT to be paid or information to enable its calculation,

10.1.7 Storage of invoices
Invoices must be kept available for 10 years.

Invoices may be stored electronically on condition that the data guaranteeing the authenticity of their origin
and their integrity are also stored. If electronic invoices are stored outside Luxembourg, the taxable person is
required to inform the VAT authorities.

When invoices are not stored electronically, taxable persons established in Luxembourg have to store them in
Luxembourg, For non-established taxable persons that have appointed a fiscal representative, all their records
must be stored electronically by the fiscal representative, A direct online access must be made available to the
VAT authorities.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is allowed on condition that the authenticity of their origin and the integrity of their
content are guaranteed. Issuing electronic invoices requires the agreement of the recipient.

Electronic invoicing must be understood as the transmission to, or the act of placing the invoice at, the
disposal of the recipient (i.e. keeping the document available for the recipient) using electronic data
processing equipment data transmission using the cable, radio, optical or electromagnetic means. Electronic
invoices can be issued in one of the formats: XML, email with PDF invoice attached, electronic fax, or an
accepted equivalent.

10.2. Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

The periodicity of filing VAT returns depends on the amount of the taxable person’s annual turnover. This can
be summarised as follows:

* turnover within the scope of VAT exceeding EUR 620,000: monthly VAT retums as well as one
recapitulative VAT return per calendar year:

* turnover within the scope of VAT between EUR 112,000 and EUR 620,000: quarterly VAT returns as well
as one recapitulative VAT return per calendar year;

+ tumover within the scope of VAT below EUR 112,000: one single annual VAT return per calendar year; and

+ turnover entirely VAT exempt but VAT registered in order to reverse charge Luxembourg VAT: one
simplified annual VAT return per calendar year,

VAT returns must be filed electronically by taxable persons subject to periodic filings.

There is no difference in the treatment of resident and non-resident taxable persons with regard to the VAT
return period, the deadline for VAT payment or for filing a VAT return.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

Monthly and quarterly VAT retums have to be filed by the 15 of the month following the period of the return.
In practice, an extension of 2 months is currently granted by the VAT authorities.

Luxembourg | 389

--- Page 30 ---
Recapitulative annual VAT retums have to be filed by the 1* of May of the year following the year of the
retum. A requested extension to 31 December of the year following the year of the retum is in practice
currently granted by the VAT authorities.

Single annual VAT returns and simplified annual VAT returns have to be filed by the 1" of March of the year
following the year of the return. A requested extension to 31 October of the year following the year of the
return is in practice currently granted by the VAT authorities.

The payment of the VAT has to be made at the time of the VAT return’s filing. The VAT return forms can be
downloaded at: htips://tinyurl.com/muscnx53

10.2.3 European sales listing

‘The European sales listing for goods must be submitted on a monthly basis for all supply of goods. The
monthly European sales listing for goods must be filed electronically.

‘The European sales listing for services can be submitted on a monthly or quarterly basis, regardless of the
periodicity of filing of the intra-Community supply of goods.

‘The deadline for filing these listings is the 15" of the month following the period concerned in case of a paper
filing and the 25" of the month following the period concerned in case of an electronic filing.

Taxable persons who file periodical VAT returns must file the European sales listing for goods and for services
electronically.

‘The VAT return forms can be downloaded at: Attps://tinyurl.com/muscnx53

10.2.4 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period in Luxembourg is 5 years. That means that the VAT authorities have 5 years from
the 1" January following the year where the VAT becomes due to assess the VAT returns.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

Each infraction to the Luxembourg VAT Law (LTVA) can be sanctioned by a penalty of an amount between
EUR 250 and EUR 10,000 per infraction. A late registration is considered as an infraction.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Each infraction to the Luxembourg VAT Law (LTVA) can be sanctioned by a penalty of an amount between.
EUR 250 and EUR 10,000 per infraction.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

Incase the VAT due is not entirely paid before the deadline, the VAT authorities can apply a sanction
amounting from 10% to 50% per year of the VAT due.

In case the VAT authorities have issued a constraint, interest of 2% per year is due as from the date of this
constraint.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VA’

In principle, when VAT is not reported correctly, the penalties mentioned above (see also sections 10.3.2 and
10.3.3) are applied.

is not reported correctly

However, in most cases, a corrective VAT return can be filed with the VAT authorities as long as the VAT
assessment has not been filed. Please note that a nihil return followed by a return based on the accounting can
be considered as tax fraud and thus subject to penalties and increase of tax.

390 | Luxembourg

--- Page 31 ---
10.4 Intrastat

In Luxembourg, separate monthly Intrastat retums need to be submitted to the State.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
The annual thresholds are as follows:

+ for the arrivals: EUR 200,000; and
+ for the dispatches: EUR 150,000.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which needs to be reported for both the arrivals and the dispatches statistics are:

+ EU Member State of origin/destination;
+ VAT identification numbers;

+ nature of the transaction;

+ mode of transport;

+ 8-digit commodity code of the goods;

+ net weight of the goods;

+ additional statistical unit;

+ invoiced value of the goods in EUR: and
+ statistical value of the goods in EUR.

This information can be less depending on the amounts of arrivals/dispatches. There is also an Intrastat

obligation for distance sales.

Malta

‘The European Union (EU) VAT legislation in Malta is implemented through the “Value AddedTax” Chapter
406 of the Laws of Malta (hereafter referred to as the “VAT Act”), and the subsidiary legislations. VAT is
referred to as “Att Dwar it-Taxxa fu il- Valur Mizjud” or “VAT”.

1. Taxable persons

Li Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Every taxable person established in Malta who performs supplies in Malta (other than supplies which are
exempt without the right to claim input VAT) must register for VAT. In Malta (as of 1 January 2015) there is
no minimum registration threshold.

A taxable person who is not established in Malta, who is not already registered under Article 10 and who is
liable for the payment of the tax on a supply taking place in Malta, shal] be required to be registered under
this article by not later than thirty (30) days from the date of that supply. Provided that such person shall be
relieved from the obligation to be registered under Article 10 when he notifies the Commissioner that any of
the special schemes provided for in accordance with Chapter 6 of Title XI of Council Directive 2006/112/
EC, will be used to declare and pay the tax which he is liable to pay, in line with the provisions of the
respective special scheme, by no tater than ten (10) days from the date of that supply.

Furthermore, a taxable person who is not established in Malta but is established in another Member State,
shall also be relieved from the obligation to be registered under Article 10 when he notifies the Commissioner
that the special scheme for small enterprises provided for in accordance with Chapter 1 of Title XII of
Council Directive 2006/112/EC, will be used by such person, subject to the Commissioner’s approval.

A small enterprise, as defined in the Maltese VAT legislation, may opt to register under Article 11 of the
VAT Act. Such taxable person does not charge VAT on his supplies and cannot claim any input VAT on his
purchases.

A taxable person may apply to be registered under Article 11 of the VAT Act insofar as his Domestic annual
turnover during the preceding year, if any, is not more than the Domestic threshold of EUR 35,000.

In cases where an Article 10 registered taxable person would like to change to an Article [1 registration, such
change may not be affected before the end of twelve calendar months from the date of such registration. The
following conditions must be satisfied for a person who is registered under Article 10 to make a request to the
Commissioner to convert to a registration under Article 11:

+ the Commissioner is satisfied that the person qualifies as a small enterprise; and
* such person would have not claimed any input tax credits during his Article 10 registration.

As from 1 January 2025, with the introduction of the SME Scheme, Malta has introduced an Article 11A
registration which caters for taxable persons established in Malta who make supplies within the territory of
another Member State.

A taxable person who is established in Malta, who makes supplies of goods and services within the territory
of another Member State and who intends to avail of the exemption in terms of the special scheme for small
enterprises applicable in the respective Member State in which the supplies are made in accordance with
Chapter | of Title XII of Council Directive 2006/112/E may apply to the Commissioner to be registered under
this article, subject to the following conditions:

+ he qualifies as a small enterprise at the time of application;
> his EU annual turnover during the preceding calendar year is less than EUR 100,000;

Malta | 393

--- Page 34 ---
+ he makes supplies in at least one Member State in which he is not established, where is eligible to avail
himself of the special scheme for small enterprises; and

+ the value of supplies made in the preceding calendar year, in the Member State referred to above, does not
exceed the threshold applicable in the Member State granting the exemption.

In addition to the SME Scheme registration under Article 11 applicable to Maltese established taxable
persons, there also exists an Article 11B registration for taxable persons established in another Member
State which enables them to avail themselves of the SME scheme in Malta in respect of supplies of goods or
services they make in Malta.

In addition to the above the following are required to be registered under Article 12 of the VAT Act:

+ a taxable person who is not registered or obliged to be registered for VAT purposes under Article 10 and
who purchases services from outside of Malta in respect of which the liability for the payment of the VAT
is shifted onto him;

+ a taxable person who is not registered or obliged to be registered for VAT purposes under Article 10 or a
non-taxable legal person, who makes an intra-Community acquisition of goods in excess of EUR 10,000
(as per acquisition threshold mentioned further below). Notwithstanding the ‘Acquisition Threshold’ such
persons may voluntarily apply to be identified for VAT purposes in Malta under Article 12; and

+ taxable person established in Malta, other than a taxable person registered under article 10, who supplies
services within the territory of another Member State for which VAT is payable solely by the recipient of
the service pursuant to Article 196 of Council Directive 2006/112/EC, shall apply to be registered under
Article 12 not later than the date on which he supplies such service.

1.1.1 Registration applications

For a company lo register for VAT under Article 10, the following documents are required to be submitted to
the MTCA together with the online registration application

+ copy of the memorandum and articles of association of the company;
+ copy of the certificate of registration issued by the Malta Business Registry and
+ copy of the identity card or passport of any one of the Directors.

Where a Partnership is to be registered, a Partnership Declaration Form needs to be submitted in addition
to identification documents whilst in case of a registration for an individual, the copy of the identity card or
passport would suffice.

‘The registration can be effected online by following this link. The taxable person should choose the
applicable application from the list included therein; htips://tinyurl.com/3jhSfeek

An application for registration should be submitted by no later than thirty (30) days from the date a taxable
supply is made in Malta. Retroactive registration is possible; however, penalties apply for late registration.
The registration application is typically processed within approximately one/two weeks.

A taxable person may also opt (voluntarily) to be identified for VAT purposes under Article 10. Under such
circumstances, there is no time limit by when he should apply to be registered.

Furthermore, with regards to Article 11 registrations, retrospective registrations are not allowed. Such
registration is only valid from the date the Commissioner receives the relevant application, or from the date of
commencement of economic activity, whichever is the later date.

With regards to Article 11A registrations, such registration only takes effect as from the date notified by the
Commissioner to the taxable person.

An Article 11B taxable person’s registration is only deemed to be effective as from the date the Commissioner
notifies the Member State of establishment of his approval to register that person.

304 | Malta

--- Page 35 ---
12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no registration threshold for foreign legal entities that carry out taxable supplies in Malta as a result
of which are required to be registered for VAT purposes in Malta. For a foreign taxable person to register for
VAT, the same conditions and requirements apply as for domestic taxable persons (see paragraph 1.1). See

also in paragraph 1.1. above, an exception for registering as a result of opting for one of the special schemes.

Tn addition, with effect from 1 January 2025, due to the introduction of the special scheme for Small and

Medium Enterprises, a taxable person who is not established in Malta but is established in another Member State

shall be deemed to have applied to register under Article 11B once the Commissioner is notified by the Member

State in which such person is established of the person’s intention to avail itself of the exemption for small

enterprises in relation to supplies of goods and services in Malta, subject to the following conditions being met:

+ his BU annual turnover during the preceding calendar year is less than the EU threshold of EU 100,000,
and

+ his domestic annual turnover during the preceding calendar year does not exceed the domestic turnover
threshold of EU 35,000.

‘The Commissioner has fifteen working days, after receiving the information, to notify by electronic means the
Member State of establishment of his approval or otherwise.

13 VAT grouping

Where two or more legal persons established or having a fixed establishment in Malta are closely bound to
one another by financial, economic and organisational links, those persons may apply to be treated as a single
taxable person for VAT purposes. In addition, at least one of the applicants should be a taxable person who is
licensed or recognised in terms of the legislation specified in the Schedule to the Regulations.

All legal persons can form part of a VAT group, including “pure” holding companies.

‘Financial links’ exist where any two or more of the following are, directly or indirectly, held as to more
than 90% by the same person or persons (whether a legal person or an individual): (i) the voting rights or
equivalent interests; (ii) the entitlement to profits available for distribution; or (iii) the entitlement to surplus
assets available for distribution on a winding up or equivalent event.

“Organisational links’ shall be deemed to exist where the applicants have a shared management structure,
whether in whole or in part.

The required ‘economic links’ shall be deemed to exist where the activity of each of the applicants is of

the same nature or within the same industry, or where the activities of the applicants are interdependent or
complementary, or where one member of the group carries out activities which are wholly or substantially to
the benefit of any one or more of the other members.

Each member of a VAT Group is jointly and severally liable for the payment of any tax, administrative
penalties and interest arising under the Act.

An application for registration of a VAT group must be made electronically through the VAT online portal of
any one of the applicants but generally, of the chosen Group Reporting Entity.

1.4 Fiscal representative

A taxable person who is established outside Malta and who is required to register for VAT in Malta may
appoint a fiscal representative. There is no obligation for taxable persons established in another EU Member
State to appoint a fiscal representative for Maltese VAT purposes. An instance where a taxable person
established is another EU Member State can appoint a VAT representative in Malta is where, that person
wishes to submit a request for the deferment of the VAT due on the importation of a luxury asset into Malta
(see paragraph 2.3.2 below) and does not wish to set up a bank guarantee or submit a deposit to the MTCA,
both calculated at 0.75% of the value of the asset (capped at EUR | million).

Malta | 395

--- Page 36 ---
In the case of persons established outside the EU, the Commissioner may designate any person established in
Malta who has a business relationship with the non-EU trader as his fiscal representative, unless the non-EU
trader designates another person as such.

A fiscal representative, once appointed, is jointly and severally liable with the person whom he is representing
for all obligations arising under the VAT Act.

‘There are no conditions regarding the designation of a fiscal representative, however the Minister can at any
time decide to enact such regulations for the respective appointment.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Articles 19 and 29 of Council Directive 2006/112/EC (the “EU VAT Directive”) have been fully implemented
in Malta. The transfer by a person of assets belonging to his economic activity shall be treated as neither a
supply of goods nor a supply of services if:

+ the assets are transferred to a taxable person, registered under Article 10 to whom he transfers his
economic activity, or part of that economic activity which is capable of separate operation, as a going
concer; and

+ the said assets are to be used by the transferee in carrying on the same kind of activity, whether or not as
part of an existing economic activity, as that carried on by the transferor; and

+ the said transfer is recorded in the records of the transferor indicating the VAT registration number of the
transferee,

In the event that the transferee is not registered under Article 10 of the VAT Act, the application of the ‘no
supply’ rule requires the pre-approval of the Commissioner.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The disposal of goods free of charge, forming part of the taxable person’s economic activity, where the VAT
on the said goods or the component parts thereof was wholly or partly deducted, shall be treated as a supply
of goods for consideration by that taxable person acting as such,

However, this provision is not applicable to gift of goods made in the course or furtherance of an economic
activity, otherwise than as one forming part of a series of gifts made to the same person, where the cost of the
donor is not more than EUR 50 (excluding VAT), and for gifts of an industrial sample in a form not ordinarily
available for sale to the public.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra~Community supply

‘An importation of goods into Malta, followed by an intra-Community supply of the same goods by the same
taxable person, registered under Article 10 of the VAT Act, is exempt from VAT. The importer must prove aL
the moment of importation that there will be a subsequent exempt intra-Community supply of the goods (i.e.
proof of transport and VAT identification number of final recipient). Additional conditions may be imposed,

including the obligation to provide security and the submission of statutory declarations.

Guidelines for the application of the Onward Supply Relief have been issued by the MTCA.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

In Malta, the payment of VAT due on an importation can be deferred following the receipt of an authorization
from the Commissioner. This is solely granted to the owner of the asset who is designated or recognized as
the importer of record.

396 | Malta

--- Page 37 ---
Specific authorization must be obtained by the taxpayer from the MTCA following the submission of an
application in writing. Such authorization may be granted subject to conditions, including, in certain cases,
the granting of a security (e.g. a bank guarantee or a deposit with the MTCA).

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Local legislation does not specifically refer to consignment transactions, however, simplification procedures
can be applied in Malta, subject to the approval of the Commissioner, in terms of which a request may be
made to permit the consignee to account for the VAT in terms of the reverse charge mechanism.

In the case of “call off stock arrangements” in terms of which a taxable person dispatches or transports goods
from another Member State to a stock in Malta for an intended acquirer whose identity and VAT identification
number are known at the time of the transport or dispatch and who has the right to take goods out of this
stock at his own discretion, at which time the property on the goods is transferred, Maltese VAT legislation
implements ad verbatim the simplification procedure set out in Article 17a of the EU VAT Directive.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

The place of supply of goods which are installed or assembled is the place where such installation takes place.
In such cases the general rule for the place of supply of goods is not applicable. Consequently, the delivery

of goods which are installed or assembled in Malta from another EU Member State is a domestic supply of
goods subject to Maltese VAT.

Where the supplier is not established in Malta and is not registered for VAT purposes in Malta and the
customer is established and registered for VAT purposes in Malta, the reverse charge mechanism applies.

3.3 Distance sales

As from 2021, the rules and thresholds for distance sales have been harmonised across the EU, with Member
States leaving no leeway in this regard. The threshold for distance sales by the supplier in one Member State
to non-taxable persons in another Member State is now EUR 10,000 for intra~Community distance sales
which also includes turnover generated from electronically supplied services, telecommunication services and
broadcasting services. In addition, a threshold of EUR 150 per consignment applies for the application of the
special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules
The simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the EU VAT Directive.
Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e.

in Countries 1, 2 and 3). The transport must be organized by either A or B and the goods must be transported
directly from A in Country | to C in Country 3.

When Malta is the Member State of arrival, the simplification procedure does not apply where the middleman
(B) is established in Malta or registered for VAT in Malta.

Where the middleman (B) is established and/or identified for VAT purposes in the Member State of dispatch
(Country 1) and in a Member State other than the Member State of arrival of the goods (Country 2 - Malta),
B may still apply the simplified triangulation rules, provided that all other conditions are fulfilled and that the
transport is ascribed to the first supply (A-B).

35

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons and taxable persons engaged in
exempt activities is EUR 10,000.

Malta | 397

--- Page 38 ---
3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

The VAT Act permits the Commissioner to apply the use and enjoyment override in order to prevent double
taxation, non-taxation or distortion of competition, in connection with services the place of supply of which
is governed by Articles 44, 45, 56 and 59 of the EU VAT Directive. To date, in practice, this override is
most commonly applied in connection with the hiring of vessels in line with Article 59a(a) of the EU VAT
Directive.

4, Chargeable event and chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, in principle, VAT becomes chargeable at the time the goods or
services arc supplied. However, where an invoice is issued by the fifteenth day of the month following the
date when the chargeable event takes place, the tax becomes chargeable on the date of the invoice.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied.

For tax to become chargeable on advance payments, al] the relevant information concerning the chargeable
event, namely the future delivery or future performance, must already be known and therefore, when the
payment on account is made, the goods or services must be precisely identified. Payments on account of
supplies of goods or services that have not yet been clearly identified cannot be subject to VAT.

43 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or
transported from Malta to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred VAT exempt, shall
be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to an
end.

In the cases of ongoing services which give rise to periodical billing or payments are deemed 1o be supplied at
the end of the period to which the billing or payment refers. Where no statement is raised/payment made, the
services would be regarded as being supplied at intervals of one year.

In the case of such ongoing services for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of
Article 196 BU VAT Directive, if no statement is raised/payment is made, they are regarded as being supplied
on the expiry of each calendar year until such time that the supply comes to an end.

44 Cash accounting

In certain cases and to the extent that certain criteria are met, VAT may become chargeable at the time when
a payment is made and to the extent covered by that payment. The cash accounting regime is available to
persons providing professional services, retailers, civil, mechanical and electrical engineering contractors.

5, Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

In Malta, a taxable person registered under Article 10 of the VAT Act and who has made supplies which
have not been paid, may claim from the Commissioner, the amount of output tax corresponding to the bad
debt to be allowed as a deduction by way of a bad debt relief. Bad debt relief can only be claimed if certain
conditions have been satisfied, including, amongst others

398 | Malta

--- Page 39 ---
+ the output tax in respect of the supply has been accounted for and paid to the MTCA; and
* a final court judgement evidencing that the debt can never be recovered.

‘The application for relief must be made within 12 months of the court judgement.
Guidelines for Bad Debt Relief claims have been issued by the MTCA.

52 Cash Discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1900
18% VAT EUR _ 180
Total to be paid EUR 1180

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days,y ou can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

The VAT Act provides that the taxable value ofa supply shall not include price reductions by way of
discounts for early payment. No official guidance is available on the treatment of cash discounts. In terms
of the general principle, the output VAT of the supplier/input VAT of the customer is the full price less the
amount deducted by way of discount.

53 Open market value

When the consideration is paid or payable wholly or partly in kind, or if the value of the consideration for
a supply cannot be easily determined, the taxable value of that supply shall be the open market value to
the extent that the consideration is paid in kind or cannot be determined. The open market value of goods
or services is the price they would achieve if sold or provided in the open market on the date and in the
condition, they were in when the goods were delivered, or the services were performed.

54 Returnable packaging

The taxable value of a supply shall not include any amount charged to the customer by way of a deposit on
returnable packing of goods where the deposit is lower than the cost of the packaging or where the deposit is
regulated in terms of the Beverage Containers Recycling Regulations or any law which may be substituted
therefor.

but excluding services falling under the exemption laid down in Item 11 of Part Two of the Fifth

+ food for human consumption;
+ pharmaceuticals (reference to Combined Nomenclature (CN or HS) codes).

See paragraph 7.1 below regarding zero rated supplies (VAT exempt with the right of deduction).

7.1 Exemptions with right to deduction (‘zero rated’)

In Malta, the following supplies benefit from a zero rate of VAT, also known as exempt with credit supplies:

* exports and like transactions;

+ international goods traffic;

+ intra-Community supplies;

+ international transport and ancillary services;

+ brokerage/intermediary services;

+ certain supplies of sea vessels and aircrafts, including certain services and ancillary services relating

+ supply of gold;

organised by employer;

+ products used for female sanitary protection; and

Suitable evidence to prove the application of the zero rate includes contracts, certificates (such as Air
Operator’s Certificate), purchase orders, transport documents and payment documents for intra-Community
supplies and the stamped customs documentation for exports.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

In Malta, the following supplies benefit from exemption, whereby no input tax credit can be claimed:

+ letting of immovable property (with exceptions as listed in the VAT Act);
+ transfer of immovable property;

+ insurance services;

+ credit, banking and similar activities;

+ cultural and religious services;

+ sports or physical recreation supplied by non-profit making organizations;

+ services by independent groups;

* postal services;

+ supply of goods in respect of which supplier did not qualify for input tax credit;
* broadcasting:

+ exemption for small enterprises.

Malta does not allow taxable persons a right of option for taxation in respect of financial services transactions.
The letting of immovable property is subject to VAT where in particular, it is made by a limited liability
company to a person who is registered under Article 10 of the VAT Act for the purposes of his economic
activity. The supply of both newly built and older immovable property is exempt (without credit).

7.3 Warehousing
7.3.1 VAT warehouse

Malta has no VAT warehousing arrangements.

7.3.2 Bonded (customs) warehouse — check with exemption

Goods imported into Malta from a third territory may be placed in a customs warehouse, if such goods are
placed under a customs duty suspension regime. This will provide a temporary exemption from customs duty
and VAT chargeable on the entry of goods in Malta, Customs duty and VAT will become due by the customer
when the goods are released from the warehouse into free circulation.

‘The release of goods from a customs duty suspension regime does not constitute an importation if the goods
are subsequently placed under another customs regime.

‘The supply of goods while under a customs duty suspension regime is exempt from VAT (‘zero-rated’).
8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In Malta, the reverse charge mechanism applies in respect of supplies of services falling within the scope of
Article 44 of the EU VAT Directive (pursuant to Article 196 of the EU VAT Directive).

Furthermore, in terms of Article 194 of the EU VAT Directive, the reverse charge mechanism applies to
supplies of goods and services by non-established suppliers provided certain criteria are satisfied.
Malta | 401

--- Page 42 ---
8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
Where a supplier is not established in Malta nor registered for VAT purposes in Matta, and the supply of
either goods or services, is made to a taxable person or a non-taxable legal person, who is established and
registered for VAT purposes in Malta (under Article 10 or 12 of the VAT Act), the obligation to account for
and pay the tax is shifted onto the customer and the non-resident supplier will not be obliged to register for
VAT purposes in Malta. If however the non-established supplier is registered for VAT purposes in Malta, the
reverse charge would not apply.

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism ~ specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)
Domestic reverse charge rules are not applicable in Malta. The Commissioner may however direct that the
reverse charge applies to specific transactions, as a revenuc protection measure. In practice this applies to
specific contracts of supply in the construction industry.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under Maltese law, a person who is a representative of another person such as any secretary, manager,
director, liquidator or other principal officer of an entity or of a body of persons shall be jointly and severally
liable for the tax together with the person of whom he is a representative.

In addition, for VAT grouping purposes each member of a VAT group shall be jointly and severally liable for
the payment of any tax, administrative penalties and interest which would have become due and payable by

the group reporting entity.

In line with the Guidelines: VAT Import One-Stop-Shop (IOSS) Intermediary Conditions, issued by the MTCA
on 15 April 2021, an intermediary who is defined as a taxable person established in the Community appointed
as such by another taxable person carrying out distance sales of goods imported from third territories or third
countries shal] be the person fiable for payment of the VAT and must also fulfil the obligations laid down in
the relevant special scheme in the name and on behalf of the taxable person.

9. Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

Input VAT incurred on the following expenditure is not recoverable:

+ non-business expenditure;

+ purchase, repair and maintenance, lease, fuelling and hiring of vehicles, vessels and aircraft (subject to
exceptions);

+ business and employee entertainment;

+ tobacco or tobacco products and alcoholic beverages;

+ works of art, collectors” items and antiques; and

+ business expenditure exclusively attributable to exempt without credit supplies of a taxable person,

Exceptions may apply.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT can be claimed through the submission of the VAT return. The excess VAT is refundable
within five months from the expiration of the time allowed for the furnishing of the tax return for that tax
period or from the day on which the said return has been furnished to the Commissioner, whichever is the
later.

In the event that the excess input VAT is not paid within the timeframe mentioned above, interest shall be

due by the Commissioner to the person to whom a refund is due from the date on which the refund becomes
payable until the date when it is actually paid.

402 | Malta

--- Page 43 ---
9.3 Capital goods — adjustment periods

+ Immovable property - 20 years
+ Other capital goods - 5 years

The period of adjustment, also known as reference period, starts to elapse from the first day of the first
month of the tax period during which the right to deduct arises or during which the goods are first used. The
adjustment for capital goods is also applicable for services, which includes contract of works consisting of
construction contracts.

9.4 VAT refund for foreigners

9.41 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

If an EU taxable person is not established in Malta and carries out transactions giving rise to a right of
deduction (i,¢. can be considered to be an eligible applicant) then a VAT refund claim must be submitted
through an electronic portal of the EU Member State where the eligible applicant is established.

When the EU taxable person carries out an economic activity in the EU Member State where he is established
both transactions that give rise to a right of deduction and transactions that do not give rise to a right of
deduction in that EU Member State, only such proportion of the VAT is refundable as is attributable to the
former transactions in accordance with Article 173 of the EU VAT Directive as applied by the EU Member
State in which the claimant is established.

Once eligibility is established, the EU taxable person may only apply for a refund on condition that during the
refund period he did not have in Malta the seat of his economic activity, or a fixed establishment from which
he effected business transactions. or, if no such seat or fixed establishment existed, a domicile or normal place
of residence.

Furthermore, the VAT refund is granted on conditions that during the refund period the claimant did not
supply any goods or services in Malta, with the exception of the following transactions:

+ transport and ancillary services thereto that are exempt and that are part of the full taxable value, as
specified under certain conditions; and

+ services and goods supplied by such taxable person not established in Malta in cases where tax is liable to
be paid solely by the persons to whom they are supplied (i.e. the reverse charge applics).

If the refund application relates to a refund period of a calendar year or the remainder ofa calendar year, the
amount of VAT may not be less than EUR 50.

When the refund application relates to a refund period of less than one year but not less than three months, the
amount of VAT for which a refund is applied for may not be less than EUR 400, An application for a refund
may only be made when the refund period is not less than three calendar months. Refund applications may
however relate to a period of less than three calendar months when the period represents the remainder of a
calendar year.

‘The maximum refund period is not more than one year.

An application is only valid if it is submitted to the EU Member State where the eligible applicant is
established at the latest by 30 September of the calendar year following the refund period. Moreover, the
application shall be considered submitted, only if all the required information is filled in. The eligible
applicant will receive an electronic confirmation of receipt from the EU Member State where he is
established,

In addition to the refund application, the claimant is required to submit by electronic means a copy of the
invoice or importation document with the refund application where the taxable net value on an invoice or
importation document is EUR. 1,000 or more, Where the invoice concerns fuel, the threshold is EUR 250.

Malta | 403

--- Page 44 ---
In addition, the claimant may be required to provide a description of his business activity by using the
harmonised codes determined in accordance with the second subparagraph of Article 34a(3) of Council
Regulation (EU) No 1798/2003.

‘The MTCA may also specify which language or languages are to be used for the provision of information in
the refund application or of possible additional information.

When the MTCA determine that they do not have all the relevant information on which to make a decision
in respect of the whole or a part of the refund application, they may request, by electronic means, additional
information, in particular from the claimant, or from the authorities of the EU Member State where the
eligible applicant is established, within four months from the date of receipt of the application.

The additional information requested may include the submission of the original or a copy of the relevant
invoice or import document when the MTCA have reasonable doubts regarding the validity or accuracy of a
particular claim. The additional information must be provided to the MTCA within one month of the date on
which the request reaches the person to whom it is addressed.

The period available for the decision in respect of the whole or a part of the refund application shall always
be at least 6 months from the date of receipt of the application by the Commissioner. Where further additional
information is requested, the claimant will be notified of the decision in respect of the whole or a part of the
refund application within 8 months of receipt of the application.

Approved refunds will be paid at the latest within 10 working days of the expiry of the above-mentioned
applicable deadline for decisions.

‘Appeals against decisions to refuuse a refund application may be made by the claimant to the Administrative
Review Tribunal in terms of Article 44 and the Ninth Schedule to the VAT Act. Any of the parties to an appeal
or a reference to the Tribunal who feels aggrieved by the decision of that Tribunal may, by means of an
application to be filed within 30 days from the date on which the decision appealed from is notified to him,
appeal against that decision on a question of law only.

An EU taxable person is allowed to deduct the VAT he paid on his purchases insofar as the goods or services
are used for his business activities. However, there is no right to deduct input VAT if he uses them for an
exempt activity or is not obliged to charge VAT on his outputs (e.g. schools, banks, insurance companies,
small businesses under the exemption threshold etc).

Link to the webpage where more information can be found is as follows: https://tinyurl.com/3nyze5sj

9.4.2 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

The relevant legislation does not require reciprocity when refunding VAT to taxable persons not established in
the territory of the Community and in practice no such requirement is typically imposed.

A taxable person not established in the territory of the Community may submit a claim for a refund of Malta
VAT paid in accordance with the provisions laid down in Council Directive 86/560/EC and would be entitled
to claim a refund of input VAT if that person does not have in Malta:

+ his place of his economic activity; or
+ a fixed establishment from which business transactions are effected; or
+ his domicile; or

+ his permanent place of residence; or

+ avalid VAT registration number issued by the MTCA.

Also, during the same period that person must not have supplied or deemed to have supplied, any goods or
services in Malta with the exception of:

404 | Malta

--- Page 45 ---
+ transport and ancillary services thereto exempted pursuant, insofar as these form part of the taxable value,
in accordance with the conditions laid down in the VAT Act; and

+ services and goods supplied by such taxable person not established in Malta in cases where tax is liable to
be paid solely by the persons to whom they are supplied (i.e. the reverse charge applies).

The claim is subject to the conditions laid down in Legal Notice 220 of 2004.

‘The refund application must be submitted to the MTCA, within six months from the end of the calendar year
in which the tax became chargeable. This is made online by submitting the prescribed form (please refer

to link below) together with other relevant documentation. The application itself includes notes on how to
complete the application,

Link to the application and relevant declarations: https://tinyurl.com/nhzdvxt8

When the refund application relates to a refund period of a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year,
the amount of VAT may not be less than EUR 25.

When the refund application relates to a refund period of less than one calendar year but not less than 3
months, the amount of VAT for which a refund is applied for may not be less than BUR 200.

‘The application shall be considered submitted, only if the taxable person has filled in all the required
information.

‘The application shall contain the following information and, or documentation:

+ an official certificate drawn up on the form prescribed in the Second Schedule to Legal Notice 220 of
2004, completed either in the Maltese or En; language, and endorsed by the competent authority of
the country where that taxable person is registered for VAT or where he exercises his economic activity;

+ the original valid invoices and/or valid importation documents; and

*  anattest of reciprocity for taxable persons not established within the EU except for taxable persons
providing electronically supplied services.

Refunds may not be granted:

+ with respect to refunds to taxable persons not established in the territory of the Community, under
conditions which are more favourable than those applied to Community taxable persons; and

+ on supplies made by that taxable person where his business would, by its nature, be treated as exempt
without credit if such business had been carried out in Malta.

Decisions concerning applications for refund shall be given within six months from the date when the
applications, accompanied by all the necessary documents required under these regulations for the
examination of the application, are received by the Maltese tax authorities.

Refunds shall be made before the end of the six month period mentioned above, at the applicant’s request,
either in Malta or in the country where he is established. In the latter case, the bank charges for the transfer
shall be payable by the applicant.

Any of the parties to an appeal or a reference to the Tribunal who feels aggrieved by the decision of that
Tribunal may, by means of an application, appeal against that decision on a question of law only.

Link to the webpage where more information can be found is as follows: https://tinyurl.com/3nyze5sj
