10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Tax Invoice content
A valid VAT invoice has to contain the following information:

+ date on which the invoice is issued;

+ sequential number, based on one or more series, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

+ name, address and VAT identification number of supplier;

* name, address and VAT identification number of customer;

* quantity and nature of the goods supplied or the extent and nature of the services rendered;

+ the time of the supply;

+ taxable value per rate or exemption, the unit price exclusive of tax and any discounts or rebates if they are
not included in the unit price;

+ the VAT rate applied;

+ the VAT amount payable, except where a special arrangement is applied under which, in accordance with
the VAT Act, such a detail is excluded;

* where the person liable for payment of VAT is a tax representative in another Member State, the name,
address and VAT identification number of that tax representative;

+ incase of self-billing a corresponding reference “self-billing” is required;

+ if VAT is chargeable at time of payment, the words “cash accounting”;

* _ ifno VAT is chargeable, a reference to the provisions of the VAT Act or EU VAT Directive indicating the
basis;

+ if VAT is payable by the customer, the words “reverse charge”;

+ in the case of an intra-Community supply of new means of transport, a description with reference to the
definition in the VAT Act;

+ ifa margin scheme has been applied, a reference to the margin scheme, e.g.:
+ “margin scheme - second-hand goods”;
+ “margin scheme - works of art”:
+ “margin scheme - collector's items and antiques” and
+ “margin scheme + ravel agents”.

As regards invoices in respect of supplies of goods or services supplied in Malta and invoices received by
taxable persons established in Malta, the Commissioner may, for certain taxable persons or certain cases,
require translation into Maltese or English.

10.1.2 Time limits

A valid VAT invoice must be issued not later than the 15" day of the month following that in which the
chargeable event takes place or the date on which a payment is received, whichever is the earlier. In the case
of an intra-Community supply of goods, an invoice should be issued by not later than the 15* day of the
month following that in which the chargeable takes place, completely disregarding the date on which the
payment is received.

10.1.3 Currency
The invoice can be expressed in any currency, however the VAT amount must be expressed in Euro.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
In order to determine the taxable amount of a transaction expressed in a currency other than in Euro, the
exchange rate applicable shall be the latest selling rate recorded, at the time the tax becomes chargeable,
applied by commercial banks in Malta or published by the European Central Bank.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Self-invoicing is possible-a_ Tax invoice may be drawn up by the customer where there is a prior agreement
between the supplier and the customer and provided that a procedure exists for the acceptance of each tax

406 | Malta

--- Page 47 ---
invoice by the taxable person supplying the goods or services. The Commissioner may require that such
invoices be issued in the name and on behalf of the taxable person.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice
Asimplified invoice may be issued if the amount of the invoice is not higher than EUR 100.

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

* a sequential number;

+ name, address and the VAT identification number of the supplier of the goods and/or services;

+ the VAT identification number of the customer;

+ a description sufficient to identify the goods or services supplied:

+ the VAT amount payable or the information needed to calculate it; and

+ ifthe simplified invoice is in reference to a document or message treated as an invoice, an unambiguous
reference to the initial invoice and the details that are being amended.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices must be retained for a period of six years from the end of the year to which they relate. However,
in the case of capital goods which fall within the remit of the capital goods scheme, the six year period
shall commence, in the case of immovable property once the twenty year period elapses, and in the case of
moveable property once the five year period elapses

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing
Invoices can be sent by electronic means subject to certain conditions:

» they must contain the same details as required for printed invoices;

* such invoices must be accepted by the person acquiring such goods or services; and

* the authenticity of the origin, the integrity of the content and the legibility of an invoice must be
guaranteed.

10.2 VAT Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The standard VAT return period is in principle quarterly, however, may also be monthly or yearly. The return
can be submitted electronically through the VAT online portal of the MICA.

In the case of taxable persons registered under Article 11 as a small enterprise, a yearly declaration must be
submitted electronically to the MTCA.

Furthermore, for taxable persons established in Malta providing cross-border trade under the SME Scheme,
and registered under Article 11A are obliged to submit quarterly declarations electronically by not later than
the last day of the month following the end of the respective calendar quarter.

An Article 12 registered taxable person is obliged to furnish the Commissioner with a notice of payment and
declarations electronically, of his intra~Community acquisitions and purchases made, on a monthly basis.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

A taxable person identified for VAT purposes under Article 10 is required to submit a VAT Return for each
VAT period. The VAT periods typically cover three (3) month periods and are generally (but not necessarily),
calendar quarters. However, the Commissioner may allow a taxable person to have monthly (e.g. exporting
companies) or anmual VAT periods.

A VAT return together with the respective VAT payment (if any) must be submitted by the 15th day of the
second month following end of the VAT period i.e. within one month and fifteen days from end of that VAT
period. Should the 15th fall on a weekend or on a public holiday, the deadline would be extended to the next

Malta | 407

--- Page 48 ---
working day. When a VAT retum is submitted electronically, the deadline is extended by an additional seven
days.

Other deadlines apply for an Article 11, Article 11A, Article 1B or Article 12 registered person.

10.2.3 Additional VAT returns
There are no additional VAT returns in Malta.

10.2.4 European sales listing
The European sales listing must be submitted on a monthly basis in the case of intra-Community supplies of
goods. However, if the value of intra~Community supplies of goods does not exceed EUR 50,000 in a given
quarter or any of the previous four quarters, such form can be submitted on a calendar quarter basis. The
European sales listing for services can be submitted on a quarterly basis and no threshold applies. The form
must be filed within fifleen days from the end of the month or calendar quarter and can only be submitted in
electronic format.

10.2.5 National recapitulative statement
There are no national recapitulative statements in Malta.

10.2.6 Tax assessment period
‘The tax assessment period in Multa is six years from the end of the relevant tax period. However, when an

assessment refers to an adjustment made in relation to capital goods, the six years shall start from the end of
the adjustment period (see also paragraph 9.3).

Similarly, when an adjustment to a particular VAT return is made, the registered person is legally obliged to
keep and provide requested documents within a period of six years which period shall start to run from the
date on which a correction is received and duly acknowledged by the Commissioner.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
Any taxable person who is required to be registered under Article 10 or Article 12 of the VAT Act, will incur
penalties for late registration as follows.

10.3.2 Article 10 Late Registration

In principle the penalty for late registration is the higher of 1% of the output tax over the deductions for the
first tax period and EUR 20 per month of delay. If the output tax over the deductions for the first tax period
following registration is EUR 2,000 or less, the administrative penalty for late registration shall not exceed
EUR 250. Where the excess of the output tax over the deductions for the first tax period following registration
is more than EUR 2,000 such administrative penalty shall not exceed 20% of such excess.

10.3.3 Article 11 and Article 11A Late Registration

In principle the penalty for late registration is that of EUR 20 per month of delay. However, such
administrative penalty shall in no case exceed EUR 250 for each such notice or information,

10.3.4 Article 12 Late Registration
In principle the penalty for late registration is the higher of 1% of the VAT due on intra-Community
acquisitions and/or services purchased from outside Malta and EUR 20 per month of delay. Where the tax
chargeable on the intra-Community acquisitions or the services received or both such intra-Community
acquisitions and services received, in respect of which that person is required to make such application is
EUR 2,000 or less, such administrative penalty shall not exceed EUR 250.

Where the tax chargeable on the intra~Community acquisitions or the services received or both such intra-
Community acquisitions and services received, in respect of which that taxable person is required to make
such application is more than EUR 2,000 such administrative penalty shall not exceed 20% of such tax
chargeable.

408 | Malta

--- Page 49 ---
10.3.5 Default penalties for not filing an Article 10 VAT return or a European sales listing on time

‘The penalty for the delay in submitting a VAT retum is the higher of 1% of excess of output tax over
deductions and EUR 20, for every month of delay. Where the tax payable does not exceed EUR 250 such
administrative penalty shall not exceed the equivalent of the tax payable or EUR 50, whichever is the greater.
In all other circumstances, the administrative penalty shall not exceed EUR 250.

‘The penalty for failure to submit a Declaration (Small Undertakings) or a Recapitulative Statement is EUR 50
per month of delay, capped at EUR 600.

10.3.6 Default penalties for not filing an Article 11, Article 11A or Article 12 declaration

The penalty for not furnishing a declaration within the time stipulated is that of EUR 10 for every month of
delay, provided that the administrative penalty shall not exceed EUR 120 for each declaration.

10.3.7 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities

See 10.2.2 above. Also, interest of 0.60% shall be due on any tax which is not paid by the date on which it
becomes payable, for each month or part thereof during which that tax remains unpaid.

10.3.8 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

When a VAT return contains an understatement of output tax or an overstatement of input tax, an
administrative penalty of 20% of the understated output tax or overstated input tax is due.

However, if the error is corrected by the taxable person before an assessment is raised, the administrative
penalty can be reduced to 10%. This is subject to the cooperation with the Commissioner and payment of tax,
interest and penalties due within one month.

10.4 Intrastat

Intrastat declarations must be submitted by the 10 day of the month following that of the movement of the
goods.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
The current Intrastat minimum threshold for both the arrival and the dispatches is EUR 700 per annum.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
The information which needs to be reported for both the arrival and the dispatches statistics is:

+ EU Member State of origin/destination;
+ VAT identification number;

+ period;

+ terms of delivery:

+ mode of transport;

+ net weight of the goods;

+ invoice value of the goods in EUR; and
+ nature of document,

Additional information which needs to be reported for arrival statistics is:

+ country and region of origin;

* commodity code;

+ date, location and signature; and

* items in total.

Additional information which needs to be reported for dispatches statistics is:
* trader number;

+ third-party declarant;

+ line number;

* region of destination;

+ — serial number of the report;

+ country of dispatch; and

+ number of lines

There are no additional requirements.

The Netherlands

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is based in the Netherlands and implemented through the “Wet op
de omzethelasting 1968”. VAT is referred to in the Netherlands as “omzethelasting” or “btw”. The overseas
islands do not belong to the Netherlands for VAT purposes.

1. Taxable persons

a Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Small businesses may apply an exemption (Dutch abbreviation: “the KOR"). Both natural persons and legal
entities are eligible for the small businesses scheme. Certain conditions need to be met if the KOR is to be
applied:

* the small business must be established in the Netherlands or have a fixed establishment there (businesses
established in other EU Member States can also apply the KOR, see below);

+ the annual turnover realized in the Netherlands must not exceed EUR 20,000; and

* — the small business must have applied for the KOR in writing and the Tax Authorities must not have
refused the application.

This special scheme is not applicable in the case of:

+ the supply of immovable or movable property for which an adjustment period applies;

+ the supply or acquisition of a new means of transport to or from another EU Member State;

+ goods, supplies and services that are performed outside the Netherlands; and

+ goods and services purchased from foreign suppliers, which are subject to VAT in the Netherlands for
which the small business is liable under the reverse-charge mechanism.

For the exemption to apply, the business must not charge VAT on its invoices issued to its customers. The
business cannot recover the VAT charged on purchased goods and services. It also does not have to keep a
complete set of accounts or file regular VAT returns, The obligation to keep copies of purchase invoices has
not been canceled.

The KOR is optional. Businesses are not obliged to apply the KOR, not even if they meet all the conditions.
Ifa business wishes to apply the KOR, it must opt for it at least 4 weeks before the start of the taxable period
from which he wants to apply the KOR. As soon as a business opts for the application of the KOR, it must
continue to do so until either the threshold is exceeded or the business notifies the tax authorities at least 4
weeks in advance that it no longer wishes to apply the KOR. The KOR will not be applicable from the start of
the next quarter.

The registration of domestic taxable persons is usually started through the registration at the Chamber of
Commerce. The following information must be provided for the registration:

+ aregistration form; and
+ the articles of association,

Further information can be downloaded at the following link: hetps://tinyurl.comy/2rjufux2

Small businesses from other EU Member States who apply the scheme for small businesses in the country of
establishment:

+ with an annual turnover in the BU of less than EUR 100,000; and

* an annual turnover of less than EUR 20,000 in the Netherlands

may also choose to apply the exemption (KOR) in the Netherlands as of 2025.

13 VAT grouping
The Dutch VAT legislation provides a possibility for VAT grouping,

VAT grouping is usually based on the choice of the taxable persons but can also be invoked by the tax
authorities on the basis of their own findings (e.g. in a tax audit).

The persons who want to form a VAT group may submit an application to the tax authorities. The tax
authorities issue a decision regarding the creation of a VAT group or inclusion of a new member in an existing
VAT group. According to a decision of the Supreme Court (Hoge Raad), the VAT group can also exist without
the decision of the tax authorities, from the moment all substantive criteria for its existence are met and the
relevant entities act as if they are joined in a VAT group.

Where two or more persons established in the Netherlands are closely bound to one another by financial,
economic and organizational links, those persons are treated as a single taxable person for VAT purposes.

Financial links exist if more than 50% of the shares of the members are directly or indirectly owned by the
same person(s), including the attached voting rights. Organizational links exist if the members are under
common management or where the management of one member is actually subordinate to that of another.
Economic links exist where members have mutual economic relations that are not negligible.

Only taxable persons can form part of a VAT group, which excludes “pure” holding companies, unless they
actually control the decision-making process within the commercial group. These holding companies can only
be part of a VAT group if applied for in writing.

Foreign companies which are not established in the Netherlands cannot generally be included in the VAT
group. However, the non-resident company with a fixed establishment in the Netherlands can be included
in the VAT group. If a head office and/or fixed establishment are members of a VAT group in the BU, the

services provided between them are considered taxable.

412 | The Netherlands

--- Page 53 ---
‘The VAT group receives a new common VAT identification (JD) number. The members of the VAT group keep
their separate VAT ID numbers which they use for, for example, intra-Community transactions.

Asa general rule, the VAT group files one VAT return for the group. It is also possible to file VAT returns and
other reports (e.g. the European sales list) separately. Members of the VAT group can apply for separate VAT
reporting,

The supplies of the goods or services within the VAT group are not subject to Dutch VAT.

‘The members of the VAT group are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of the group as
from the moment and for the period they are part of the VAT group as confirmed in writing by the Dutch Tax
Authorities, Without this written confirmation, no joint and several liability exists.

1.4 Fiscal representative

A foreign taxable person from EU or non-EU countries does not have to appoint a fiscal representative for
VAT registering in the Netherlands. It is possible to VAT register directly in the Netherlands.

However, a foreign taxable person must appoint a fiscal representative if the foreign taxable person wishes to
apply for the deferred import VAT payment license (so-called Article 23 license) (see also paragraph 2.32). A
fiscal representative is also required for zero rating certain supplies or purchases of goods in a VAT warehouse
(see also paragraph 7.1.3) or an excise duty warehouse. A foreign taxable person must also appoint a fiscal
representative if it takes goods out of the VAT warehouse.

A fiscal representative must have a (limited or general) license from the tax authorities, A fiscal representative
must live or be established in the Netherlands and must meet certain requirements. For example, he should
not have been convicted of tax offenses within the past 5 years, In addition, a bank guarantee or security
deposit into the bank account of the authorities is required by the fiscal representative.

A fiscal representative is jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT and penalties due by his principal
(the foreign taxable person). If the fiscal representative has a limited license, he is allowed to report only
certain types of transactions by his principal:

+ the importation and subsequent supplies by the principal in the Netherlands;
+ trade in a VAT- or excise warehouse; and
+ intra-Community acquisitions of excise goods in the Netherlands,

The liability of a fiscal representative with a limited license’s is unlimited.

The fiscal representative with a limited license files one single return for all its customers (principals)
combined. All imports and subsequent sales by these principals are then added up into one single amount
reported in the return filed with the authorities.

A fiscal representative with a general license can only be appointed when the foreign taxable person has its
own Dutch VAT ID number. Therefore, there is no limitation to the types of transactions that can be reported
by the fiscal representative. In addition, his liability is limited per year to the amount of the security deposit
required by the authorities,

A foreign taxable person can have only one fiscal representative with a general license, but he can appoint
multiple fiscal representatives with a limited license for individual flows or transactions,

Strict rules apply in relation to the invoicing by the foreign taxable person when he has appointed a fiscal

representative in the Netherlands. On all sales invoices for supplies from the Netherlands the details of the
Dutch fiscal representative must be mentioned.

‘The Netherlands | 413

--- Page 54 ---
2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Article 34 of the Dutch VAT Act 1968 implements Articles 19 and 29 of the LU VAT Directive and reads as
follows:

“Tn the case of a transfer of all or part of a totality of goods, whether or not for compensation or in the form
of a contribution to a company, no supplies of goods or services are deemed to be taking place and the entity
to which the goods are transferred takes the place of the transferring entity, unless otherwise stipulated by
Ministerial Order”.

Although the transfer of a business that meets these legal conditions is not taxed with VAT, there are VAT
consequences. The taxable person that takes over the business of the transferring entity takes over all rights
and obligations, claims, liabilities and responsibilities for VAT such as the remainder of the adjustment
periods for (im)movable tangible property (see also paragraph 9.3).

2.2 Supplies free of charge

In the Netherlands, Articles 16 and 26 EU VAT Directive only apply to free supplies of goods and services
under specific circumstances. Under a standstill provision, the Netherlands disallows VAT deduction on costs
for private use by staff and for gifts to businesses and private individuals that, if they would have purchased
the goods or services themselves, would not be able to fully deduct the VAT on those purchases. This system,
referred to as ‘BUA’ or ‘BUA adjustment’ is included in paragraph 9.1.

Free supplies of goods or services not covered by BUA, are covered by Articles 16 and 26, In relation to
Article 16, the free supply of ‘gifts of small value’ is not taxed in the Netherlands if the purchase price per
item is less than EUR 15 (excluding VAT). This is not a cumulative threshold and not limited to a specific
period —a business can give away as many ‘gifts of small value’ to the same recipient as it wants, without
VAT consequences.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1. Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

Itis possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into the Netherlands if it is followed by av
intra-Community supply. However, many conditions must be met in order to apply the exemption, such as a
license, a VAT registration number of the importer or its fiscal representative, and a security. Due to the many
conditions, the exemption is hardly ever used. It is easier to apply for an import VAT deferment license (and,
in some cases, appoint a fiscal representative for that purpose) (see paragraph 1.4 and 2.3,2).

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation
When goods are imported into the Netherlands and cleared for free circulation in the EU, Dutch import VAT
is payable unless a deferment system can be applied to the import VAT. Depending on the type of goods this
system is either obligatory (certain raw materials) or the importer can use a license. Dutch legal entities and
Dutch fixed establishments can apply for a special deferment license (code number) to import goods from
non-BU countries. With this license, importers avoid payment of VAT at the time of importation. Instead, with
the deferment system the importer will have to account for the import VAT due as payable in its Dutch VAT
return (in box 4a). If the importer is entitled to a full deduction, the VAT paid can be reclaimed in box 5b of
the same VAT return. On balance, there is no payment of VAT. Therefore the Dutch VAT deferment system
has a cash flow advantage for the importer.

The application of the deferred payment system is obligatory for:

+ all taxable persons and legal entities importing goods listed in Appendix A of the VAT Order (Bijlage A,
Uitvoeringsbeschikking omzetbelasting 1968). This is also compulsory for non-residents. The goods listed
in Appendix A are mainly raw materials not suitable for private use; and

414 | The Netherlands

--- Page 55 ---
+ taxable persons and legal entities (non-taxable persons) provisioning means of transport with goods which
are not in free circulation.

In other cases it is possible to apply for an import VAT deferment license from the tax authorities. Permission
will only be granted if:

+ the applicant is a resident. A non-resident ~ not having a fixed establishment in the Netherlands — will not
be granted the permit unless a fiscal representative is appointed:

+ the applicant must import goods on a regular basis; and

+ the applicant must keep a clear administrative record of the imported goods.

Taxable persons that are not established in the Netherlands can use the VAT deferment code number and apply
postponed accounting on import if they appoint a fiscal representative (see also 1.4). A foreign business can
either appoint a limited fiscal representative or a general fiscal representative for the import of goods in the
Netherlands. A limited representative can only be used for the importation of the goods and the subsequent
supply. This limited fiscal representative uses his own deferment code number and fulfills the VAT obligation
under its own VAT ID number. If the foreign taxable person appoints a limited fiscal representative in the
Netherlands, it does not need to be registered for VAT purposes itself. A foreign taxable person can have
multiple limited fiscal representatives. However, he can only appoint one general fiscal representative in the
Netherlands. When a fiscal representative with a general license is appointed, the foreign taxable person will
be registered himself, can apply for his own license to defer the import VAT and all transactions are reported
in his own VAT return,

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

The transfer of goods by a VAT registered taxable person from one EU Member State to the Netherlands to
create a stock of goods from which their customer can ‘call-off” (use and pay for) the goods as and when they
require them, can be described as call-off stock. If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a
transfer of own goods followed by a local supply, but an intra-Community supply to his customer at the time
that the goods are called off. The rules relating to this simplification, however, don’t allow Member States
much room for maneuvering.

3.2 Installation supplies

Certain construction and installation supplies are treated as supplied where they are constructed or installed.
Tn the Netherlands, there is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly”, The
Dutch VAT Law only uses the notion of attaching a good to another good. It is assumed that if the attached
goods are the subject of the agreement and retain their identity after being attached, an installation supply
occurs.

Construction work is, as a general rule, considered a supply of services related to immovable property.
Construction work is seen as a supply of goods if it produces a new building.

On both supplies the reverse charge may apply (see also paragraphs 8.1.1 and 8.1.2).

33 Distance sales

From 2021, the rules and thresholds for distance sales (both intra-Community distance sales and distance
sales of imported goods) have been harmonized across the EU with no leeway for Member States. The
distance selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT registration
number (e.g. private individuals) is now EUR 10,000 for intra-Community distance sales. Turnover
generated from the supply of “electronically supplied services’, telecommunication services and broadcasting
services are included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment applies for the
application of the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

‘The Netherlands | 415

--- Page 56 ---
34 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Dutch VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have mostly been implemented in line with
the EU VAT Directive.

Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e.

in Countries 1, 2 and 3). The transport must be organized by either A or B. IB arranged the transport, B must
have provided A with a valid VAT ID number of another country than the country of dispatch and arrival. ‘The
goods must be transported directly from A in Country 1 to C in Country 3.

The simplified triangulation rules can be applied if (among other conditions):

+ the Netherlands is the country of arrival of the goods (i.e. Country 3);

* the person to whom the subsequent supply is rendered (C) is established (not just VAT registered!) for
VAT purposes in the Netherlands; and

*  Bprovides A with a valid VAT ID number from another country than the country of dispatch or arrival;

+ where the Netherlands is the EU Member State of arrival of the goods, the wording of the Dutch
legislation does not preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is also VAT
registered in the Netherlands. It does preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman
B is established in the Netherlands. The Dutch VAT Act still mentions that B must not be registered in the
country of dispatch. But this is not in line with ECJ Case Hans Biihler.

The Dutch Tax Authorities have published guidance on the application of the simplified triangulation rules.
According to this guidance, the simplified triangulation can also be applied to a three-party transaction in a
chain of more than three businesses. The example given in the guidance is a four-party transaction, where A
is established in MS1, B is established in the Netherlands, and both C and D are established in France. The
goods are transported directly from A to D where B arranges that transportation. In this example, the Tax
Authorities agree that the simplified triangulation can be applied to the transaction between parties A, B and C
if all other requirements are met. Of course France should also agree with this solution.

35 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons engaged in exempt
activities and flat-rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000.

3.6 _ Effective use and enjoyment

The Netherlands have not implemented any effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59a sub a of
the EU VAT Directive. The Netherlands did, however, implement effective use and enjoyment rules based on
Article 59a sub b of the EU VAT Directive in relation to:

+ B2C rental of means of transport by a non-EU established service provider to a Dutch private person or a
Dutch legal entity which is not a business for VAT purposes; and
+ the following services rendered by a non-EU established company to a Dutch legal entity which is not a
business for VAT purposes:
*  transfers/assignments of copyrights, etc.;
+ advertising services;
+ consultancy, etc., data processing, the provision of information;
+ obligation to refrain from pursuing or exercising a business activity or right;
+ banking, financial and insurance services, except hire of safes:
+ supply of staff and
+ _ hiring out of movable property, except all means of transport.

The place of supply of the above-mentioned services if situated outside the EU, is considered to be the
Netherlands if the effective use and enjoyment of the services takes place in the Netherlands.

416 | The Netherlands

--- Page 57 ---
4. Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied.

‘These principles, however, ate overruled if there is an obligation to issue an invoice. In those cases, VAT
becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice or ultimately when the invoice should have been issued,
ie. on the 15% day of the month following the chargeable event. This rule applies even if the invoice is issued
before the taxable transaction takes place. The VAT on the purchase of a service which falls under the main rules
of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT Directive, is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied, If invoices have to be issued for these transactions,
the invoice date determines the tax point, under the rule mentioned in 4.1 above.

4.3. Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or trans-
ported from the Netherlands to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred VAT exempt,
shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to
an end.

Services ongoing over a period of more than I year, that do not give rise to settlements or payments in that
period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of Article 196 EU VAT Di-
rective, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of the
services ceases.

The VAT due on private use of goods belonging to the business and free of charge services provided by the
business to its staff (Article 26 EU VAT Directive), becomes payable at the end of the financial year. If those
services have not been finalized, the VAT due relating to the relevant financial year becomes payable at the
end of that financial year.

4.4 Cash accounting

In certain cases, it is allowed to pay VAT to the Dutch state on the basis of cash received during the respective
VAT period as opposed to making payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during the period

The cash accounting regime is available to persons engaged in the supply of taxable services or goods almost
exclusively to private persons and for which there is no obligation to issue an invoice. If an invoice is sent,
the receiver of the invoice can reclaim the VAT upon receipt of the invoice under the normal conditions. He
doesn’t have to wait until he paid the invoice.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

It is possible to reclaim the VAT for invoices due that were left unpaid for one year after the consideration has
become due. The VAT reclaim can be made through the periodical VAT return. It is possible to reclaim the
VAT for invoices due at an earlier stage, but the supplier will have to make plausible that the customer did not
and will not pay. If (partial) payment is received once the VAT reclaim has been filed, the supplier is required
to remit VAT to the extent payment is received.

The Netherlands | 417

--- Page 58 ---
The customer has to repay the VAT originally deducted on the invoice due when it can be reasonably
suspected that he will not pay the invoice. The VAT deducted by the customer has to be repaid to the tax
authorities ultimately when one year has passed and the customer still has not paid.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 100
21% VAT EUR_210
Total to be paid EUR 1210

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days,
you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

If payment is indeed received within 14 days, the supplier still had to pay EUR 210 in its VAT retum. The customer
can deduct EUR 210 in its VAT return. Therefore, the supplier paid too much VAT and the customer deducted too
much. The Ministry of Finance has approved that the supplier does not have to send a credit invoice for the 2%, as
the overpaid VAT amount is usually deducted anyway. If the customer does not have the right to recover the input
VAT in full, he should ask the supplier for a credit invoice to reflect the amount of the discount.

5.3 Open Market Value

In the Netherlands, the option for Member States to use the ‘open market value’ as the taxable amount for
specific transactions is only implemented for the private use of company cars where the employees pay a low
consideration for that private use. As a result, VAT due on private use is a fixed amount of 2.7% of the car’s
catalogue value, including VAT and road taxes, unless the VAT due on the actual payment for the private use
is a higher amount.

54 Returnable packaging

With regard (o returnable packaging, the Dutch Ministry of Finance allow businesses to not include the
consideration received for the supply of returnable packaging in the taxable amount of the total supply. This
also applies to cases where the returnable packaging is, in the end, not returned.

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate

The standard rate in the Netherlands is 21%.

62 Reduced rate

The reduced rate in the Netherlands is 9% and applies for instance on:

+ the supply of food and beverages (except alcoholic beverages);

+ hotel accommodation;

+ (electronic) books, magazines and newspapers;

+ domestic passenger transport;

+ medication;

+ the letting out of holiday homes;

+ performances by performing artists;

+ the granting of admission to: circuses, amusement parks, theatrical performances, and sports events; and

+ certain labor intensive services, e.g. hairdressing services, bicycle repairs, and certain paintwork on
dwellings and cleaning of houses.

418 | The Netherlands

--- Page 59 ---
The Dutch Government is considering to bring hotel accommodation, books, magazines, newspapers and
certain cultural services under the standard rate per | January 2026. Prepayments and the supply of single-
purpose vouchers for these supplies that will take place from 1 January 2026 are subject to the 21% standard
VAT rate from 1 January 2025 (for hotel accommodation) or from 1 July 2025 (for the other supplies).

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rate

No super-reduced rate, The Netherlands has a zero rate for:

+ the deemed supply to a facilitating platform in the case of distance sales; and

+ the supply and the installation of solar panels if they are installed or to be installed on or in the direct
vicinity of residential buildings.

Wi Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right to deduction

The Netherlands has zero rates for the following supplies of goods and services related to international trade
and trade under special regimes (warehouses):

* exports and intra-Community supplies of goods:

+ goods supplied to travelers resident outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops;

+ supplies of sea vessels, aircraft, and related goods and services;

* supplies made to customers with (diplomatic) tax immunity;

* supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

+ supplies of (excise) goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

+ international passenger transport by seagoing vessels and airplanes;

+ provisioning of seagoing vessels, with the exception of vessels for inshore fishing with foodstuffs;

+ services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the EU;

* services of intermediaries relating to transactions carried out outside the EU; and

* services to goods under customs control, in a VAT warehouse, services in connection to goods exported or
imported.

7A. Intra-Community supplies

In order to make the applicability of the zero rate in case of intra-Community supplies plausible, the
following is required:

‘The supplier must have a combination of documents, such as a copy of the sales invoice, proof of payment
(from abroad), a purchase order, correspondence with the customer (if available), and most importantly,
transport documents such as a completely signed CMR. These documents, or a sufficient combination
thereof, must provide sufficient proof that the goods in question were transported to another EU Member
State. The Statement of Transport (Vervoersverklaring) can be used if a regular customer picks up the
goods. The invoice must include a reference that a supply is exempt (e.g. “VAT exempt intra-Community
supply, Article 138 of the EU VAT Directive 2006/112/EC” or in Dutch, “intracommunautaire levering” or
“intracommunautaire levering op grond van de Wet OB,t abel Il,onde rdeel a,pos ¢ 6”) and the VAT numbers
of both the seller and the customer.

‘The above rules can still be used in order to prove the transport besides the presumptions of transport as
mentioned in Article 45a Implementing Regulation.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to apply the zero rate in the case of export, the taxable person must have the required evidence in its
files that the goods have left the EU. For example:

* an export declaration with a confirmation of exit;
+ transport documents (bills of lading/CMR) signed by the customer on receipt of the goods;

The Netherlands | 419

--- Page 60 ---
+ import documents stamped by the non-EU customs authorities;

+ payment from outside the EU;

* invoices from providers of transport and customs procedures; and
+ correspondence with the customer.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

The Netherlands has introduced VAT warehouses for the goods listed in Annex V of the VAT Directive. There
are two types of VAT warehouses:

+ the location-bound VAT warehouse; and
+ the administrative (non-location bound) VAT warehouse.

Participants in transactions within the location bound warehouse do not necd to be established or registered in
the Netherlands. In case of an administrative (non-location bound) VAT warehouse, a fiscal representative is
required if a non-resident taxable person wants to buy or sell goods within this regime in the Netherlands.

The VAT warehouse regime can only be applied in case of the supply of certain unfinished products/bulk
goods (e.g. goods that need further processing in order to be sold for final consumption). These goods include,
€.g., cocoa beans, mineral products, certain chemical products, unrefined sugar, tapioca (see also Annex V of
the VAT Directive). For both types of VAT warehouses, the authorities published a separate list of goods that
qualify for each regime. If goods are not listed on either list, the VAT warehouse regime cannot be applied.

When goods are shipped from another EU Member State into the Netherlands to be placed in either VAT
warehouse regime, the customer must first account for the intra-Community acquisition in the Netherlands.
There is no relief from this obligation and will therefore trigger the obligation to register and report the
acquisition for VAT purposes. The same applies for the import alien of goods to be placed under the VAT
warehouse regime. The import itself triggers VAT obligations (payment) for the importer.

7.1.3.2. Administrative (non-location bound) VAT warehouse

The supply of goods which are brought to or are located in the warehouse is subject to the 0% rate. The goods
may be held anywhere in the Netherlands by a VAT warehouse licensee. Basically, this regime can only

apply for sales between administrative VAT warehouse license owners. Every taxable person can apply for

a license. To obtain a license, an application must be made to the tax authorities, If the supplier or customer
of the goods is a non-resident, a fiscal representative must be appointed. There are certain administrative
requirements that must be met.

7.1.3.3 Location-bound VAT warehouse

The supply of goods which are located in the warehouse is zero rated. The warehouse keeper should apply

for the license. The taxable persons trading in goods which are located in the warehouse do not have to be
registered for VAT purposes in the Netherlands. In fact, there are no obligations towards the Dutch authorities.
Anon-resident customer releasing goods from the warehouse must appoint a fiscal representative.

VAT becomes due when the goods are taken out of the warehouse. The supply of goods before the goods
leave the warehouse is then taxed at the VAT rate applicable to the goods. This VAT is collected from

the supplier who releases the goods from the warehouse via the reverse charge mechanism. The fiscal
representative of the foreign supplier must report this reverse charge in the VAT return. The sale by the
foreign supplier to the customer outside the warehouse, follows the normal rules (i.e. either a domestic supply
or intra~Community supply or export). There are certain administrative requirements that will have to be met.

7.13.4 Excise warehouse

Excise goods under suspension of excise duties can be stored in an excise warehouse. The supply of excise
goods which are brought to or are located in the warehouse is subject to the 0% rate. In case of the excise
warehouse, a fiscal representative is required if a non-resident taxable person wants to buy or sell goods
within this regime in the Netherlands, There are certain administrative requirements that will have to be met.

420 | The Netherlands

--- Page 61 ---
‘The supply of mineral oils which are brought outside the excise warehouse and which are not brought to
another excise warehouse, may also be subject to the 0% rate if certain conditions are met

7.1.3.5 Bonded (customs) warehouse
It is possible to store goods coming from outside the EU in a bonded warehouse before import occurs.

If goods coming from a third country are directly placed under a bonded warehouse regime, no customs
duties and VAT become due. When the goods are released from the warehouse for free circulation, duties
and VAT become due, The importer who declares the goods into free circulation becomes responsible for the
payment of the VAT.

The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods are
subsequently placed under another customs regime.

The sale of goods while still in bond is subject to the zero rate of Dutch VAT (Table II(a)(2) of the Dutch
VAT Law). Evidence that the sales took place in the bonded warehouse should be kept in files (Le. list of
the transactions from the warehouse keeper, customs documents, the agreements, the invoices, etc.). Certain
services related to bonded goods are also subject to the zero rate of Dutch VAT.

72 Exemptions without right to deduction

721 Exemptions in the public interest

Certain hospital services, medical and paramedical care, cultural activities, ambulance services, services
rendered by undertakers, educational services, services of a cultural or social nature, some services connected
with sports, etc, are examples of exemptions in the public interest. Some exemptions, however, are granted
on the condition that the supplier is a non-profit institution and/or on the condition that no distortion of
competition will occur as a result of the exemption.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

The other exemptions are financial and banking services, insurance services, management of collective
investment funds, certain games of chance, postal and telecommunication services, non-commercial activities
of public radio and television broadcasting organizations, letting of immovable property, the supply of (old)
immovable property, services by employers’ or employees’ organizations, or by political, religious, charitable
organizations, etc.

73 Option for taxation

‘The supply of newly built real estate which has not been used or has been used for less than 2 years, is taxable
with VAT. VAT is also due on the sale of a building site. For other supplies and the letting of immovable
property, parties may opt to be taxed for VAT purposes. The option for taxable supply and lease is only
possible if the purchaser or the lessee is entitled to deduct the VAT on the purchase or rent for at least 90%
(for certain taxable persons 8% applies).

It’s not possible to opt for taxation for financial services.

8. Liability

81 Reverse charges

In the Netherlands there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general
B2B services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Dutch VAT ID number
established in the Netherlands, This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles
44 and 196 EU VAT Directive. There is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity
supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in the Netherlands (Article 195 EU VAT Directive).

‘The Netherlands | 421

--- Page 62 ---
8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
In the Netherlands, the reverse charge applies to all domestic supplies of goods and services by non-
established taxable persons if the customer is a taxable person resident or established in the Netherlands.
The reverse charge also applies if the customer is a legal entity although not a taxable person. The reverse
charge does not apply in case the customer is a non-resident company (without a fixed establishment in

the Netherlands). The reverse charge also applies if'a non-established supplier is registered for VAT in the
Netherlands (i.e. has a Dutch VAT ID number).

The application of this reverse charge is obligatory. It applies to all supplies of goods and services which are
subject to VAT in the Netherlands.

On the invoice, reference should be made to the application of the reverse charge. A foreign supplier could
use the following text on the invoice: “biw verlegd, VAT due by the recipient under Article 12, paragraph 3 of
the Dutch VAT Act”.

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)
A domestic reverse charge rule applies in the case of the supply of:

+ construction work in relation to immovable property and shipbuilding, including repair, cleaning,
maintenance, alteration and demolition services by a subcontractor for a contractor, and from the
contractor to a “self-builder”:

+ staff engaged in the construction sector;

+ immovable property (when seller and buyer opted to tax the supply);

+ old used materials, scrap, waste, and specific services;

* goods provided as security by one taxable person to another in execution of that security;

+ immovable property sold by a judgment debtor in a compulsory sale procedure;

+ investment gold;

+ CO2 Emission Rights;

+ telecommunication services performed to other providers of telecommunication services;

+ mobile phones, computer chips, game consoles, laptops, and tablets (if the value of sale exceeds EUR
10,000); and

+ the supply proceeding the release of the goods from a VAT warehouse regime (see 1.3) .

The supplier must refer to the reverse charge. The following text could be mentioned on the invoice: “bav
verlegd”. This reverse charge must be applied even if the recipient is not established in the Netherlands.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under the Dutch VAT rules, managing directors of businesses can be held jointly and severally liable for
the unpaid taxes of the businesses they manage, unless they submit timely information about the business’
inability to pay these taxes as a result of its bad financial situation.

Joint and several liability also exists for businesses that form part of a VAT group that is confirmed in writing
by the Tax Authorities. This liability exists for the VAT debts of each member of the VAT group for the entire
period that the relevant entity was part of that VAT group. This is one of the reasons why it is relevant to
inform the Dutch Tax Authorities in writing when a business no longer forms part of a Dutch VAT group. See
also paragraph 1.3.

Joint and several liabilty also exists in situations where, for example, there is a supply of staff, the lease of
means of transportation to private individuals by non-EU established businesses and carousel fraud.

9 Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

+ The input VAT is not recoverable in case of restaurant and catering supplies;

* Under a standstill provision (‘BUA’), the Netherlands disallows VAT deduction on costs for
remunerations in kind, fringe benefits for staff (provision of food and drink, accommodation, sporting and
recreational facilities, transport for personal use, etc.), and business gifis and gifts to others who cannot
deduct more than 30% of the input tax, where the total value per beneficiary exceeds BUR 227 per year.

9.2 Excess input VAT
Excess input VAT is immediately recoverable and refunded by the tax authorities.

Acclaim has to be made within 5 years, See also paragraph 10.2.6 “Tax assessment period”.

9.3 Capital goods — adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 9 years following the year of first use
+ movable property - 4 years following the year of first use

From 1 January 2026, an adjustment period for services related to immovable property will be introduced,
if the value of the services exceeds EUR 30,000 and the business purchasing the services would normally
activate and depreciate the costs for corporate tax or income tax purposes. Also, the Supreme Court (Hoge
Raad) decided that certain services relating to immovable goods may qualify as a capital good to which the
adjustment period applies.

9.4 VAT refund for foreigners

941 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the
country in which the claimant is established.

Annual minimum limit (for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year) is
EUR 50, Interim minimum limit (for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months)
is EUR 400.

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year. The refund application must be
filed in the Dutch, English, or German language.

Deadline for making the claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made. It
is possible to submit a claim within five calendar years from the end of the calendar year in which the VAT
was incurred, but if the claim is filed after the original deadline of 30 September then no appeal can be filed.

Supporting documents (c.g. the originals/copies of invoices) are generally not required. However, in certain
cases the tax authorities may require additional information.

‘The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within four months of receipt of the claim. The
period in which the authorities must make a decision, will be extended to six months or eight months when
the authorities request additional information.

Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account number
provided to the tax authorities. Time limit for appeals is 6 weeks following the decision. If this appeal is
unsuccessful, the claimant can initiate proceedings before the national court (within 6 weeks after a receipt of
the decision on appeal).

10. | Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content
A full VAT invoice must contain the following information:

» date at which the invoice is issued;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ VAT identification number under which the supply or service is performed:

+ full name and address of the supplier or service provider;

* full name and address of the customer/recipient;

+ VAT identification number of the customer when making zero rated intra-Community supplies of goods,
or the customer is liable for payment of VAT;

+ the date of supply of goods or the period of supply of services as well as the date(s) on which any advance
payment falls due if different from the date of invoice;

+ the quantity and a clear description of the goods or services supplied;

* the taxable amount;

* the applied VAT rate;

* the VAT amount in Euro;

+ in case of self-billing the following reference is required, “factuur uitgereikt door afnemer™;

* _ the reference to the exemption if a supply is VAT exempt:

+ the reference to the intra-Community supply if such a transaction takes place:

+ reference to the reverse charge (e.g. “biw verlegd”) if VAT is imposed on the customer;

+ in case of a supply of the new means of transport, the information necessary to determine whether a
means of transport is ne

* the reference to the special scheme if any special arrangement is applied, e.g., the reference “bijzondere
regeling — reisbureaus” in case of the travel agents’ margin scheme; the reference “bijzondere regeling
-ge bruikte goederen” in case the margin scheme for second-hand goods is applied; the reference
“bijzondere regeling -k unstvoorwerpen” in case the margin scheme for works of art is applied; the
reference “bijzondere regeling -v oorwerpen voor verzameling of antiquiteiten” in case the margin
scheme for collector’s items and antiques is applied; and

+ name, address, and VAT identification number of the fiscal representative (if applicable)

‘The Netherlands | 425

--- Page 66 ---
10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice needs to be issued before the 16" day of the month following the month in which the supply or
service was rendered. For continuous supplies of services at least once per year an invoice must be issued.
The VAT is due in the month the invoice is issued. If the invoice is issued too late or not at all, the VAT is
due in the month the invoice should have been issued. This does not apply for services to which the reverse
charge of Article 196 applies.

10.1.3 Currency
Itis allowed to issue invoices in a foreign currency. The VAT amount must be in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
When computing the amount of VAT into Euros, the amount in foreign currency must be converted into Euros

using the last official conversion rate of the Dutch National Bank or European Central Bank on the day the
VAT became due.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Dutch VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed in case there is an agreement between the two parties (for
instance a contract or written agreement).

10.1.6 Simplified invoice
A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ identity of the supplier of the goods and/or services;

+ type of goods or services supplied;

+ the VAT amount payable in Euro or the information needed to calculate it; and

+ specific, unambiguous reference to the initial invoice and the details that are being amended (on a credit
note, debit note or other document treated as an invoice).

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the amount of the invoice is not higher than EUR 100 or the
invoice issued is a document or message which changes the initial invoice and a specific and unambiguous
reference to the initial invoice is made with a specific mention of the details that are amended.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices and other documents need to be stored for Ye ars. Invoices relating to immovable property and
electronic services must be stored for a period of 10 years.

Invoices can be stored in any form. The authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability must be
guaranteed during the storage period. When taxable persons store invoices by electronic means, the tax
authorities must have the right to access those invoices, and to download and use them by electronic means, if
the tax authorities need them for auditing purposes.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

‘The taxable person can choose any form for the electronic invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin, integrity
of content and readability are guaranteed. A PDF is possible. A customer is allowed to refuse an electronic invoice.

All public organisations (such as Ministries, municipalities, provinces and non-departmental public bodies)
are obliged to be able to receive and process elnvoicing. elnvoices can be submitted to the government in the
following ways:

+ via the secure Peppol network;

+ via the Central Government elnvoicing Portal; or

+ via Digipoort which is the ICT center where message traffic for the government is handled.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period
The standard VAT return is filed quarterly. A domestic taxable person may have to file a monthly VAT return,
426 | The Netherlands

--- Page 67 ---
if the quarterly VAT returns are frequently filed late, or at the request of the taxable person, A foreign taxable
person may also apply for monthly filing, An annual return can only be submitted if:

+ the annual amount of VAT due is Jess than EUR 1,883;

+ the intra-Community supplies and acquisitions do not exceed the yearly threshold of EUR 10,000;
+ the taxable person is not a legal person; and

+ the taxable person does not have an import VAT deferment license.

VAT returns must be submitted electronically.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment
The VAT due has to be received on the bank account of the tax authorities by the last day of the month

following the reporting period (i.e. the VAT due for the second quarter of 2025 must be received by the tax
authorities by 31 July 2025), The same deadline applies for the VAT return.

Foreign taxable persons must pay the VAT by the last day of the second month following the reporting period
(i.e. the VAT for the second quarter of 2025 must be received by the tax authorities by 31 August 2025). The
same applies for the VAT return.

Further information can be downloaded at the following link: https://tinyurl.com/4d42y2k6.

10.2.3 Additional VAT returns (‘suppleties’)

Within 8 week from the moment as a taxable person establishes that he underpaid or overpaid VAT in the last
5 calendar years, he has to file an additional VAT return to adjust this mistake. Not filing an additional VAT
return, or not filing it in time, can result in penalties. The VAT amount due may be increased with interest.

If the amount that needs to be adjusted for a specific taxable period does not exceed EUR 1,000, the
adjustment can be included in the VAT return for the current taxable period. Also see section 10.3.4.

10.2.4 European sales listing

A European sales listing must be submitted on a monthly basis if the intra-Community supply of goods
exceeds EUR 50,000 in the current quarter or has exceeded the threshold in any of the four preceding
quarters, The European sales listing for services can be submitted on a quarterly basis, regardless of the
period for the intra-Community supply of goods.

European Sales Listings must be submitted electronically.

Further information can be downloaded at the following link: http:/bit.ly/IRpaxcW
hitps:/inyurl.com/ydj23f4j.

10.2.5 National recapitulative statement

There are no national recapitulative statements in the Netherlands.

10.2.6 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period or statute of limitations in the Netherlands is 5 years excluding the current year.
This means that in the year 2025, the last open year for assessments (payable or reclaimable VAT) is the year
2020.

10.3 Penalties

The Dutch tax authorities are able to impose two types of penalties: default and offense penalties. Offense
penalties can only be levied in case of gross negligence or intentional act. Once a choice is made for either a
default or offense penalty, the authorities cannot inapose the other type of penalty as well for the same fact.
Next to tax penalties, in case of e.g, fraud the authorities can decide if they proceed instead with criminal
offense charges and proceedings. In case the authorities impose a penalty the tax payer can appeal against the
decision to impose a penalty.

‘The Netherlands | 427

--- Page 68 ---
10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

When a company becomes liable for VAT in the Netherlands, it needs to register for VAT immediately and
inform the authorities. When the company does not fulfill this obligation and as a result the authorities are
not able to collect VAT, the authorities are entitled to assess a penalty for late registration. They can choose
between a default and an offense penalty. The tax authorities can issue a default penalty of not more than
EUR 6,99. When the tax payer can prove that the non-compliance is not his fault, no penalty is applicable.
Usually in case of (retroactive) voluntary correction, the default penalty is mitigated to a smaller amount or
sometimes even to nil. If the authorities want to assess an offense penalty, they must prove that the non / late
registration constitutes gross negligence (see for the offense penalty paragraph 10.3.5).

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT return is not filed on time, the penalty is usually EUR 68 per return not filed or filed late. In
exceptional cases this can be increased to EUR 165. A return is considered late if the return is received by the
authorities more than “Ha ys after the legal deadline of that return.

Penalties for the European sales listing depend on the type and frequency of offenses. A distinction is made
between not filing in time, not reporting all or incorrect customer VAT numbers, and not reporting correct
amounts of intra-Community supplies. If the European sales listing is not filed on time or correctly, the tax
authorities can issue a penalty of EUR 167 for a first offense (2.5% of EUR 6,709). For a second or third
offense, it is EUR 335 (5%). For a following offense, it is EUR 1,67 25%). In exceptional cases, the penalty
can be increased to EUR 6,09.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

VAT due on the VAT return is payable within the deadline (see paragraph 10.2.2). If the money is received
by the authorities more than Ha ys after the deadline, then a penalty applies. If the VAT is not paid on time
or not paid at all, the tax authorities can issue a penalty of 3% of the VAT due or the amount not received,
starting at a minimum of EUR 50 and a maximum of EUR 6,09. I f the VAT due is paid within Ha ys after
the date it should have been paid and the VAT of the previous VAT return was paid on time, in principal no
penalty will be issued.

10.3.4 Default penaities on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
If the VAT return mentions the wrong amount of VAT due, an incorrect VAT return is filed. In case of

underpayment of VAT the authorities will issuc a VAT assessment to collect the underpaid VAT and a default
penalty for filing a wrong VAT return.

The taxpayer is obliged to inform the tax authorities as soon as he discovers he made an error in a previous
return (within the statute of limitation of 5 years), and at least within 8 weeks after establishing the error. For
errors in excess of EUR 1,000 a separate additional VAT return has to be filed. Failure to notify the authorities
of underpayment of VAT can be punished with an offense penalty of 100%.

If the VAT is paid on the basis ofa voluntarily disclosure, only a default penalty is applied of 5% with a
maximum of EUR 6,09 i n the following cases:

+ the voluntarily disclosure leads to an amount of VAT payable of more than EUR 20,000; and
+ the amount of VAT payable is more than 10% of the amount previously reported for the respective VAT
period,

A voluntarily disclosure should relate to a calendar year at the most. If the voluntary disclosure relates to
more years, the tax inspector can apply penalties per year. In some cases, the authorities can decide not to
apply penalties.

10.3.5 Offense penalties

In case of gross negligence, intentional act or fraud, a minimum penalty of 25% can be levied. In case
of intentional act, 50% can be levied. In case of fraud the penalty is increased to 100%. The Dutch tax
authorities may also decide to prosecute for a criminal offence

Besides penalties, the Dutch tax authorities levy interest which is currently 6.5%.
428 | ‘The Netherlands

--- Page 69 ---
10.4 Totrastat

In the Netherlands, a separate monthly Intrastat return needs to be submitted to the Statistics Netherlands
(CBS) by the 10th working day of the month following the reporting month. Some companies who exceed
the threshold and have to report acquisitions of goods in the Netherlands are relieved to file monthly returns
as the CBS obtains its information via other sources (VAT authorities). These companies need to file a yearly
return in November,

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

There is no threshold for filing Intrastat returns in the Netherlands. Intrastat returns only have to be filed by a
business if it is asked to do so by Statistics Netherlands (CBS).

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
The information which needs to be reported for both the arrivals and the dispatches is the following:

+ VAT number supplier;

+ ifused, VAT number of third party declarant;

+ reference period (i.e. the reporting month);

+ EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ country of origin

+ nature of the transaction (e.g. purchase, sale, return of goods, repair etc. );
+ means of transport, for instance “by road” or “by sea”;

+ HS Code (8-digit harmonized commodity code according to Combined Nomenclature (CN));
+ statistical value;

+ partner ID: VAT ID number of the customer;

+ quantity of the goods (i.e. net weight in kilograms);

+ other measure for certain goods (liters, m? or number of pieces); and

+ invoice value of the goods.

When Intrastat reports are not filed, reminders are sent by the authorities. If these reminders are ignored the
penalty can go up to EUR 16,000. In special situations a penalty can be imposed up to EUR 500,000.

Itis also obligatory to report distance sales if Intrastat reports must be filed.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There are no additional requirements.
