Norway

Norway is not a Member State of the European Union (EU) and thus, EU VAT Law such as Directives and
Regulations do not apply. Consequently, even though the Norwegian VAT legislation is based on the same
basic principles as the VAT system in the EU Member States, Norwegian VAT rules and interpretation of the
rules differ in many areas from EU VAT Law and its interpretation by the Court of Justice of the European
Union

A VAT system was introduced in Norway in 1970. The legislation was little changed until 2001, when a

VAT Reform broadened the taxable transactions and introduced general liabilities to charge VAT on services.
Over the years, the VAT legislation had become out of date, and in 2009 a new VAT Act was passed by the
Norwegian Parliament, Stortinget, and the new VAT Act became effective on I January 2010. VAT is referred
to in Norway as “merverdiavgift” or “MVA".

The main act relating to VAT in Norway is the Value Added Tax (VAT) Act of 19 June 2009 No 58 (in
Norwegian, Merverdiavgiftsloven).

Simultaneously, important rules were enacted and compiled in the VAT Regulation of 15 December 2009

No 1540 (Merverdiavgifisforskrifien), which provided for more detailed regulations on specific fields of
legislation. The regulation uses the same numbering system as the VAT Act, and usually it is possible to find
relevant rules in the regulation by comparing it with the same rules in the VAT Act. For example, § 2-1 of the
VAT Act relates to the rules on the obligation to register for VAT. Sub-chapters 2-1 and § 2-1-1 of the VAT
Regulation provide more detailed rules concerning the registration.

Important rules applicable for VAT are also prescribed in the Bookkeeping Act of 19 November 2004 No

73 (Bokfaringsloven), the Bookkeeping Regulation of ! December 2004 No 1558 (Bokforingsforskriften),
the Tax Collection Act of 17 June 2005 No 67 (Skattebetalingsloven) and the Tax Collection Regulation of

21 December 2007 No 1776 (Skattebetalingsforskriften). When importing goods to Norway from abroad,
important rules can be found in the Customs Act of 21 December 2007 No 119 (Tolloven) and the Customs
Regulation of 17 December 2008 No 1502 (Tollforskriften), On 1 January 2017 the Tax Administration Act of
27 May 2016 No 14 entered into force. This act governs rules concerning the tax administration, VAT Audits,
penalties, complaints ete.

Norway has specific and separate legislation outside the VAT Act that provides compensation to public
bodies, municipalities, etc. and to certain private businesses for VAT paid to its contractors. Such legislation is
set out in the VAT Compensation Act of 12 December 2003 No 108 (Lov om kompensasjon av merverdiavgifi
for kommuner, fylkeskommuner mv) and the VAT Compensation Regulation 12 December 2003 No 128
(Forskrift om kompensasjon av merverdiavgift til kormuner, fylkeskommuner mv).

The purpose of this legislation is to compensate these institutions for VAT paid to its contractors and to
provide neutrality for these institutions between buying goods and services from VAT-registered businesses
and providing (producing) such goods and services themselves.

1. Taxable persons

Ld Registration threshold & SME scheme for domestic taxable persons

Persons engaged in trade or business whose taxable supplies and withdrawals (private use) exceed NOK
50,000 (approximately EUR 5,000) over a period of twelve months, are liable to register for VAT and charge
VAT on their sales and pay the VAT to the state. For charitable or non-profit institutions and organisations,
the registration threshold is NOK 140,000 (approximately EUR 14,000). Sales by commission are considered
to be supplies both by the principal and the commission agent. In the case of sales by auction, VAT shall be
calculated and paid by the auctioneer.

Norway | 431

--- Page 72 ---
In addition, there are special rules for the estate of a deceased person, estates in bankruptcy and cooperative
businesses. Multiple enterprises that are operated by one owner must be registered as one taxable person.
‘The tax authorities may decide to grant a part of a taxable person to be a separate taxable person for VAT
purposes, provided that these activities arc physically and formally separated from the rest of the business.

1.2 Registration threshold & SME scheme for foreign taxable persons

Foreign taxable persons with a fixed establishment in Norway are subject to the same obligations as domestic
taxable persons. Ifa taxable person has taxable supplies above the registration threshold (i.e, NOK 50,000
(EUR 5,000), see paragraph L.1), he is liable to register for VAT in Norway. The VAT registration in Norway
is a two-step process. First an organization number must be obtained from the Central Coordination Register
for Legal Entities (Enhetsregisteret) thereafter a VAT registration must be completed with the VAT register.
‘The VAT number is identical to the organization number and must be followed by the letters MVA.

Special forms are required to register for VAT purposes. The different registration forins may be downloaded
from the website of the Central Coordination Register from the Bronnoysund Central Registration Office
(www.brreg.no). Confirmation of VAT registration usually takes two to four weeks.

Taxable persons without a fixed establishment in Norway are also liable to register for VAT if their sales
exceed the registration threshold (see also paragraph 1.1). In these circumstances, the foreign taxable
person is required to appoint a fiscal representative or if certain conditions are met register directly. See also
Paragraph 1.4 regarding fiscal representation

Foreign taxable persons trading goods with private individuals in Norway (B2C) over the internet, may also
be liable to register and charge VAT. See Paragraph 3.3 regarding VAT on Electronic Commerce for more
information.

Generally, a taxable person must document that he has exceeded the registration threshold when applying for
a VAT registration. This is a very unpractical solution.

13 VAT grouping

‘Two or more cooperating companies may be registered as one taxable person if at least 85% of the capital
in each company is owned by one or more of the cooperating companies. VAT grouping is optional. All
companies that participate in a VAT group shall be jointly and severally liable for the payment of the VAT.
The group registration is available for each company subject to the Norwegian VAT system (i.e. registered

for Norwegian VAT) regardless of the nationality of the owner(s), even if the company is not established in
Norway. Companies that are separate legal entities and want a group registration must apply to the Norwegian
VAT Authorities. If approved, the group registration becomes effective as of the VAT period of the date

the application is sent. When entities are registered as a VAT group, there will be no VAT chargeable on
transactions between these companies. Pure holding companies and branches of foreign companies may be
accepted to take part ina VAT group.

The companies in the group will only have one VAT identification number but may have separate business
registration identification numbers, The group of companies will file a single VAT return for the group as a whole.

1.4 Fiscal representative

A business with taxable supplies in Norway that does not have its registered place of business or residence
in Norway must register through a VAT representative. The representative must have his registered place of
business or residence in Norway. Both the foreign business and the representative will be jointly and severally
liable for the calculation and payment of the VAT,

As of | January 2017, the rules regarding VAT representation were modified for foreign businesses

established in countries that Norway has a Tax Cooperation and Enforcement agreement with. Today this
includes most EU Member States and the UK. The agreement with UK has been upheld also after Brexit,

432 | Norway

--- Page 73 ---
Pursuant to this set of rules, foreign businesses established in any of the above-mentioned countries may

opt for a direct registration from I April 2017 or appoint a local VAT Representative. From 2013 the VAT
representative is no longer jointly and severally liable with the foreign business for the correct payment

of the VAT for businesses established in these countries. The background for the new rules is that Norway
has concluded Mutual Assistance Agreements with these countries with respect to the enforcement of the
collection of taxes and that the EFTA Surveillance Authority (ESA) ordered Norway to liberalize its rules on
VAT representation.

If the foreign business opts for direct registration, the business must maintain separate accounts for the
Norwegian business activities and accounting material must be stored in Norway. It is, however, possible to
be granted exemptions from these rules.

For businesses established in other countries, a VAT Representative is still required and the VAT
representative is jointly and severally liable for the correct payment of the VAT. The VAT representative will
also be jointly responsible for compliance with the bookkeeping rules and the filing of the VAT return.

1.5 Simplified registration procedure for providers of electronic services and providers of
electronic communication services

Foreign providers of electronic services, including electronic communication services covered by § 3-30
Subsections (4) and (5) of the VAT Act, must register for VAT when the registration threshold of NOK 50,000
(approximately EUR 5,000) is exceeded. When such electronic services are supplied by a foreign supplier to
non-taxable persons (private individuals) who reside in Norway, the foreign supplier is obliged to register and
charge and pay VAT on the supply. The same applies to other services when they are supplied or mediated by
a provider of the electronic services by means of electronic communication and the consideration is collected
by such provider. In the case of electronic communication services that are performed via a fixed terminal
located in the VAT Area (the mainland of Norway and Norwegian Territorial water, excluding Spitzbergen,
Jan Mayen and the Norwegian dependencies), VAT shall be payable even if the customer of the service is not
resident in the VAT area.

These suppliers can use a simplified registration platform available from the website of the Norwegian VAT
Authorities, www. voesnorway.com. It is also possible to submit quarterly VAT returns through this website.

1.6 Voluntary VAT registration and pre-registration

Taxable persons and public entities that let out buildings or hire out plants may voluntarily register for VAT
if the building or plant is used by a taxable person involved in a VAT-registered business. Also, certain other
enterprises involved in letting out real estate may opt for a voluntary registration for VAT.

A taxable person who has not reached the registration threshold of NOK 50,000 (approximately EUR 5,000)
can apply for an advanced registration or pre-registration if he has substantial purchases that are directly
related to subsequent taxable supplies or his taxable supplies will exceed the registration threshold within 3
weeks of the date when such supplies will commence.

17 Second-hand goods,w orks of art,c ollectors’ items and antiques

For the sale of second-hand goods, works of art, collectors’ items and antiques, a special scheme is applicable.
According to this scheme, the taxable amount of the supplies of second-hand goods, etc. shall be the profit
margin made by the supplier, less the amount of VAT relating to the profit margin.

‘The taxable dealer may, according to these rules, not show separately on the invoice the VAT relating to the
supply of goods that he makes under the special margin scheme. The same applies on any other documents
serving as an invoice. The customer will, in these situations, not be able to deduct the VAT. Thus, the
regulation allows the supplier alternatively to apply the normal VAT rules to any supplies covered by these
special arrangements.

Where the supplier uses both normal and special arrangements, the taxable dealer must specify in his accounts
the particular transactions falling into each of these arrangements.
Norway | 433

--- Page 74 ---
2. Taxable transactions

21 ‘Transfer of business

The transfer of stock and other assets that are linked to the transfer of a business or part of a business to anew
proprietor is an exempt transaction with a right of deduction (zero-rated).

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Norway is not an EU Member State, thus EU legislation rules regarding supplies free of charge does not
apply. Supplies supplied free of charge may be subject to the ordinary VAT rate and payable by the supplier,
based on the general rules at the full market value of the goods and services. See also paragraphs 5.3 and 9.1)

Similarly, as a general rule, there is no right of deduction of input tax on goods and services supplied free of
charge for private use, as gifts and marketing purpos

Goods supplied as a gift or for marketing purposes for free, with a value of less than NOK 100 (approx.
EUR 9) may be supplied without charging VAT while the right of deduction is maintained (see also 9.1).

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

Norway is not an EU-Member State and thus no rules apply similar to those of the EU member states,
exempting such transactions,

In Norway, such situations will be governed by the ordinary import and export rules according to the customs
regulations. The transaction, provided the goods are not imported, could also be considered to take place
outside Norway, for example, if the transaction takes place in a customs warehouse or in a free trade zone,

For the B2C sale and the importation of low value goods through the internet, please see paragraph 3.3 -
Distance sales threshold - VOEC (VAT on Electronic Commerce)
2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

A deferred payment of VAT on imported goods by VAT registered businesses (taxable persons) applies in
Norway. VAT is therefore not payable to the customs authorities at the time of importation by taxable persons.
The fact that the import VAT is reported through the VAT return has a positive cash flow effect, as the import
VAT is now reported and deducted in the same VAT return.

Other importers (non-taxable persons, public bodies or private individuals) will have to pay the VAT to the
customs authorities at the time of importation of the goods.
2.4 Newrules for customs clearance of imported goods from 1 April 2025

From | April 2025, importers of goods, transport companies and freight forwarders must report the entry

of the transport vehicle, train, ship or airplane electronically to the customs authorities via a new electronic
portal “Digitoll”. It is recommended that businesses being affected by the new rules should seck professional
advice in due time before the new rules enter into force.

3. Place of supply
3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

There are no simplification rules that apply to consignment or call-off stock in Norway. Normal Norwegian
rules apply. The supply will be regarded as a domestic supply of goods by the foreign taxable person. The

434 | Norway

--- Page 75 ---
foreign taxable person is required to register for VAT in Norway. Under call-off and consignment stock rules,
both the consignee and the consignor may be held jointly liable for paying the VAT on the supply.

Sales taking place within a customs warehouse or customs-free zone may be considered exempt from VAT.
When goods are taken out of the warehouse, the goods must be declared for customs by the importer.

3.2 Installation supplies

‘There are no VAT simplification measures in place for installation supplies. Providers of a supply of goods
with installation or assembly are, in principle, liable to register for VAT in Norway and charge and pay
Norwegian VAT. Although some simplifications may be possible under the customs legislation.

33 Distance sales threshold - VOEC (VAT on Electronic Commerce)

EU rules on distance sales do not apply to Norway because Norway is outside the EU. Goods must normally
be declared for customs and VAT must be paid by the importer upon eniry of the goods into Norway.

However, from | April 2021, Norway introduced a liability to register and pay VAT for foreign taxable
persons selling goods to private persons on the internet. The obligation applies when the supplier supplies
goods to customers in Norway above the general registration threshold of NOK 50,000 (approximately EUR
5,000). Suppliers with sales below the threshold, can opt to register to benefit from the simplified import
formalities procedures.

The above-mentioned suppliers involved in internet sales, can either register in the ordinary VAT Register, or
opt to register under the simplified procedures in the electronic portal VOEC (VAT on Electronic Commerce).
The VOEC rules apply to the sale of goods where each item sold has a value of less than NOK 3,000
{approximately EUR 300), VAT, freight and insurance etc not included. The exemption also includes duty free
import for customs duties. Among products that are covered by the exemption are clothes, textiles, footwear
etc, which normally would be subject to high customs tariffs. The exemption does not include foodstuff,
tobacco, alcoholic or non-alcoholic beverages, and other goods subject to excise duties or restrictions (drugs,
pharmaceuticals, weapons etc). By using the VOEC-rules, the seller collects the VAT from the customer and
pays the VAT to the authorities via the portal quarterly.

‘Traders registered under VOEC benefit from simplified customs procedures upon import.

The VOEC does not apply to products with a value above NOK 3,000 (approximately EUR 300). In these
cases, the normal customs procedures must be followed and VAT, excise duties and customs duties have to be
paid by the importer. The VOEC simplifications do not apply to business to business (B2B) transactions. If
the seller has both sales under VOEC and outside VOEC (B2B) it is assumed that he cannot use VOEC.

More information on the VOEC is available from the Norwegian Tax Authorities WEB-portal www.
skatteetaten.no.

When VOEC was introduced, the threshold for the duty-free importation of low value goods (goods with a
value of less than NOK 350 per consignment) was abolished. As of the introduction, VAT and duties must be
paid from the first NOK.

‘The simplification rules for gifts from private individuals abroad to private individuals resident in Norway
with a value of less than NOK 1,000 (approximately EUR 100) per consignment, is still valid.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

EU simplified triangulation rules do not apply because Norway is not an EU Member State. No other

simplification rules apply to chain supplies in Norway.

35 Acquisition threshold

No threshold with regard to intra-Community acquisitions applies because Norway is outside the EU.

Norway | 435

--- Page 76 ---
3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Norway does not apply the effective use and enjoyment rules pursuant to EU VAT Directives. It is important
to note that Norway does not have clear place of supply or taxation rules similar to the EU. There are however
many elements in Norway’s VAT provisions reflecting an effective use and enjoyment principle.

In this respect, the Norwegian VAT rules may differ in many areas from the rules that apply within the
countries of the EU, in particular where EU VAT Directives shift the obligation to pay and report VAT to the
customer of the service, described as ‘reverse charge’. Due to the differences between the Norwegian and the
EU VAT systems, situations with double taxation or non-taxation of certain services may occur.

4, Chargeable event and chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles
VAT is due when the taxable event takes place. This will usually be when the goods or services are delivered.
According to good accounting standards an invoice shall be issued within | month of the supply.

Supplies of goods and services that are invoiced on a monthly basis can be invoiced within the first 15 days of
the month after the month of supply.

4.2 Advanced payments — VAT

For the supply of taxable goods and services the main rule is that it is prohibited to make an invoice with VAT
before the supply has taken place. There are a few exemptions from the main rule.

For supplies that are not subject to VAT there is no limitation for issuing an invoice before the supply takes place.

43 Continuous supplies

Ongoing supplies of services (and related goods) shall be invoiced within 1 month of the expiration of the
said bi-monthly reporting period. If invoicing is subject to down payments in a tender or similar arrangement,
the invoicing and stipulation of the taxable period, could be according to the down payment plan,

Certain services (electricity, telecom- and electronic services, and similar) based on actual consumption, may
be invoiced for a longer period, limited to one year.
44 Cash accounting

Norway does not have specific rules for cash accounting.
5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

Bad debts can be corrected if an outstanding claim is, finally, deemed to be a loss due to the debtor’s
insolvency. In practice, the right to reduce the output tax on the lost claim can be made when the taxable
person has taken reasonable measures to collect the debt.

A bad debt relief claim is made in the bi-monthly VAT return in the period when the loss is claimed, No
specific evidence is required other than some type of proof that the taxable person has tried to collect the debt.
5.2 Cash discounts

Pursuant to Norwegian VAT and bookkeeping rules, VAT on cash discounts can be deducted/corrected in the
accounts of the customer and seller to the extent the cash discount becomes effective. It is not required to
issue a credit note.

436 | Norway

--- Page 77 ---
5.3 Open Market Value

Norway does not have rules that are similar to the EU’s Open Market Value rules (Art. 80 of the EU VAT
Directive) though the taxable amount may be corrected in the case of gifts and close relationships

54  Returnable packing

Norway does not have specific VAT rules for retumable packaging.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

61 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate is 25%.

6.2 Reduced rate

A reduced rate of 15% applies to foodstuffs. The reduced rate does not apply to medicine, tobacco products,
alcoholic beverages or water from waterworks. Nor does the reduced rate apply to foodstuffs that are part of
catering services, or supplied in restaurants, etc.

From I May 2025 the reduced rate of 15% would also apply to water from waterworks and sewage fee. (the
change was decided by the Parliament in December 2024 and with a planned date of entry of 1 May 2025).

A reduced rate of 12% applies to the following services:

+ passenger transport;

* transportation of vehicles on vessels (ferry);

+ letting out of rooms in hotels or similar enterprises or letting out of cabins, camping areas, etc;
+ admission to cinemas;

* television license fees; and

+ admission to museums, galleries, amusements parks, sporting events, etc.

ya Exemptions

TA Exemption with right of deduction (zero rate)

In Norway, many supplies of goods and services are exempt from VAT, but with the right of deduction of
input VAT (zero-rated supplies). The exemptions may be conditional. However, it is not possible to outline
those conditions in detail within the framework of this publication.

Zero-rated supplies of goods and services include for instance:
Domestic supplies

+ books, newspapers and periodicals, etc.;

+ electronic news media services;

+ certain advertising publications;

+ electricity for households in northern Norway;

+ second-hand cars;

+ ships and seagoing vessels of at least 15 metres in length;
+ goods and services for such ships and seagoing vessels;
+ oil rigs and pipelines;

+ goods and services for use in petroleum activities;

+ aircrafts for use in commercial aviation;

+ goods and services for aircrafts in commercial aviation;

Norway | 437

--- Page 78 ---
+ foreign embassies and supplies to NATO or other foreign military services:
* transfers of undertakings;

* certain biological materials to hospitals, etc.;

+ funeral services;

+ workplace canteens:

* certain goods for scientific use;

+ “Cross border” supplies of goods and services:

+ tax-free sales to tourists;

+ — international transport services;

+ certain services provided by intermediaries;

+ certain warranty or guarantee repairs; and

+ electronic news media services (electronic newspapers)

Vehicles with electric propulsion

Up until 1 January 2024 vehicles with electric propulsion were exempted from VAT. From | January 2024,
only the sales price up to NOK 500.000 (appox. EUR 50,000) is exempt. For the part of the sales price
exceeding NOK 500,000, 25% VAT applies pursuant to § 6-8 of the VAT Act.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

Norway is not an EU-Member State and thus there are no possibilities of intra-Community supplies. All
cross-border transactions with Norway are regarded as export-import transactions.

7.1.2. Export Supplies

Norway is not an EU-Member State and thus no rules apply similar to those of the EU Member States
exempting such transactions,

In Norway, such situations will be govemed by the ordinary import and export rules according to the customs
regulations. A supply, provided the goods are not imported, may also be considered to take place outside
Norway, for example, if the supply takes place in a customs warehouse or in a free trade zone.

For B2C sales and the importation of low value goods through the internet, please see chapter 3.3 - Distance
sales threshold — VOEC (VAT on Electronic Commerce).
713 Warehousing

7. 1 VAT warehouse

Norway has not introduced a VAT warehousing regime. It is not possible to operate a VAT warehouse in
Norway. However, several types of customs warehouses are available (see also paragraph 2.3.2 on deferred
payment of the VAT on imports).

7.13.2 Excise warehouse
Norway has not introduced excise warehousing.
7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Itis possible to apply for a license to operate several types of customs warehouses and free-trade zones in
Norway. The license is issued by the customs authorities.

There are no general restrictions on who may be granted a license or the type of goods that can be stored ina
customs warehouse or a free-trade zone. However, the customs authorities may set conditions for issuing such
licenses.

In Norway there are four different types of customs warehouses:

+ Type A customs warehouse (general warehouse);

+ Type B customs warehouse (central warehouse);

+ Type C customs warehouse (duty-free sales at airports); and

+ Type D customs warehouse (processing warehouse).

438 | Norway

--- Page 79 ---
7.2 Exemption without right of deduction

Several supplies of goods and services are exempt from VAT. Suppliers of these goods and services are not
entitled to register or to deduct input VAT.

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Such goods and services include:

+ health care services;

+ cosmetic surgery which is needed from a pure medical need;
+ social security services:

+ educational services;

+ exercise of public authority; and

+ intra-governmental services.

Alternative treatment and cosmetic surgery services are taxable at the standard rate.
Other exemptions

Such goods and services include:

* — financial services;

+ — supply and letting out of immovable property (option to tax for the letting out of immovable property, see
7.3 below);

+ admission to sports events and certain sports services;

* admission to cultural events and certain goods and services supplied by artists:

+ sales by charitable institutions or organisations of goods and services of insignificant value, etc:

+ sales by non-profit organisations or associations;

+ lottery services;

+ funeral services;

+ management services by housing associations;

+ postage stamps, notes and coins as collectors’ items; and

+ _ offsetting emission allowances.

7.3. Option for taxation

Such goods and services include:
+ letting of immovable property;

8. Liabilities

8.1 Reverse charges

Norway is not a Member State of the EU and, thus, reverse charge rules do not comply with the reverse
charge rules in force in the EU Member States.

8.11 Reverse charge mechanism -- B2B services

Reverse charge is in the Norwegian VAT legislation indicated as “snudd avregning”.

Norway applies a limited system of reverse charge on the supply of certain services from abroad. In reverse
charge situations, the Norwegian customer of the service must report and pay the VAT on the purchased
services. According to the Norwegian VAT Act, businesses and public authorities located in Norway
must pay VAT on all purchases of services “capable of delivery from a remote location” from businesses
located abroad. This wording is used in the official translation of the VAT Law. Norway applies a special
interpretation of what is a remotely supplied service. The obligation to pay VAT arises if the customer is
a taxable person or a public body residing in Norway and the service is taxable in Norway. The services
covered are similar to the services listed in Article 59 of the EU VAT Directive (so-called “intangible
services”), In the Norwegian VAT Law, the purchase of these services is classified as an “import of services”
and the sale of such services to a foreign taxable person is classified as a zero-rated “export of services”
Norway | 439

--- Page 80 ---
A special reverse charge system also applies to electronic services and electronic communication services (see
also paragraph 1.5).

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers
Norway has no reverse charge system applicable for the supply of goods by non-residents, Therefore, if
goods (located in Norway) are supplied by a non-resident supplier to a Norwegian customer, the non-resident
supplier must be registered and charge Norwegian VAT, provided that the registration threshold of NOK
50,000 (approximately EUR 5,000) is exceeded. For services the general rules for supply of services apply.

8.1.3 Reverse charge mechanism — supply of natural gas and electricity
No reverse charge applies to the supply of natural gas or electricity by non-residents. Ifa non-resident
supplier makes domestic supplies of natural gas or electricity, the non-resident supplier must register for VAT

and charge Norwegian VAT, when the registration threshold of NOK 50,000 (approximately EUR 5,000) is
exceeded.

8.1.4 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules

As an anti-fraud measure Norway has introduced a reverse charge on the domestic supply of emission
allowances (effective 26 March 2010) and gold (effective 1 January 2014).

8.2 Joint and several liability

Norway applies a joint and several liability for fiscal representatives, see section 1.4, and in a VAT Group.
9. Recovery of input VAT

o1 Non-recoverable input VAT

In general, input tax is deductible from the output tax. Input VAT is not deductible on the following items:
+ catering;

* costs relating to hiring out rooms in connection with catering;

+ works of art or antiques;

+ representation or business entertainment;

+ gifts, goods and services for advertising purposes;

+ buildings, cabins, ete. that are to serve as housing or welfare needs; and

+ goods or services related to the use or ownership of passenger vehicles.

If an acquisition is for mixed use in taxable and non-taxable activities, rules of apportionment apply. The
basis for the apportionment for recovering input tax is the expected use of the goods or services in the part of
the enterprise having taxable activities. For certain business areas, there are simplification rules applicable

92 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT, for example, when the deductible input tax is higher than the output tax in a VAT period,
shall be paid back by the VAT authorities within 3 weeks of the receipt of the VAT return. If the payment is
not made within this time limit, the taxable person may under certain conditions be entitled to interest. On
certain conditions, shorter VAT periods may be granted.

9.3 Capital goods — adjustment periods

Special rules apply to the adjustment or deduction of input tax on capital goods and immovable property as
follows.

Capital goods are defined as machinery, fixtures and other operating assets for which the deductible input
tax on the cost price is NOK 50,000 (approximately EUR 5,000) or higher. Adjustment rules also apply to
immovable property that has been subject to new construction, extension or alteration work for which the
input VAT on the costs amounts to at least NOK 100,000 (approximately EUR 10,000).

440 | Norway

--- Page 81 ---
The applicable adjustment periods are:
+ immovable property - 10 years
+ capital goods - 5 years

94 VAT refund for foreigners

9.41 VAT refunds to EU and non-EU taxable persons

A VAT refund system for foreign taxable persons was introduced as of 1 July 1996. Foreign taxable persons
are entitled to a refund of the VAT on goods and services that they buy in Norway, or on goods they import
into Norway. The Norwegian legislation on VAT refunds to foreign taxable persons does not make a
distinction whether the taxable person is established in the EU or outside the EU. A Norwegian VAT refund
may, however, be subject to reciprocity. So far reciprocity has not been required and refunds are granted to
businesses established in countries without a refund scheme. VAT refunds will also be granted to taxable
persons from countries that do not have a VAT system such as the United States.

A VAT refund is subject to the following conditions:

+ the foreign taxable person does not perform any activities from which the liability to register for VAT in
Norway arises;

+ the business activity of the foreign taxable person would have been liable to VAT in accordance with the
VAT Law if it had been carried out in Norway; and

+ the VAT would have been deductible if the activity of the foreign taxable person would have been carried
out in Norway,

The right of refund is otherwise mainly in accordance with the right of deduction for input tax (see pharagraph 9.1).

‘An application for refund of the Norwegian VAT must relate to a period of not less than 3 months and not
more than a calendar year. The application must relate to goods and/or services imported or supplied during
the application period.

The application must be submitted no later than 6 months after the end of the calendar year to which it relates.
Thus, the due date by which the form with documentation must be received by the VAT authorities is 30 June
of the year following the year to which the application relates. Claims received after the deadline will not be
refunded.

‘The amount of the VAT claim must not be less than NOK 5,000 (approximately EUR 500). If the application
relates to an entire calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year, the minimum amount is NOK 500
(approximately EUR 50).

The application for a refund is made with a special form available from the local tax offices (“Skattekontoret”)
in Norway, or can be downloaded from the website of the VAT authorities: www.skatteetaten.no
hitps://tinyurl.com/m3bym2m5.

The form includes guidelines for the refund procedure.

A VAT Refund Application will usually be decided within 3 months of the date it is received.

However, there are no specific time limits provided for in the VAT legislation. The claim will usually be
refunded within 3 months after it is received, provided no further investigation or information is required. No

specific time limit for refunds is provided for in the VAT legislation.

Ifa claim is rejected, it is possible to file a complaint. The time limit for filing a complaint is usually 3 weeks
after the date of receiving the decision.

‘The application form is available in both Norwegian and English, and the application may be submitted in
Norwegian, Swedish, Danish or English.

Norway 1 441

--- Page 82 ---
In addition to the application form, the following documents must be submitted when making a claim:

* — original invoices or import documents as required in the general rules for authenticating the right of
deduction;

+ _aclear description of the nature of the business; and

+ acertificate from a public authority confirming that the applicant is engaged in such taxable business.

The application should be addressed to:
The Norwegian Tax Administration, V AT Refunds,P.O. Box 103,N O - 1501 Moss,N orway
Ifa courier is used, the correct address is:

The Norwegian Tax Administration.B ernt Ankers gate 17,1534 Moss,N orway

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ the date at which the invoice is issued;

+ aninvoice number (sequential);

+ the name and address of the supplier or service provider;

+ the nine-digit VAT identification number of the supplier followed by the postfix MVA (abbreviation for
VAT in Norway);

+ the name and address of the customer;

* aclear description of the delivered goods or the rendered services as well as the quantity;

+ the date on which the supply or service took place;

+ the taxable amount;

+ the applicable VAT rate;

+ the VAT in Norwegian Kroner (NOK); and

+ the total sum payable.

In the case of a foreign taxable person who is registered with a VAT representative, the following items must
also be included:

+ the Norwegian 9-digit VAT ID number of the foreign taxable person followed by the postfix MVA; and

+ the name and address of the VAT representative.

10.1.2 Time limits

There are a number of rules as to time limits for issuing an invoice. Generally, an invoice should be issued
within | month of the supply.

10.13 Currency

An invoice may be issued in any currency, but if'a currency other than Norwegian Kroner (NOK) is used,
then the VAT amount must be calculated in Norwegian Kroner (NOK) at the exchange rate valid on the date
of issue of the invoice. The applicable exchange rate can be found on the website of the Norwegian National
Bank (Norges Bank): htips://www.norges-bank.no/tema/Statistikk/Valutakurser/

Foreign suppliers of electronic services who are registered under the simplified scheme for such suppliers, the
applicable exchange rate is published weekly by the customs authorities.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

The applicable exchange rate on the date of issue of the invoice should be used when calculating the VAT
amount, if the invoiced amount is in another currency than Norwegian Kroner (NOK).

442 | Norway

--- Page 83 ---
10.1.5 — Self-billing

In general, it is not allowed for businesses to apply self-billing. Certain buyers may, however, issue the
invoice on behalf of the supplier (self-billing). Examples of such buyers are buyers having all information
on volume, weight, quality etc in order to calculate and pay the correct consideration and certain types of
businesses, mainly in the agricultural and fishing industries.

10.1.6 Simplified invoices

Norway has no rules that allow for simplified invoices. See 10.1.1 for the general invoice requirements.
10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices and other accounting materials must be kept for 5 years. If invoices, etc. are kept electronically,
paper documents may under certain conditions be destroyed after 3 years.

For certain types of businesses, in particular in the financial sector, certain accounting materials and
documentation must still be kept for 10 years.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

There are no special conditions for electronic invoicing. An electronic invoice can be sent in any format,
including PDF. There is, however, a condition that the electronic document cannot easily be altered.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period
‘The normal VAT return period is bi-monthly. It is possible to apply for shorter VAT periods for taxable
persons that have negative VAT returns on a regular basis.

Taxable persons with VAT-registered sales of less than NOK 1,000,000 (approximately EUR 100,000) in a
calendar year can apply for a single annual VAT return.

VAT returns must be submitted electronically via the website: www.altinn.no.

‘The VAT return period for foreign suppliers of electronic services is quarterly (see also paragraph 1.5).

10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment

‘The VAT return must usually be submitted within 1 month and 10 days after expiry of the VAT period. The
VAT payable must be paid to the Treasury within the same time limit.

VAT Period Month Due date
1 January—February 10 April
2 March-April 10 June
3 May-June 31 August
4 July-August ——~—~=*S «10 October.
5 September October 10 December
6 November-December 10 February

Annual VAT returns must be submitted and the VAT must be paid by 10 March of the following year.
‘Taxable persons that file quarterly VAT returns under the VOES and/or the VOEC, must pay the VAT within
20 days after the final VAT filing date.

10.2.3. Additional VAT returns

A taxable person may for a period of up to 3 years submit corrective VAT returns, If VAT is overpaid, the
VAT will be paid back by the VAT Authorities with the addition of interest. If VAT is underpaid, the VAT
authorities will normally charge interest on the underpaid amount. See also under penalties in paragraph 10.3.

Norway | 443

--- Page 84 ---
10.2.4 European sales listing
European sales listing does not apply because Norway is outside the EU.

10.2.5 National recapitulative statement
National recapitulative statement does not apply in Norway.

10.2.6 Tax assessment period
‘The tax assessment period in Norway is 5 years from the period in which the transaction took place. If the
taxpayer is imposed an increased additional penalty tax (for gross negligence or intent) or has been charged
(and convicted) under certain provisions of the Criminal Code, the tax assessment period is 10 years.

10.2.7 SAF-T

Norway introduced SAF-T (Standard Audit File — Tax) from | January 2017, SAF-T became mandatory as of
| January 2020, Exempt from the mandatory SAF-T reporting are businesses that are not required to perform
bookkeeping in Norway and businesses with an annual turnover of less than NOK 5,000,000 (EUR 500,000)
or have less than 600 accounting documents a year,

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return on time
From | January 2017 a running daily fine may be imposed for not filing the VAT retum on time. The fine will be
fixed annually and is linked to the Court fee (R). The daily running fine is / R per day, or in 2024 NOK 638,50
(approximately EUR 63) per day. The maximum fine is for 2024 NOK 63,850 (approximately EUR 6,300).

10.3.2 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities
If VAT is not paid on time, interest is applied to the unpaid amount. The amount of the interest is the same
that applies for delayed payments in general. The interest is fixed by the Ministry of Finance every 6 months,
Norway does not levy any penalties other than the above-mentioned interest if the VAT is paid too late.

10.3.3 Default penalties or criminal charges on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
With the introduction of the Tax Administration Act rules concerning violation of the VAT rules were
considerably changed. The rules concerning penalties were moved from the VAT Act to the new Tax
Administration Act. The normal additional tax (or penalty) payable if the taxable person has violated the VAT
legistation by negligence, will be 20% of the VAT amount involved.

Tf the violation is deliberate or committed under gross negligence, additional tax (“skjerpet lilleggsskatt”)
of 20% or 40% on top of the penalty pursuant to § 14-5, may be imposed. The maximum additional tax that
could be imposed for VAT violations is thus 60%.

From | October 2015, criminal offences regarding VAT and other taxes may also be prosecuted under the Criminal
Code as Tax Fraud or Serious Tax Fraud. Gross negligence tax fraud may be punishable under the Criminal Code.

Tax fraud can be punished by fines and/or up to 2 years of imprisonment. Serious tax fraud is subject to fines
and/or imprisonment of up to 6 years. Gross negligence tax fraud may be subject to fines and/or imprisonment
of up to 1 year, but up to 6 years if the negligence is considered to be serious.

Any person who deliberately or by negligence fails to comply with the bookkeeping legislation may be
subject to fines or imprisonment of up to 3 years. In severe cases, imprisonment of up to 6 years may apply. In
case of negligence, usually only fines are applied.

10.4 Intrastat
As Norway is outside the EU, no Intrastat reporting applies.

Poland

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented through the Act dated 11 March 2004 on the
taxation of goods and services (hereafter: the VAT Act). In Polish, the name of the VAT Act is “Ustawa z dnia
11 marca 2004 r, 0 podatku od towaréw i usiug”.

Apart of the VAT Act, there are also Decrees of Polish Minister of Finance implementing specific provisions
of the VAT Act. The examples of Decrees are as follows:

+ dated 9 March 2020, on the layouts of registration documents for VAT purposes;

+ dated 14 November 2014, on taxable persons who are not obliged to register for VAT;

+ dated 15 October 2019, on data details of the tax returns and VAT records;

+ dated 27 December 2021, on use of National e-Invoicing System;

+ dated 29 October 2021, on issuance of invoices;

+ dated 26 June 2020, on layouts of the recapitulative statements on intra-Community transactions relating
to movements of goods under the call-off stock procedure;

+ dated 9 December 2024, on reduced rates of tax on goods and services;

+ dated 20 December 2013, on VAT exemptions and conditions under which these exemptions apply;

+ dated 20 October 2021, on the VAT refunds for certain categories of taxable persons;

+ dated 29 April 2019, on cash registers;

+ dated 12 September 2021 on technical requirements for cash registers;

+ dated 17 December 2024, on exemption from the obligation to keep sales records using the cash registers;

+ dated 30 March 2023, on VAT evidence kept by Members of VAT Group;

* dated 22 December 2024, on notification of the intention to benefit from exemption in a Member State
other than Poland and quarterly information (SME);

+ dated 19 May 2020, on the layout of import declaration for VAT purposes.

Value Added Tax (VAT) is commonly called *VAT” or ‘ptu’.
1. Taxable Persons

Ll Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Any person or organization unit which conducts in Poland business activity subject to VAT, is obliged to
register for VAT before the first activity is performed and is referred to as a ‘taxable person’. The taxable
persons who perform only activities exempt from VAT or whose annual turnover is below the threshold for
registration, may opt for registration voluntary.

‘There is an annual threshold for VAT registration PLN 200,000 (net) apart of the SME threshold. The values of:
+ intra-Community distance sales of goods which are not subject to VAT in Poland;
+ distance sales of imported goods which are not subject to VAT in Poland;
+ supplies of goods and services exempt from VAT except for transactions related to:
+ immovable properties,
* certain financial, security and re-security services,
if these transactions do not have an ancillary character; and
+ supply of goods which are considered, based on the separate provisions on corporate income tax, as fixed
or intangible asset subject to a depreciation;
are not included in the above registration threshold.

The taxable person may opt for VAT registration before crossing the threshold for registration. The
notification should be filed to the tax office before the beginning of the month from which the taxable person
opts for taxation or before making his first taxable transaction (in the case of starting the activities during the
tax year).

‘The threshold for VAT registration does not apply to the taxable persons who supplies the following:

+ goods listed in the Appendix No 12 to the VAT Act, ie., for goods made from precious materials or with
participation of such materials or others (CN ex 71, ex 8549, ex 9602, ex 9706);

* — goods subject to the excise tax, except for:

To register for VAT, it is necessary to submit to the competent tax office the registration forms called VAT-R
and NIP.

The tax office has the obligation to verify the registration forms before a VAT Id. No. is granted. The tax

office does not register the person for VAT if:

+ the data included in VAT registration form (i.e. VAT-R) is not true;

+ the person/entity does not exist;

+ despite attempts of the tax office, it is not possible to contact the person/entity or his attorney;

+ the person/entity or his attorney does not appear at the request of the tax office;

* according to the information available by the tax office, it appears that the taxable person may conduct
activities with the intention of using activities of banks for purposes related to tax fraud; and

+ a judgment was issued prohibiting the taxable person from running a business.

In the above cases, the tax office does not have the obligation to inform the taxable person about denying his

application for VAT registration.

If the VAT registration form is submitted to the tax office by the attorney acting on behalf of the taxable
person, the attorney is jointly and severally liable for overdue VAT together with this person. The attorney
is liable for overdue VAT resulting from activities made by the taxable person during the first 6 months after
VAT registration. The liability of the attorney is limited to PLN 500,000.00 (approx. CUR 116,000). The
attorney is liable if overdue VAT results from any unreliable settlement made with the intention to receive
financial advantage (i.e, VAT fraud).

The tax authorities confirm registration via the issuance of a special confirmation called VAT-5. The

confirmation is issued only at the request of the taxable person after payment of a stamp duty of PLN 170
(approximately EUR 40).

As of | January 2025, Poland adopted the SME scheme resulting from VAT Directive, The taxable person
established in Poland may exempt from VAT supply of the goods and services with the place of supply in
other MS than Poland, if his entire annual net sales is in not higher than EUR 100,000, In order to benefit
from the exemption, the Polish taxable person should notify the tax authorities about his intention to benefit
from the exemption. Such prior notification should cover data of this taxable person and information about
the MS in which he wants to benefit from the exemption as well as his annual turnover in the previous and
current year in the EU, Polish tax authorities exchange the data with the other MS and in 35 days issues

for him an “EX Identification number’. If the taxable person does meet the condition to benefit from the
exemption for SME than the tax authorities refuse to issue Polish ‘Ex Identification number’. The taxable
person using SME scheme is obliged to file to the tax office a quarterly information covering the following:
- data of the taxable person including his ‘Ex Identification number’;

~ the amount of turnover for each MS for the quarter; and

- the amount of turnover for Poland for the quarter.

if the taxable person acts via an attomey (plus confirmation of payment of the stamp duty, i.e. PLN 17
(approximately EUR 4) on the same bank account as the registration fee).

The original copies of the above documents or copies certified by a notary are required, If the documents are
issued in another language than Polish, they must be transtated into Polish by a sworn/certified translator.

The foreign taxable person should submit the registration documents to the following tax office:

Drugi Urzgd Skarbowy Warszawa Srédmiescie
Jagielloriska St. 15

03-719 Warsaw

Poland

If the foreign taxable person has a fixed establishment for VAT purposes in Poland, the registration documents
should be filed to the competent tax authority (which depends on the place of business and income value)

Non ~ FU taxable persons, except for persons established in Norway and the UK, must appoint a fiscal
representative to register for VAT purposes in Poland.

De-registration from VAT

If the taxable person stops performing taxable activities he is obliged to notify the tax authorities (form
VAT-Z) and the tax office de-registers the taxable person from the register of the taxable persons (the
Whitelist of taxable person). The tax authorities, in specific cases, are entitled to de-register the taxable person
on their own accord. Depending on the circumstances of the case, the tax office restores the taxable person’s
registration for VAT, or the taxable person must register for VAT once again if needed. De-registration from
VAT results in withdrawing the taxable person from VIES.

The tax office is entitled to de-register the taxable person from VAT without sending the notification to the

taxable person, if:

* the taxable person does not exist;

+ — despite attempts of the tax office it is not possible to contact the taxable person or his attorney;

+ data included in the VAT registration form are not true;

* the taxable person or its attorney does not appear at the request of the tax authorities;

* according to the information available by the tax authorities, it appears that the taxable person may
conduct activities with the intention of using the activities of banks or cooperative savings and credit
unions for purposes related to tax fraud; or

+ a judgment was issued prohibiting the taxable person from running a business.
Poland | 449

--- Page 90 ---
The tax office is also entitled to de-register the taxable person from VAT, if the taxable person:

+ suspends conducting the business activity based on the provisions of the Act on the freedom of
conducting business activity for the following 6 months;

+ — did not submit to the tax office VAT returns for 3 consecutive months or ] quarter (depending on the
method of filing of VAT returns);

+ — submitted to the tax office for 6 consecutive months or 2 consecutive quarters VAT declarations in which
no sales or purchases from which it deducts input VAT are reported;

+ issued invoices or correction invoices which certify activities which have not been performed;

+ while conducting business activity, knew or had reasonable grounds to suspect that his suppliers or
customers directly or indirectly participate in tax fraud involving goods and services provided by or to the
taxable person.

If the taxable person makes taxable supplies despite of suspension of his business activity, he is obliged to
notify the tax office about the period in which he will be making supplies during the suspension. In such
case the tax office does not de-register the taxable person from VAT or restore VAT registration for the period
indicated in the notification. As soon as the taxable person resumes his business activity after suspension, the
tax office is required to register the taxable person for VAT once again without filing a new registration form.
In that case the taxable person keeps the same VAT status as he had before the suspension of his business
activity.

The tax office does not de-register the taxable person if the taxable person did not file VAT returns but - upon
the tax office’s request - proves that he conducted business activities. The taxable person must then submit the
overdue VAT returns within 2 months of notification of the de-registration. The tax office will then be required to
resume the VAT registration without the obligation for the taxable person to file a new registration form.

‘The tax office does not de-register the taxable person if the taxable person provides the tax office with
explanations demonstrating that not reporting sales and input VAT in VAT returns, results from the specifics
of the taxable person’s business activity. In that case the tax office resumes the VAT registration without the
obligation to file the registration forms if the taxable person proves the specifics of his business activity.

If the taxable person issues invoices which certify activities which have not been actually performed, the
tax office does not de-register the taxable person from VAT if the taxable person makes this only by mistake
or has no knowledge about the issuance of such unreliable invoices. In this case the tax office resumes VAT
registration without the obligation to file new registration forms. In this case the taxable person keeps the
same VAT status as it had before the de-registration. Simultaneously the tax office de-registers the taxable
person from the list of VAT EU taxable persons (i.e. VIES) if the taxable person files to the tax office

for 3 consecutive months or one quarter (depending on the method of filing) VAT return with no sales or
acquisitions effecting the right to deduct input VAT. In that case the tax office is obliged to notify the taxable
person about the de-registration from VAT EU. The same applies, if the taxable person does not file to the tax
office the EC Sales and Purchases Lists for 3 consecutive months.

13 VAT Grouping

A VAT Group can be created by taxable persons who are financially, economically, and organizationally
related entities and who conclude an agreement based on which the VAT Group is established, The VAT Act
specifies in detail the content of such an agreement. The VAT Group can be established for a period no shorter
than 3 years.

The VAT Group becomes a single taxable person on the date specified in the agreement establishing the VAT
Group, but not earlier than on the date of registration by the tax office as so called ‘Active VAT taxpayer’.
The VAT Group assumes the rights and obligations of the Members of the VAT Group regarding tax for the
period in which this Group has the status of the taxable person.

‘As soon as the VAT Group is registered for VAT, the tax office automatically de-registers the Members of
VAT Group from VAT. Hence, there is a single VAT registration under one VAT identification number of the
taxable persons belonging to the VAT Group.

450 | Poland

--- Page 91 ---
The taxable persons are:

* financially related if one of the taxable persons who is a member of the VAT Group directly holds
more than 50% of the shares in the share capital or more than 50% of the voting rights in the control,
constituting or management bodies, or more than 50% of the right to participate in profit of each of the
other taxable person who are Members of the Group;

* economically related if the main activity of the members of the VAT Group is of the same nature or the
activities conducted by the members of the VAT Group are complementary and interdependent, or the
member of the VAT Group conducts activities that are wholly or largely used by other members of the
VAT Group; and

+  organizationally related if the members are legally or in fact, directly or indirectly, under common
management, or organize their activities wholly or partly in agreed.

‘The conditions relating to the existence of financial, economic, and organizational links between the members
ofa VAT Group must be met continuously for the period during which this Group has the status of one
taxpayer.

An entity can be a member of only one VAT Group. The members of the VAT Group cannot be modified, i.e.,
expanded to include other members or reduced by any of its members.

‘The VAT Group may be created by taxable persons:
+ established in Poland; or
+ not established in Poland but conducting business activities in Poland via a branch located in Poland.

14 Fiscal Representative

The VAT Law differentiates between the foreign taxable persons established within the EU and outside the
EU as regards to the obligation to appoint a fiscal representative.

‘Taxable persons established in the EU - or not established in the EU but having a fixed establishment in the
BU - may appoint a fiscal representative. Hence, appointing a fiscal representative is optional for taxable
persons established in the EU.

‘Taxable persons not established in the EU and not having any fixed establishments for VAT purposes in
one of the EU MS, are required to appoint a fiscal representative to register for VAT in Poland. No fiscal
representative needs to be appointed for taxable persons established in the UK and Norway.

A legal entity or an individual person or organizational unit not having a legal personality must fulfil certain

conditions to act as the fiscal representative of the foreign taxable person. Such person must:

* be established or domiciled in Poland;

* act as a taxable person registered with the tax authorities as a so-called VAT ‘Active VAT payer’;

* in case of taking over the obligation to settle VAT due on the importation of goods followed by an intra-
Community supply of the goods from Poland, he must also be registered as a VAT EU taxable person;

+ have no overdue tax liabilities payable over the past 24 months to the Polish State (i.e. not exceeding 3%
of the tax due; calculated separately on cach type of tax which is payable in the given settlement period).
This condition is met if the overdue tax is paid with penalty interest within 30 days from the day in
which the overdue tax had appeared and the overdue tax related to no more than 2 periods in the given 24
months, calculated separately on each type of the tax;

+ _ over the past 24 months have not been punished for committing a crime within the meaning of the Fiscal
Penal Code. In case of fiscal representatives who are not natural persons - this condition applies to a
person who is a shareholder of a civil or commercial entity without legal personality, a member of the
management or its the chief accountant; and

* be entitled to render tax advisory services within the meaning of the Act on Tax Advisory services or
is entitled to render accounting services within the meaning of the Act on Accounting. The condition
to possess the license to act as a tax advisor or licensed accountant, does not apply for customs agents
who act as the fiscal representative of taxable persons whose transactions exclusively consist of the
importation of goods followed by the intra-Community supply of those goods.

Poland | 451

--- Page 92 ---
There is an obligation to conclude a written agreement in which the fiscal representative is appointed. The
VAT Act provides a list of information which should be covered by this agreement.

The fiscal representative is required to perform in the name and on behalf of the client all activities needed to
settle the VAT, including preparation of VAT returns and EU sales and purchase listings, keeping and storing
VAT evidence and other activities specified in the agreement.

A fiscal representative is jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT due by his principal (the foreign
taxable person). With regard to the liability of a fiscal representative representing a taxable person who is not
registered for VAT in Poland, effecting an importation followed by an intra-Community supply of the goods
please see paragraph 2.3.1.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The transfer of a business/enterprise or an independent part of a business/enterprise is out of the scope of
Polish VAT.

The VAT law defines ‘an independent part of an enterprise’ (the equivalent of ‘a part of a totality of assets’)
as a set of tangible and non-tangible components organizationally and financially separated in an existing
enterprise, including liabilities, intended for performing specific economic tasks, which could form an
independent enterprise performing such tasks on its own.

Additionally, the acquirer of the business or organized part of the business is required to continue adjustment
of input VAT which is subject to the pro-rata deduction (if the assets were used for mixed supplies) until the
end of the adjustment period for the given asset.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

A supply of goods or services fee of charge, as a rule, is subject to VAT in Poland.

‘The supply of goods free of charge is subject to VAT if the goods are provided for the private use of

the taxable person or his employees, including former employees, partners, shareholders, members of
cooperatives and their houschold members, members of decision-making bodies of legal persons, members
of associations or donated - if the taxable person was entitled to deduct the input VAT at the moment of
purchase, import or production of these goods or their parts.

There is no VAT on the goods provided as a gift of low value and samples, if the transfer is made for purposes
related to the taxable person’s business activity. As gifis of low value are treated goods free of charge
provided to one person with a total value not exceeding PLN 100 (net) (approx. EUR 26) in the tax year, if
the taxable person keeps records enabling the identification of these persons. If there is no record of the goods
provided free of charge, the supply of goods which price does not exceed PLN 20 (approx. EUR 4.70) is not
taxed.

There is no VAT taxation on samples provided free of charge. A sample is identified as an example of a good,
ora small quantity thereof, provided free of charge, identifiable as a sample which allows for the assessment
of the characteristics and properties of the goods in their final form, and their handing over by the taxable
person aims to promote this product and it does not serve the purpose of essentially meeting the needs of the
final recipient in terms of a free product, unless meeting the needs of this recipient is an inherent element of
the promotion of this product and is intended to encourage this recipient to purchase the promoted product,

Subject to VAT is also rendering services free of charge. Such supply involves:
+ the use of the taxable person’s goods for purposes other than business activity (in particular for the private
use of the taxpayer or his employees, including former employees, partners, shareholders, members of

452 | Poland

--- Page 93 ---
cooperatives and their household members, members of the decision-making bodies of legal persons ,
members of the association) if the taxable person was entitled, in whole or in part, to deduct input VAT at
the time of purchase, import or production of these goods or their parts;

+ free provision of services for the private purposes of the taxable person or his employees, including
former employees, partners, shareholders, members of cooperatives and their household members,
members of the decision-making bodies of legal persons, members of associations, and any other free
provision of services for purposes other than the business activities of the taxable person.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

Importation followed by an intra-Community supply of the goods, is exempt from VAT if the following
conditions are met:
* the importer who subsequently makes the intra-Community supply of the goods is registered for intra-
Community transactions in Poland and provides his PL EU VAT Id, No. at the time of importation;
+ ifthe importer is not registered for the intra~Community transactions in Poland, he must appoint a fiscal
representative and use EU VAT Id. No. of this representative;
+ the importer provides proof that the goods are intended for the intra-Community supply to another EU
Member State;
+ the importer provides his own EU VAT Id. No., or the EU VAT Id. No. of his customer granted in the
country to which the intra-Community supply will be made;
+ within 4 months from the month in which the tax obligation in respect to importation of the goods arose,
the importer presents the tax authorities with:
+ the document confirming the reporting of this intra-Community supply in the European sales and
purchase list; or
+ the confirmation from the head of the tax office that this intra-Community supply was reported in the
European sales and purchase list if multiple intra-Community supplies were made and it is not clear
from the European sales and purchase list alone that this transaction has been reported;
+ the importer has not violated the obligation to submit the European sales and purchase lists in respect to
imported goods (intended for the intra~Community supply of the goods) for 12 of the last 17 months.

If the above conditions are not met, the taxable person is required to submit a security for the VAT due on the
importation of the goods to the tax authorities. The amount of the security is equal to the amount of the VAT
due on the importation of the goods. The security should be provided based on the Customs regulations.

If the importer of the goods is not registered for VAT in Poland and appoints a fiscal representative, this fiscal
representative must report the transactions in his own name but for the benefit of the importer. In such case
the importer is jointly and severally liable for VAT together with the fiscal representative.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

It is possible to settle VAT duc on importation of goods in a periodical VAT return without physical payment

to the tax authorities. In such cases, the taxable person reverse charges import VAT in the VAT retum over

the period when the tax obligation arose. To benefit from the simplification, it is necessary to submit to the

customs-fiscal office before each 6-month period in which the taxable person declares importation of goods

(issued no earlier than 6 months before the importation of goods):

+ acertificate of absence of tax arrears with respect to tax due to the State and social insurance
contributions; and

+ the confirmation of registration for VAT purposes in Poland.

Alternatively, the taxable person may submit its own statement confirming that he does not have any

outstanding arrears of tax and social insurance contributions.

In order to apply the reverse charge, customs declarations must be made by a customs representative, either

directly or indirectly. This condition does not apply if the taxable person:

+ is holder of an AEO Certificate; or

+ has customs authorizations to clear the goods with simplifications, i.e. file simplified declaration or entry
of the goods the declarant’s register.

Poland | 453

--- Page 94 ---
In the case of entities, which are not established and do not have a fixed establishment in Poland, the
documents described above must be submitted to Dolnoglaski Urzed Celno-Skarbowy we Wroctawiu.

If the customs clearance is performed by a taxable person who does not have its own permission on customs

simplifications and uses the permission granted to the customs agent, this agent when acting as the direct

representative of the taxable person importing the goods, is jointly and severally liable for VAT. The principle

of joint and several liability does not apply to customs representatives if the taxable person they represent:

+ is holder of the AEO Certificate; or

+ has customs authorizations to clear the goods with simplifications, i.e. file simplified declaration or entry
of the goods the declarant’s register.
