VAT import declaration

This special VAT import declaration must be submitted to customs authorities by the 16" day of each month
following the month in which the tax obligation resulting from the importation of the goods arose. This same
deadline applies for scttling amounts of VAT due on importation,

35 Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call of stock

Poland implemented simplification rules for goods supplied via a call-off stock resulting from Article 17a of
the VAT Directive 2006/112/EC.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

Similarly to the provisions of the VAT Directive, the place of supply of goods which are installed or
assembled (with or without a trial run), by an entity making the supply of goods or entity acting on its behalf,
is the place where the goods are installed or assembled. Simple activities that enable the functioning of the
goods that are installed or assembled in line with their purpose, cannot be considered as an installation or
assembly supply. There is no formal definition of the term ‘supply with installation’ in Polish VAT law.

The supply of goods with installation is treated as a domestic supply of the goods. The VAT due on such
supply may be reverse charged to the customer under certain conditions. The conditions to apply the reverse
charge on domestic supplies of goods (including the supply of goods with installation) are explained below
(see chapter 8).

33 Distance sales

Polish VAT Law has been harmonized in line with the VAT Directive and Poland applies the same rules as in
the other Member States of the EU. There is one registration threshold, i.c., EUR 10,000 calculated based on
the intra-Community distance sales as well as TBE services. There are special schemes for the importation of
goods with an intrinsic value below EUR 150. The VAT returns relating to OSS and JOSS can cover amounts
in EUR. Where payments for the supply of goods or services were made in currencies other than EUR, the
exchange rate published by the European Central Bank on the last day of the relevant settlement period shall
be used to convert them into EUR, and if it is not published on that day - the exchange rate published on the
following day.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Where Poland is the EU Member State of the arrival of the goods, the wording of the Polish VAT legislation
does not preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered in the
country of arrival of the goods but to benefit from the simplification, the middleman (B) cannot use a Polish
VAT registration number. However, the middleman cannot be established in Poland.

454 | Poland

--- Page 95 ---
Additionally, the VAT Act requires that party B includes in its invoice the following information:

“VAT: Faktura WE uproszezona na mocy art, 135-138 ustawy 0 ptu” or “VAT: Faktura uproszona na mocy
art. 141/197dyrektywy 2006/112/WE” and information that the last taxable person in the chain is liable to
settle VAT. This description means that the transaction is subject to the simplified procedure. This provision
of the VAT Act should be applied with consideration of the respective VAT provisions in force in the EU
Member State in which B reports this supply.

There is also an obligation to keep a special accounting of the goods supplied based on this simplification.
Poland did not implemented the derogation specified in Article 197 (2) VAT Directive.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

Generally, the threshold for the intra-Community acquisition of goods is PLN 50,000 (net) (approx. EUR
11,900). This threshold is not applicable to the acquisition of a new means of transport (e.g. cars) and excise
goods.

3.6 Effective Use & Enjoyment

Poland bas implemented effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59a of the VAT Directive with
respect to the transport of goods carried out for the benefit of taxable persons.

When transport of goods is carried out entirely outside of the EU, the place of supply is considered outside
the EU if the transport is provided to a taxable person established in Poland or having a fixed establishment to
which the services are rendered. When the transport of goods is carried out entirely on the territory of Poland,
the place of supply is in Poland if the transport is supplied for the benefit of the taxable person established
outside the EU or having a fixed establishment outside of the EU to which the service is rendered

4. Chargeable event & Chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes chargeable at the time the goods or services
are supplied, For goods, this is the date when the purchaser receives the right to dispose of the goods as the
owner. A supply of service is deemed to be carried out on the date the service is finally executed.

However, in case of clearly enumerated activities VAT becomes chargeable as soon as the payment is
received, or the invoice is issued. The list of activities for which a specific chargeable event occurs, is quite
extensive, ¢.g.:
. receipt of all or part of the payment for:

release of goods by the principal to the commission agent based on a commission contract;

+ transfer based on the order of a public authority or an entity acting on behalf of such a body of
ownership of goods in exchange for compensation;

+ carried out during the execution of the delivery of goods;

+ providing, based on separate provisions, at the request of common courts, administrative, military,
or public prosecution services related to court or preparatory proceedings, except for general B2B
services;

+ provision of certain tax exempt financial and insurance services;

+ subsidies and other similar payments;

* the issuance of an invoice due to:

* provision of construction or construction-assembly services;

+ _ delivery of printed books (CN ex 4901 10 00, 4901 91 00, ex 4901 99 00 and 4903 00 00) - excluding
maps and leaflets - as well as newspapers and magazines: and

* activities involving the printing of books (CN ex 4901 10 00, 4901 91 00, ex 4901 99 00 and
4903 00 00) - excluding maps and leaflets - as well as newspapers, magazines (CN 4902), except for
services to which Art. 28b relates (i.¢. services to which the Article 44 of VAT Directive 2006/112/EC
applies) constituting the “import” of services;

Poland | 455

--- Page 96 ---
+ electricity, heat or cooling, and gas; and
+ provision of services:
+ telecommunications;
+ several types of waste disposal and water treatment services;
+ lease, tenancy, leasing, or services ofa similar nature;
+ protection of persons and services of protection, supervision and storage of property; and
= permanent legal and office services;
+ — distribution of electricity, heating or cooling and gas; and
+ except for services to which Article 44 VAT Directive applies, constituting the “import” of services.

For the intra-Community acquisition of goods, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice, latest on
the 15" day of the month following the acquisition if no invoice has been issued by that time.

For the intra-Community supply of goods, the chargeable event occurs on the date of issue of the invoic
latest on the 15% day of the month following the date of the supply.

In case of B2B services (i.e. covered by the Art. 44 of VAT Directive), the general rules relating to the
chargeable event apply.

42 Advance payments

‘Where a payment is to be made before the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes chargeable on receipt
of the payment.

4.3 Continuous supplies

If services are accepted partly, it is deemed that the service is supplied partly if for this part of service
payment is received. If in respect to services, consecutive payments or settlements are established, it is
deemed that the services are performed by the end of each period to which these payments or settlements
relate, until the provision of this service is completed.

Aservice provided on a continuous basis for a period longer than one year, for which due to its supply in a
given year, payment or settlement deadlines do not expire, shall be deemed to have been performed by the
end of each tax year, until the service is completed.

4.4 Cash accounting

So-called ‘small taxable persons’ may opt for cash accounting. The following taxable persons are considered

as ‘small taxable persons’:

+ taxable persons whose value of sales (including the tax amount) did not exceed the amount expressed in
PLN in the current and preceding tax year, corresponding to the equivalent of EUR 2,000,000; and

+ taxable persons that run a brokerage company, manage investment funds, manage alternative investment
funds, are an agent, contractor or other person providing services ofa similar nature, with the exception
of a commission - if the amount of the commission or other remuneration for the services rendered
(including the tax amount) did not exceed in the current and preceding tax year EUR 45,000.

“Small taxable persons’ must notify the tax authorities about opting for the cash accounting regime by the end

of the month preceding the period from which the taxable person would like to apply the cash accounting.

If the small taxable person opts for cash accounting than the chargeable event takes place

+ on the date of receipt of the entire or part of the payment - in the case of the supply of goods or services to
the taxable person who is registered as so-called VAT active taxable person; and

+ on the date of receipt of the entire or part of the payment, no later than the 180th day from the date of the
supply of the good or service - in the case of the supply of goods or services to other persons than taxable
persons who are registered as so-called VAT active taxable persons.

The invoice issued by ‘small taxable persons’ which opted for cash accounting should mention ‘metoda
kasowa’ (i.e., ‘cash accounting’).

456 | Poland

--- Page 97 ---
5. Taxable amount

SA Bad Debt Relief

As a rule, the supplier is entitled to adjust the taxable base and output VAT if the receivable is not paid in
any form within 90 days from the date of payment resulting from the agreement or the invoice (i.e. the
‘un-recoverability of the debt is substantiated). It is possible to adjust output VAT partly if the receivable is
partially paid.

There are a few conditions which should be met by the supplier to adjust output VAT:

+ on the day of filing of the VAT return in which the output VAT is adjusted, the supplier must be registered
as an active taxable person; and

+ the invoice documenting the receivable is not older than 3 years counted from the end of the year in
which it was issued.

The output VAT can be adjusted in the VAT return filed for the period in which the recoverability of the debt
becomes substantiated. The tax authorities should be notified in the VAT return about the amount of the
adjustment.

On the other hand, the customer who does not pay the receivable within 90 days from the due date of
payment, is obliged to adjust the amount of input VAT resulting from the invoice not paid.

If the customer does not pay the amount due for the supply within 90 days from the elapse of the payment
deadline indicated on the invoice or respective agreement, he excludes the amount of input VAT recovered
with respect to that supply, regardless of whether the supplier decreased his output VAT under the bad debt
relief simplification. The exclusion of input VAT should be made by the customer in the VAT return filed over
the period in which the 90 days’ time limit for payment elapses.

5.2 Cash Discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
23% VAT EUR_230
Total to be paid EUR 1,230

If payment is received within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier should report EUR 230 on its VAT return
and should issue the correction invoice to adjust the taxbase and input VAT. The customer can deduct
EUR 225.40 in total.

Polish VAT law provides detailed conditions relating to the method and time limits in which both the
customer and the supplier should make the adjustment.

53 Open Market Value

Basically, if the supplier and customer are related parties, the value of the supply (the tax base of the

transaction) should be established based on the open market value if one of the parties of the supply has

a limited right to deduct input VAT. The tax authorities are entitled to determine the tax base based on the

market value if it turns out that the connections between the parties of the transaction had an impact on the

determination of remuneration for the supply of goods or services if the renumeration is:

+ lower than the market value and the customer of goods or services does not have the full right to deduct
the amount of input VAT:

+ lower than the market value and the supplier of goods or services does not have the full right to deduction
of input VAT and the supply of the goods or services is exempt from VAT;

+ higher than the market value and the supplier of goods or services does not have the full right to deduct
VAT.

Poland | 457

--- Page 98 ---
The tax authorities have no right to determine the tax base based on the open market value if the transaction
has been subject of the advanced pricing agreement concluded with the tax authorities based on special tax
provisions being in force in Poland.

5.4 — Returnable Packaging

There is a general rule based on which the value of the packaging increases the tax base of supplied goods as
such. If the packaging is retuned, then the tax base of supplied goods should be decreased accordingly.

If the taxpayer collects a deposit for the packaging, the value of the deposit is not included in the tax base.

However, such a deposit must be specified in the contract the for supply of the goods. If the customer fails to

return the packaging for which the deposit has been taken, the tax base should be increased accordingly. The

tax based should be increased:

+ onthe day following the day on which the contract provided for the return of the packaging - if the
packaging was not returned within the period specified in the contract; and

+ the 60th day from the date of delivery of the packaging - if the contract does not specify the deadline for
returning this packaging.

In the case of reusable packaging, its value is not included in the tax base if the taxpayer delivered the goods
in such packaging. If the buyer fails to return the reusable packaging, the entity introducing products in a
beverage packaging increases the tax base by the value of this packaging. The entity introducing products in
beverage packaging calculates the amount of change in the tax base on the last day of the year by determining,
the difference between the number of reusable packaging placed on the market and the number returned in a
given year. The amount of the difference includes VAT. If there is VAT payable on the difference it should be
setlled in relurn for the first month following the year for which VAT on reusable packaging has to be paid. If
the number of reusable packaging returned in a given year is higher than the number of reusable packaging
placed on the market in a given year, the entity introducing products in beverage packaging, takes this
difference into account when determining the value of the tax base for the following years.

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate

Currently the standard rate in Poland is 23%.

6.2 Reduced rate

There are two reduced VAT rates 8% and 5%.

‘The reduced 8% VAT rate applies to (for example):
+ cerlain categories of foodstuffs;

+ living animals;

+ hotel accommodation;

+ fertilizers;

+ newspapers;

+ products connected with healthcare:

+ passenger transport;

+ admission to certain sport and cultural events;
* construction work on certain houses;

+ services related to waste disposal; and

* certain labor-intensive services such as hairdressers shoemakers.

The reduced 5% VAT rate applies to (for example):

+ wide variety of foodstuffs;
+ some hygienic products.

458 | Poland

--- Page 99 ---
6.3 Super—reduced and zero rates

Poland has not introduced a 0% VAT rate to be applied to goods or services included in Annex III to the EU
VAT Directive or similar supplies.

7. Exemptions

71 Exemptions with the right of deduction

Poland has exemptions with the right to deduct VAT (‘zero rates’) for the following exemplary supplies of

goods and services:

* exports (export in Poland is subject to 0% VAT rate which is equivalent to exemptions with the right to
deduct of input VAT);

+ intra-Community supplies of goods (the intra~Community supply of goods is subject to 0% VAT rate
which is equivalent to exemptions with the right to deduct input VAT);

+ the following supplies made to seagoing ship owners:
+ supplies of seagoing passenger liners, cruisers, ferries, and similar units used for passenger transport;
+ supplies of seagoing tankers used for transport of petroleum and chemicals; and seagoing fishing

vessels, etc,

+ importation of means of sea transport, fishing vessels and sea salvage boats by seagoing ship owners; and
* — supplies of parts and equipment for means of sea transport, fishing vessels and sea salvage boats;

+ importation of means of air transport and related spare parts and equipment by air carriers performing
mainly international transport;

* services related to air-traffic control supplied for air carriers performing mainly international transport;

+ services supplied on the territory of seaports, related to international transport;

+ services related to rental, leasing, charter etc. of means of sea and air transport;

+ services directly related to the arrangement of the export of goods, in particular issuance of forwarding
documents, bills of lading and dealing with customs formalities;

* services directly related to the importation of goods in the case where the value of these services has been
included in the tax base, excluding insurance services and importation of tax-exempt goods; and

+ the supply of international transport services.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

The zero VAT rate on the intra-Community supplies of goods applies if the customer and the supplier are
taxable persons having appropriate VAT EU Id. No. The supplier is obliged to collect the proof that the goods
have been delivered to the customer located in the other EU MS. The taxable person can prove the intra-
Community supply of the goods either by providing documents mentioned in the Polish VAT Act or Article
45a of Implementing regulation. Both lists of proofs are allowed and the decision of which documents are
collected, is up to the taxable person.

According to Polish VAT Act, to apply the zero VAT rate on the intra-Community supply of goods, the taxable

person should collect:

* transport documents received from the transport company, which confirm that the goods have been.
delivered to the destination in a different EU Member State than Poland; and

* a specification of the delivery.

If the above documents jointly do not prove the delivery of the goods, the supplier may prove the delivery by
other documents such as:

+ correspondence with the customer, including the purchase order;

+ insurance and freight documents;

* confirmation of the payment for the goods; and

* confirmation that the customer received the goods in a different EU Member State than Poland.

If the goods were transported by the supplier’s own trucks, the supplier must prepare a special document of
delivery which includes the following information:
+ the names and addresses of the supplier and the customer;

Poland | 459

--- Page 100 ---
+ the address of the delivery;

+ description and quantities of the goods:

+ type and the truck registration number; and

+ aconfirmation that the customer received the goods in a different EU Member State than Poland.

These pieces of evidence must be collected by the supplier before the VAT return over the period in which
the intra~Community supply took place, must be filed. If this does not happen, the supplier has an additional
two months (in case of monthly filing) or one quarter (in case of quarterly filing) to collect evidence. If no
evidence is collected, the supplier is then required to charge VAT on the supply.

If the above pieces of evidence are collected with a delay, the supplier has the right to report the intra-
Community supply in the VAT return for the period in which the supply was made and report it in the relevant
European sales listing. The supplier has also the right to adjust paid output VAT.

The zero VAT rate cannot be applied to the intra~Community supply if the taxable person does not file
European sales and purchase listings at all, files it after the time limit or it covers incorrect data, unless the
taxable person reports to the tax office a written explanation of the irregularity.

7.1.2 Export supplies

The export of goods is understood as the supply of the goods which are dispatched or transported from the

territory of Poland to a destination outside the territory of the BU by:

+ the supplier or on his behalf; or

+ the customer established outside the territory of Poland or on his behalf, except for goods transported by
the customer himself for the equipping, fueling, and provisioning of pleasure boats and tourist aircraft or
any other means of transport for private use;

if the export outside of the territory of the EU is confirmed by the Customs Office specified in the customs

law.

In order to prove the export of the goods, the supplier must collect - before the deadline to file the VAT

return for the period in which the export took place - a document confirming the export of the goods outside
the EU. In case of indirect export, the customs documents must confirm the identity of the goods supplied.
Evidence confirming the export that is accepted includes an electronic customs communication, generated

by the customs authorities’ tele-information system or a printout from this system which is confirmed by the
customs authorities, If the electronic communication is not directly delivered from the customs authorities’
tele-information system, its authenticity must be ensured. If the customs declaration is not filed electronically,
a paper version of the customs declaration must be confirmed by the customs authorities.

If the evidence is not collected before the VAT return for the period in which the export took place must

be filed, the supplier has the right (o wait with declaration of the export one more period if it possesses
confirmation that the customs export procedure has been commenced. However, if the proofs are still not
collected before the VAT return over the next period must be filed, the supplier is obliged to charge VAT on
the supply in the VAT return for the next period. If documents are collected later, the supplier has the right to
report the export and adjust output VAT in the period the export confirmation was received.

7.

Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Poland has not implemented any simplifications related to the supply of goods via a VAT warehouse.
7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

There is no VAT simplification to the supply of the goods in an excise warehouse.

7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse

Article 160 of the VAT Directive has been implemented in the VAT law in a way that the supply of goods in a

bonded warehouse is subject to the 0% VAT rate, under the following conditions:

+ the goods in the customs warehouse are moved into the customs bonded warehouse from an EU MS other
than Poland;

460 | Poland

--- Page 101 ---
+ the goods are covered by the customs warehousing procedure under condition that:
+ the goods are supplied to the taxable person;
+ the goods are under customs control and are included in the goods’ registers provided under
prescribed regulations; and
+ the goods under customs control, other than under the customs warehousing procedure, are not moved
outside the customs bonded warehouse as a result of the supply or after the supply.

72 Exemptions without the right to deduction

Polish VAT law provides for several exemptions which are regulated in the VAT Act as well as in the Decree
of the Minister of Finance dated 20 December 2013.

Exemption in the public interest
Exempt from VAT are among others, the following activities:
+ medical services;

+ educational services; and

+ certain postal services.

Other exemptions

Exempt from VAT are among others the following activities:

+ the supply of goods which were used exclusively for exempt activities if the taxable person did not have
the right to deduct input VAT;

+ financial and insurance services;

+ rental of apartments exclusively for residential use: and

+ tenancy of ground for agricultural purposes.

7.3. Option for taxation

As regards the supply of immovable properties, the Polish VAT law exempts:
the supply of undeveloped plots of ground other than those destined to be developed;

* the supply of buildings or structures or parts of them, except if the supply is made before the first
occupancy or less than 2 years have elapsed after the first occupancy. The taxable person has the option to
tax such supply under the following conditions
+ the supplier and the customer are registered for VAT as active taxable persons;

+ before the supply the supplier and the customer file to the tax authorities relevant for the customer,
a statement that they opt for taxation of the building, structure or part thereof. This statement should.
include the following information:

+ the names, addresses and VAT ID numbers of the supplier and the customer;
+ planned date of the supply of the building, structure or part thereof; and
+ the address of the building, structure or part thereof.

The supply of buildings other than described above, are also exempted under the following conditions:

+ the taxable person had no right to deduct input VAT at the moment of acquisition/construction of the
buildings, structures, or parts thereof; and

+ ifthe buildings, structures or parts thereof have not been modernized by the taxable person or the value
of expenses (ftom which the taxable person had the right to deduct input VAT) of modernization was not
higher than 30% of the initial value of the building, structure or part thereof.

The possibility to opt for taxation of financial services has not been introduced.

8.1 Reverse charges

‘There are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B services
provided by non-resident suppliers to the taxable persons and legal entities with VAT EU Id. No. established

Poland | 461

--- Page 102 ---
in Poland. This reverse charge applies in all EU MS and follows from Art. 44 and Art, 196 EU VAT Directive.
There is also a reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity and the transfer of Co, emission
rights. (Article 199 EU VAT Directive),

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

In the case of supply the goods (which a few exceptions mentioned below) the reverse charge applies under
the following conditions:
+ the supplier:
+ does not have its registered office or a fixed establishment in Poland that intervenes in the transaction;
and
+ is not registered for VAT purposes in Poland; and
«the customer:
+ is a Polish taxable person who has its registered office or a fixed establishment in Poland; or
+ isa legal entity not being a taxable person but having its registered office in Poland and is registered
for intra~Commumity transactions in Poland, i.e., has VAT EU Id. No.; and
+ the supply is not made within the framework of the intra-Community distance sales of goods or distance
sales of imported goods (Import One Stop Shop (IOSS)).

According to the above rules, the reverse charge does not apply if'a non-resident supplier of goods is
registered for VAT in Poland.

The reference ‘odwrotne obcigzenie’ meaning the reverse charge, is required on the invoice.

In the case of supply of gas or energy via a distribution system, the reverse charge applies if the supplier
does not have its registered office or a fixed establishment in Poland that intervenes in the transaction and the
customer is registered for VAT in Poland or for VAT EU transactions in Poland and the supply is not made
within the framework of the intra~Community distance sales of goods or sales of distance sales of imported
goods (Import One Stop Shop (IOSS)).

The reference ‘odwrotne obcigzenie” meaning the reverse charge, is required on the invoice.

In the case of transfer of an SPY (if the place of supply of the goods to which the SPV refers is Poland) the
reverse charge applies if the supplier does not have its registered office or a fixed establishment in Poland
that intervenes in the transaction and the customer is a taxable person or a non-taxable legal person and the
supply is not made within the framework of the intra-Community distance sales of goods or sales of distance
imported goods (Import One Stop Shop (IOSS)).

The reference *odwrotne obciazenie’ meaning the reverse charge, is required on the invoice.

In the case of supply of services (other than B2B-services and a with a few others) the reverse charge is
applicable, on the following conditions:
+ the supplier;
+ does not have its registered office or an intervening fixed establishment in Poland; and
+ the customer:
+ isa taxable person within the meaning of Polish Jaw having in Poland its registered office or a fixed
establishment taking part in the transaction; or
+ isa legal entity not being a taxable person but having its registered office in Poland and registered or
required to register for intra~-Community transactions in Poland, i.e., has a Polish VAT Id. No.

The reference ‘odwrotne obciazenie’ meaning the reverse charge, is required on the invoice.

The reverse charge on the supply of services connected to an immovable property, is applicable on the
following conditions:
+ the supplier:

+ does not have its registered office or an intervening fixed establishment in Poland;

+ is not registered for VAT in Poland, and

--- Page 103 ---
+ the customer:
+ _ isa taxable person within the meaning of Polish law having a registered office or a fixed
establishment in Poland; or
+ is a legal entity, not being a taxable person but being registered or required to register for intra-

‘The reference ‘odwrotne obciqzenie” meaning the reverse charge, is required on the invoice.

In the case of transfer of an SPV (if the place of supply of the service to which SPV refers is Poland) the
reverse charge applies if if the supplier does not have its registered office or a fixed establishment in Poland

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

Poland has not implemented specific domestic reverse charges apart from the reverse charges mentioned in
paragraph 8.1.2

8.2 Joint and several liability

There is joint and several liability of the customer together with the supplier for VAT due on the supply of the
goods mentioned in the Annex No 15 the VAT Act (i.e. the same goods to which the obligatory split payment
apply) if at the time of making the supply, the customer knew or had reasonable grounds to assume that the
supplier will not pay the VAT due to the tax office. The taxpayer has reasonable grounds to assume that the
entire amount of VAT attributable to the delivery of goods made to him, or part thereof, would not be paid if
the circumstances surrounding the delivery of goods or the conditions under which it was made differs from
the circumstances or conditions usually occurring in trade in such goods, in particular if the price for the
goods delivered to the taxpayer was, without economic justification, lower than their market value.

‘There is an exception for:

+ purchases made at fuel stations or liquefied gas stations, into standard tanks of vehicles used by taxpayers
purchasing these goods, to power these vehicles;

+ the supply of goods made by a taxpayer supplying gas through its own transmission or distribution
networks;

+ ifthe occurrence of tax arrears was not related to the participation of the supplier in an unreliable tax
settlement to obtain financial benefits;

+ for the purchase of goods documented by an invoice in which the total amount due exceeds PLN 15,000
(approximately EUR 3,500) or its equivalent expressed in a foreign currency:

* to purchase goods for which the taxpayer paid using the split payment mechanism. As mentioned, Poland
implemented a specific joint and several liability for the VAT due on the goods mentioned in the Annex
no 15 to the VAT Act (for which the obligatory split payment rules apply). Besides this liability, there
is a general joint and several liability for the VAT due on the supply of goods and services (the value of
the supply must be higher than PLN 15,000 (approx. EUR 3,500), if the customer pays the supplier on
a bank account that is not listed on the so-called ‘whitelist of taxable persons’. The whitelist of taxable
persons is a register of all taxable persons registered for VAT in Poland. The whitelist is kept by the Polish
tax administration and is publicly available for everybody interested. The whitelist is available under the
following link https://www.podatki.gov.pl/wykaz-podatnikow-vat-wyszukiwarka. The joint and several
liability is excluded if the customer pays for the invoice to the supplier with so-called spilt payment. The
joint and several liability does not apply also to invoices relating to the intra~Community acquisition of
the goods, acquisition of the goods and services to which the reverse charge applies.

Poland | 463

--- Page 104 ---
9. Recovery of input VAT

9.1 Non-recoverable input VAT

‘The input VAI’ is not recoverable in case of:

+ accommodation and restaurant services (except the ready-for-consumption meals served by companies
transporting passengers or if the accommodation services are acquired by the taxable person to re-invoice
them);

+ the invoice is issued by a non-existent entity;

+ the invoice is issued for a VAT exempt transaction or for a non-taxable VAT transaction;

+ the invoice is issued for a transaction which has not been performed, indicates amounts not corresponding
to the facts, proves transactions which are invalid or the purpose of which is to circumvent provisions of
civil law, ie. pretense activities;

+ the invoices and correction invoices issued by the customer have not been accepted by the supplier;

the invoice indicates VAT which should not have been indicated on the invoice; and

+ reporting VAT on the intra-Community acquisition of goods if the intra~Community acquisition of the
goods has been reported under a Polish VAT identification number, but the goods are delivered to a
different EU Member State than Poland.

Poland implemented limitations of deduction to 50% of input VAT related to the acquisition of passenger

cars and petrol, gas, diesel fuel, repair services and spare parts used for cars whereby the taxable person has a
limited right to dedact input VAT. The limitation on deduction of input VAT does not apply if the car is used
exclusively for business activities or is designed for 10 passengers including the driver.

If cars are used exclusively for a business activity, the taxable person is entitled to deduct 100% of input VAT

under the following conditions:

+ the construction of the car excludes the possibility of using the car for other than business purposes;

+ the internal rules relating to using the car within the taxable person’s business (an instruction) ensure that
the car may only be used for business purposes;

+ the taxable person keeps detailed evidence of the car’s mileage; and

+ the taxable person informs the tax office about its intention to use the car exclusively for business
purposes within 7 days from the date of incurring the very first expense related to this car.

The limitation in deduction of input VAT does not apply to cars which are intended for re-sale or lease/rent,
etc.

In the case of an intra-Community acquisition of goods, the input VAT becomes deductible in the month in
which the taxable event occurs under condition that the taxable persons settle output VAT for that period.

92 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT can be refunded to the bank account of the taxable person. However, it must be remitted on
the bank account of a bank established in Poland (refunds made to foreign bank accounts are not possible) or,
alternatively, can be carried forward to the VAT returns for subsequent periods. It is up to the taxable person
how the excess of input VAT is settled, i.e., the structure of SAFT includes special fields in which the taxable
person asks for a VAT refund or transfers VAT to future VAT settlements.

If the taxable person asks for the VAT refund on his bank account, the tax authorities must pay the refund

within the following time limits:

+ 60 days from the filing date of the VAT return in which the VAT refund is reported; or

+ 180 days, if there was no taxable activity on the territory of Poland and no supply of goods or services
outside Poland on which VAT would have been deductible if it had been made in Poland.

If certain additional conditions are met, the above time limits for the VAT refund can be shortened in the
following ways:

+ from 180 days to 60 days in the case of the provision of special security to the tax authorities; or

464 | Poland

--- Page 105 ---
+ from 60 days to 40 days in case of issuance of only structured invoices or 25 days in case all amounts
charged on the purchase invoices by the suppliers were paid and reported in the VAT return.

If the tax authorities have doubts that the VAT refiund is justified, they can prolong the time limit for the
refund. The VAT law does not specify for how long the VAT refund can be prolonged. The law states,
however, that if prolongation is not justified, the tax authorities are required to refund VAT with interest

93 Adjustment periods

Poland implemented an obligation to adjust the input VAT deducted the acquisition of goods or services
which became fixed assets including ground, perpetual usufruct and intangible assets, if their initial value is
higher than PLN 15,000 (approximately EUR 3,575).

The adjustment period is:
+ immovable property, ground, perpetual usuftuct - 10 years following the year of first use;
+ movable property, intangible assets - 5 years following the year of first use.

‘The application for a VAT refund for non-Polish taxable persons but established in one of the other EU MS,
should be filed electronically in the EU MS in which the taxable person is established. The relevant tax
administration subsequently transfers the electronic application for the VAT refund to the Polish tax office
responsible for making the VAT refund.

The following tax office is responsible for dealing with the application for the VAT refund:

Jagiellonska St. 15

03-719 Warsaw

Poland

‘The application for the VAT refund should be filed by 30 September of the year following the year for which
the taxable person applies for the VAT refund. As a rule, it is not possible to file the application successfully
after the above-mentioned deadline. The taxable person may apply for the VAT refund over a period not
shorter than 3 calendar months and not longer than 1 calendar year. The minimum amount of the VAT refund
is EUR 400 for an application filed for a period shorter than 1 year but not shorter than 3 months. EUR 50 for
an application filed for a period of an entire year or a period shorter than 3 last months of a calendar year.

The taxable person is required to attach to its electronic application, copies of the invoices and customs
documents in which the tax base exceeds EUR 1,000, or EUR 250 for purchases of fuel.

the moment of receiving the application. If the tax authorities have not received all required documents/
information to issue the decision, they are entitled to ask the taxable person for additional explanations or
documents, The taxable person has | month to provide these additional explanations/documents to the tax
authorities.

If the tax authorities request additional data, VAT refund should be made within:

* 6 months from the date of receiving the application for the VAT refund if the tax authorities ask for
additional explanations/documents; and

« 8 months from the date of receiving the application for the VAT refund if the tax authorities ask for

additional explanations/documents twice.

The money should be refunded on the taxable person’s bank account within 10 days from the date of issue of
the decision on the VAT refund.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoices content

The VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ the date of issue;

+ the sequential number, based on one or more series, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

+ the name and surname or business name of the supplier and the customer and their addresses;

+ the supplier’s VAT number,

+ the supplier's VAT identification number with country prefix “PL” in the case of intra-Community
supplies of the goods and in the case of intra-Community supply of services to which Art. 44 EU VAT
Directive applies;

+ the customer’s VAT identification number, under which the customer received the goods or services;

+ the customer’s VAT identification number with the country prefix valid for the relevant BU Member
State under which the customer received the intra~-Community services to which Art. 44 of the EU VAT
Directive applies or in case of the intra-Community acquisition of goods;

+ the date on which the supply of goods was made and/or the services were completed as far as that date
can be determined and differs from the date of issuance of the invoice;

+ the date of receiving the prepayment for the supply of goods or services as far as that date can be
determined and differs from the date of issue of the invoice except for an intra~Community supply of
goods:

+ the nature/description of the goods or services;

« the measure and quantity of the goods supplied or the scope of the services rendered;

+ _ the unit price of goods or services exclusive of VAT (unit net price);

+ any discounts or rebates, including the rebates granted on account of earlier payment, if they are not
included in the unit price;

+ the value of the goods supplied or services rendered covered by the transaction without the amount of the
VAT (net value of sale);

+ the applied VAT rate;

+ the sums of the net values of the goods or services grouped by their applicable VAT rates or exemptions
from VAT;

+ the total VAT amount per VAT rate;

+ the total amount due:

+ in the case of cash accounting corresponding reference: “metoda kasowa”;

+ the reference “odwrotne obciqzenie” meaning the reverse charge — in case of invoices documenting
transactions in which the customer is the entity obliged to settle the VAT;

+ incase of self-billing corresponding reference: “samofakturowanie”;

+ the reference “procedura marzy dla biur podrézy” in case of the margin scheme for travel agents;

+ the reference “procedura marzy — towary uzywane” in case of the margin scheme for second-hand goods;

+ the reference “procedura marzy — dziela sztuki” in case of the margin scheme for works of art;

+ the reference “procedura marzy — przedmioty kolekcjonerskie i antyki” in case of the margin scheme for
collector’s items and antiques;

+ incase of a supply which is VAT exempt, the reference either to the Polish VAT provisions or the VAT
Directive or any other legal basis indicating that the supply is VAT exempt;

* where the invoice is issued on behalf of a debtor by an enforcement authority or a court executive officer,
the name and address of the executive authority or the court executive officer and name and address of the
debtor;

* the name, address, and VAT identification number of the fiscal representative (if the invoice is issued by
the fiscal representative);

* — in case of an intra~-Community supply of a new means of transport, the first entry into service of the new
means of transport and the information necessary to determine whether the means of transport is new:

* the reference “mechanizm podzielonej ptatnosci” in case of domestic supplies of goods or services
mentioned in the Annex no 15 to the VAT Act which value is higher than PLN 15,000.00 (approximately
EUR 3,500), ie. the invoices for which the payment must be made with the split payment; and

Poland | 467

--- Page 108 ---
+ incase of an intra-Community triangular transaction (simplification), when the invoice is issued by the
second taxable person (party B):
+ the note: “VAT: Faktura WE uproszezona na mocy art, 135-138 ustawy o ptu” or “VAT: Faktura WE
uproszezona na mocy artykutu 141 dyrektywy 2006/112/WE”;
+ the indication that the VAT will be settled by the third taxable person;
+ the VAT identification number of the second taxable person; and
+ the VAT identification number of the third taxable person.

10.1.2 Time limits

As a rule, the invoice should be issued no later than the 15" day of the month following the month in which
the supply took place (delivery of goods or provision of services or receiving the prepayment). At the same
time the invoice cannot be issued earlier than 60 days before making the supply of the goods or completion of
the service or receiving the prepayment for the future supply. There are a few exceptions from this principal
rule relating to certain types of services.

10.1.3 Curreney

Polish currency is called zloty, i.e., “PLN”. The invoice can be issued in any currency, i.e., there is no
obligation to issue it in PLN. There is only the obligation to show on the invoice the amount of output VAT in
PLN. So, the amount on the invoice can be issued for example in EUR. But the VAT amount must be shown
in PLN separately.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

If the invoice is expressed in a foreign currency (i.e. not in PLN) conversion for into PLN should be made
based on the average exchange rate published by the Polish National Bank valid for the last business day
preceding the day in which the tax point arose,

The taxable person may also apply the exchange rate published by the European Central Bank. In such a
case it is necessary to use the exchange rate published by this bank on the last business day preceding the
day in which the tax point arose. Currencies other than EUR should first be re-calculated into EUR and next
recalculated from EUR into PLN.

If the invoice is issued before the tax point, it is necessary to convert it into PLN based on the average
exchange rate published by the Polish National Bank or the European Central Bank valid on the last business
day preceding the day of issuance of the invoice.

In the case of import, the amounts should be converted into PLN based on the customs exchange rates.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Self-billing is allowed on condition that:

* there is a prior agreement between the 2 parties; and

+ there is a procedure specified for the approval of individual invoices by the taxable person performing the
supply.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

If the total value of the invoice is not higher than PLN 450 or EUR 100, the taxable person may issue a

simplified invoice. The simplified invoice should include the following information:

+ the date of issue;

+ the sequential number, based on one or more series, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

+ the name and surname or business name of the supplier;

+ the supplier’s VAT identification number;

+ the supplier’s VAT identification number with country prefix “PL” in the case of intra~Community
supplies of the goods and in the case of intra~Community supply of services on which Art. 28b of VAT
Act (ie., Art. 44 EU VAT Directive) applies;

* the customer’s VAT identification number, under which the customer received the goods or services;

+ the customer’s VAT identification number with the country prefix valid for relevant EU Member States
under which the customer received the intra~Community services to which Art. 44 EU VAT Directive
applies or in case of the intra~Community acquisition of goods;

468 | Poland

--- Page 109 ---
+ the date on which the supply of goods was made and/or the services were completed as far as that date
can be determined and differs from the date of issuance of the invoice;

+ the date of receiving the prepayment for the supply of goods or services except for an intra-Community
supply of goods, as far as that date can be determined and differs from the date of issue of the invoice;

+ the nature/description of the goods or services;

* any discounts or rebates, including the rebates granted on account of earlier payment, if they are not
included in the unit price;

«the total amount due; and

+ under condition that the simplified invoice includes all information needed to determine the VAT rates
and, based on these VAT rates, the VAT amounts.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

The taxable person is required to store all VAT records, including invoices, until the lapse of the assessment
period for VAT, i.e. 5 years from the end of the year in which the VAT was payable to the tax authorities.
‘There is an exception for taxable persons supplying telecommunication, broadcasting and electronic services
which are taxed based on the special scheme referred to in the Articles 3584-369 and 369a-369k of the EU
VAT Directive. In that case documents must be stored for 10 years from the end of the year in which the
services are completed.

Structured invoices issued via KSeF should be stored for 10 years counted from the end of the year in which
they have been issued.

Stored invoices should be divided into settlement periods so that they are easy to find and the authenticity

of origin, integrity of content and readability must be guaranteed during the whole VAT assessment period.
Taxable persons established in Poland must store the invoices on the territory of Poland unless they are stored
electronically in a way to allow the tax authorities online access to them.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

‘The use of an electronic invoice is subject to acceptance by the customer, The taxable person may choose any
format for the electronic invoicing if the authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability of such
invoices are guaranteed.

As of 1 January 2022, taxpayers can voluntarily issue structured invoices via KSeF (i.e. Country System of
Electronic Invoices). Issuance of structured invoices require acceptance by the customer. As of 1 February
2026 issuing structured invoices will become obligatory for the taxpayers whose turnover exceeded PLN
200 million (approximately EUR 47 million) in the previous year and from 1 April 2026 for other taxpayers.
Issuance of the structured invoices via KSeF will be obligatory for taxpayers established in Poland.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

All taxable persons are required to file SAF-T returns. The periodical SAF-T consists of 2 parts, i.e. the
declaration part and the registers part. In the declaration part of SAF-T, the taxable person provides summary
data about sales and purchases realized in the settlement period. In the registers part of SAF-T, the taxable
person reports sales and purchases invoices one by one

SAF-T returns should be filed monthly.

So-called ‘smal! taxable persons’ (i.¢., whose annual turnover is lower than EUR 2,000,000 including VAT/
or EUR 45,000 in the case of so-called agents) who opt for so-called cash accounting,’ file SAF-Ts ona
quarterly basis,

“Small taxable persons’ who do not opt for ‘cash accounting can choose for a quarterly filing of SAF-T’s.

Quarterly SAF-T filing is also available for taxable persons who opt for paying CIT based on a simplification
called ‘Estonian CIT” if the value of its annual turnover for the previous year is lower than EUR 4,000,000.

Poland | 469

--- Page 110 ---
Quarterly SAF-T filing is not available at all for:

+ newly registered taxable person, i.e., for the first 12 months from the date of registration;

+ taxable persons who in the given quarter or in the last four quarters supplied goods for which split
payment applies obligatory, unless the net value of the goods was lower than PLN 50,000 (approximately
EUR 11,600) in each month of these quarters; and

+ who settles import VAT using so called ‘postponed accounting’ scheme.

There is no difference in the methodology of filing between taxable persons established and not established in

Poland; all taxable persons must follow the same rules.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

Both resident and non-resident taxable persons must file and pay VAT due within the same time limits. All
taxable persons are obliged to file SAF-T electronically to the tax office.

In case of monthly filing, SAF-T called JPK_V7M should be filed and the VAT due should be paid by the 25
day of the following month

In case of quarterly filing, for the first 2 months of the quarter, the taxable person files SAF-T called JPK.
V7K in part relating to registers. The deadline for filing is always the 25" day of the following month. For
the last month of the quarter, the taxable person also files a JPK_V7K but in the part related to registers, the
taxable person provides the data for the last month of the quarter but in the declaration part he provides the
data for the entire quarter,

If the taxable person detects a mistake in a filed SAF-T in the part relating to registers or changes the data in
this register, he is required to file to the tax office the updated version of SAF-T within 14 days.

The same applies, ic., the taxable person must file an updated version of SAF-T within 14 days, if the tax
office detects an irregularity or a mistake in SAF-T. The obligation to file the updated version of SAF-T
appears on Lhe request of the lax authorilies if the authorities are nol able Lo verify the correctness of the
reported transactions in SAF-T due to mistakes or irregularities in it. In that case the tax office sends a written
notification (o the taxable person with a request for the correction of SAF-T. Depending on the situation, the
taxable person files the correction of SAF-T or provides an explanation that the filed SAF-T is correct and
justifies this standpoint. If the taxable person does not respond to the request of the tax office, a fine is issued
for PLN 500 (approximately EUR 116) for each detected mistake.

If the taxable person is required to file VAT returns electronically, it must file a special authorization for the
person who will send VAT returns to the tax authorities electronically. The authorization is called “UPL-1”.

10.2.3. European sales listing
Poland has not implemented any thresholds to file the European sales listing (the form called: VAT-EU
and VAT-EUK). Hence, the obligations arise as soon as the taxable person registered for intra-Community
transactions starts performing the following activities:

+ intra-Commumity transfer and supply of goods;

+ intra-Community acquisition of goods;

+ supplies of goods on which the simplified triangulation rules apply;

+ intra-Community supply of services referred to in Article 44 of the EU VAT Directive; or

+ transfer of goods from Potand under the call-off stock simplification.

‘The European sales (and purchase) listings should be filed electronically monthly by the 25" day of the
following month.

‘The layouts of the EU sales and purchases listing (VAT-EU and VAT-EUK) are published on the website:
Attps://tinyurl.com/Sn86sz2v

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
There are no national recapitulative statements in Poland.

470 | Poland

--- Page 111 ---
10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period is 5 years calculated from the end of the year in which the VAT should have been
paid.

10.2.6 SAFT reporting

Apart of forms JPK_VAT7M and JPK_VAT7K, the Ministry of Finance has published the following structures
of SAF-T’s which may relate to VAT settlements:

+ JPK_FA~ ie. SAF-T file relating to invoices;

+ JPK_MAG - ie. SAF-T file relating to warehouses;

+ JPK_WB ~ ie. SAF-T file relating to bank accounts;

Depending on the settlement period chosen by the taxable person, the JPK_VAT7M or JPK_VATTK is
required to be filed to the tax office, without any request by the tax authorities, within the time limits resulting
from the VAT Act.

The other SAF-T’s, i.e, JPK_FA, JPK MAG, JPK_Bank are to be filed at the request of the tax authorities.

10.2.7 Split payment

Poland obtained the Decision (i.e. Council Implementing Decision (EU) 2022/559 of 5 April 2022 authorizing
Poland to introduce a special measure derogating from the Article 226 of Directive 2006/112/EC on the
common system of value added tax) authorizing Poland to implement the obligatory split payment for certain
goods and services listed in this Decision.

Based on the above Decision, Poland implemented the so-called ‘voluntary split payment’ and ‘obligatory
split payment’, The split payment means that the customer pays for the invoice with a so-called split where
the net amount goes on the supplier’s business bank account and the VAT amount goes on the supplier's VAT
bank account. The banks operating in Poland are obliged, among others, to:

+ open a VAT account for the business bank accounts of the taxable persons; and
+ create a special layout of the bank transfer which allows for the payment of invoices to be made with a
split payment.

The taxable persons who do not have an establishment or a fixed establishment in Poland, who will carry out
supplies covered by the obligatory split payment, are required to open a bank account in a bank established
in Poland, The costs of maintenance of this bank account can be refunded by the Polish tax office to foreign
companies registered for VAT in Poland.

Money collected by the supplier on his VAT account can be used to pay for:
* the VAT to the tax authorities;

+ the VAT to the suppliers;

* corporate income or personal income tax;

+ excise tax:

* customs duties; and

* social security liabilities.

The taxable person can also ask the authorities to refund the money kept on the VAT account.

The split payment may be applied by the customer to pay the supplier for all invoices which cover VAT.
However, in case of certain goods and services recognized as “sensitive”, the customer must pay for the
invoice when the value is higher than PLN 15,000. (approximately EUR 3,500) gross with the split payment.
The list of goods and services to which the split payment must be applied obligatory, is covered by the Annex
no 15 of the VAT Act, The Annex covers the list of 150 items of goods and services, for example: ferro-alloys,
non ~alloys steel, copper, investment gold, electronic integrated circuits, laptops and notebooks, mobile
phones, video game consoles (such as those used with a television set or a stand-alone screen) and other
gaming or gambling apparatus with electronic display - excluding parts and accessories, waste materials,
construction services, spare parts and accessories to cars.

Poland 1 471

--- Page 112 ---
The invoices documenting transactions covered by the obligatory split payment must contain the following
clause in Polish ,.mechanizm podzielonej platnosci” (in English “split payment mechanism”). If the invoices
do not contain this note, the taxable person will be charged with a penalty sanction equal to 30% of the VAT
indicated on the invoice. The sanction is not imposed if the customer pays with the split payment, despite
theomission of the clause in Polish on the invoice. Moreover, the net amount of the invoices could not be
treated as tax deductible for respectively CIT or PIT purposes.

‘The payment for the supply of the goods via the split payment exempts also the customer from joint and
several liability for VAT due on the supplied goods.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Penalty interest

If VAT due to the tax office is not paid by the taxable person on time (or at all) or the taxable person claims
and receives an amount of VAT refund more than what he is entitled to, he must pay this VAT back to the tax
office together with a penalty interest. The rate is 14.5% per year. The percentage of interest varies in time,
hence the percentage of interest is not fixed as such.

There is an exemption from the obligation to pay the penalty interest if the overdue amount is lower than PLN
8.70 (approximately EUR 2).

The rate of the penalty interest may be decreased to 50% (i.e, 7.25%) or increased up to 150% (i.e. 21.75%)
of the basic rate (i.e. 14.5%).

The decreased 50% rate of penalty interest applies if the taxable person:

+ files a valid correction of the VAT return within 6 months from the initial time limit for filing that VAT
return to the tax office; and

+ pays the VAT due within 7 days after filing the correction VAT return to the tax office.

However, the decreased 50% penalty interest cannot be applied if the taxable person files his correction VAT

retum:

+ after receiving a notice of intention to initiate a tax audit, and where the notice does not apply - after the
closing of a tax audit;

+ after the taxable person received an authorization to start a customs-fiscal inspection;

«asa result of the supervision of the VAT return by the tax office.

The increased 150% penalty interest applies, if:

+ the tax authorities detect during the tax audit or customs-fiscal inspection or tax litigation that the taxable
person has diminished the VAT due or overestimated an overpayment of VAT or of a VAT refund;

«the taxable person files its corrected VAT return;

+ _ after receiving the notice of intention to initiate a tax audit, and where the notice does not apply - afler the
closing of a tax audit;

+ after receiving the authorization to start a customs-fiscal inspection; and

+ asa result of the supervision of the VAT return by the tax office.

if the incorrectly reported VAT is higher than 25% of the VAT due and is lower than the amount being

equivalent of 5 times the statutory minimum salary in Poland valid on the day following the date of the VAT

payment or refund.

Additionally the increased 150% penalty interest applies if non filing of the tax return has been detected
by the tax authorities in the result of the tax audit, customs ~ fiscal inspection, supervision activities or tax
litigation.

The increased penalty interest does not apply if additional VAT penalty sanction applies.

10.3.2 Penalty sanetion
The VAT Act provides with a few types of penalty sanctions, i.e., up to 15 %, up to 20%, up to 30% and 100%.

472 | Poland

--- Page 113 ---
‘The sanction up to 30% to applies if the tax office detects that the taxable person reported in his filed VAT

return:

+ an amount of VAT due to the tax office lower than should have been reported;

+ an amount of VAT refund higher than should have been reported;

+ an amount of VAT carried forward to the VAT retum for the following periods is higher than should have
been reported;

+ an amount of VAT refund or VAT carried forward when VAT is due to the tax office; and

+ did not file a VAT return and did not pay VAT due to the tax office.

The sanction is calculated up to 30% of underestimated VAT due to the tax office or overestimated VAT
refund or VAT carried forward depending on the circumstances of the case.

The sanction up to 30% shall be decreased by the tax office up to a 20% sanction, if after the tax inspection
the taxable person:

+ corrects irregularities detected by the tax office; and

+ files overdue VAT returns and pays overdue VAT to the tax office.

The sanction up to 15% shall apply if the taxable persons after receiving the notification from the tax and
customs authorities about incoming customs ~ fiscal inspection, files within 14 days a correction of VAT
return and pays the overdue VAT as a result thereof.

While the amount of penalty sanction is established by the tax authorities they shall consider:

+ the circumstances of the irregularity;

+ the type and degree of breach of the taxpayer's obligation, which resulted in the irregularity;

+ the type, degree and frequency of irregularities identified so far concerning non-statutory tax liabilities;

* the amount of irregularities identified, including the amount of understatement of tax liability, the amount
of overstatement of the tax difference of refund, the refund of input tax or the tax difference to reduce the
tax due for the next settlement periods; and

* actions taken by the taxpayer after the irregularity was identified to eliminate the effects of the
irregularity.

The penalty sanctions do not apply if, before initiation of a tax audit or a customs-fiscal inspection, the

taxpayer:

* submitted correction of VAT return; or

* submitted correction of VAT return with the tax amounts shown and paid the amount resulting from the
submitted retum or correction of the tax return together with default interest to the tax office.

The penalty sanctions also do not apply if irregularity results from:

* arithmetic errors or obvious mistakes made in the declaration; and

+ failure to include the tax due or input tax in the settlement for a given settlement period, and the tax due
or input tax was included in previous settlement periods or in periods following the relevant settlement
period, if this occurred before the date of initiation of a tax audit or a customs and fiscal audit.

The 100% penalty sanction applies to the deduction of input VAT resulting from invoices:

+ issued by a non-existent entity;

+ — for activities that have not been performed:

+ that show amounts inconsistent with reality; and

+ _ if the tax office proves that the transactions were invalid or the purpose of which is to circumvent the
provisions of civil law, i.e. pretence activities.

10.3.3 Offence penalties
According to the Fiscal Penal Code, the penal responsibility effects any individual person who is responsible
for conducting the VAT settlement of the taxable person and/or sign tax returns. Hence, the fine resulting
from the Fiscal Penal Code is not directed to the company/the taxable person as such, but to its employees,
Members of the Board, VAT Managers, etc. Such person may avoid the fine if voluntary disclosure is

filed to the tax authorities. The voluntary disclosure is effective only if it is submitted to the tax authorities
before the tax authorities learn about the violation of the tax law. Additionally, if the irregularity results in

Poland | 473

--- Page 114 ---
overdue VAT, it should be paid to the tax authorities by the taxable person together with the penalty interest.
It is necessary to emphasise that the tax office has limited rights to issue fines for small offences of the tax
law. The tax office cannot issue a fine higher than PLN 23,330 (approximately EUR 5,500). If the case is
more serious, then the tax office does not handle the case and it falls under the jurisdiction of the criminal
courts, Relating to serious crimes with tax affairs, the court may issue very substantial sanctions, including
imprisonment.

10.3.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

If the taxable person does not register for VAT on time (i.e. before making his very first activity subject to
VAT) the person responsible may receive a fine for the tax offence. The tax office may issue a fine up to PLN
23,330 (approximately EUR 5,500). If the case is more serious and the offence of VAT law leads to avoidance
of VAT, the tax office is not competent to issue the fine. In such a case the tax authorities will direct the case
to the competence of the criminal court. The criminal court may issue different types of penalties such as a
financial penalty, restriction of personal liberty, or imprisonment.

10.3.3.2, Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Ifa VAT return or European sales listing is not filed on time, the tax authorities may issue a penalty to the
person responsible for not keeping to the time limit for filing. The penalty, issued by the tax office, cannot be
higher than PLN 23,330 (approximately EUR 5,500). The tax authorities’ right to issue penalties is limited
to minor offences of the tax law. If the case is more serious and the offence of VAT law leads to avoidance
of VAT, the tax office is not competent to issue the fine. In such a case the tax authorities will direct the case
to the competence of the criminal court. The criminal court may issue different types of penalties such as
financial penalty, restriction of personal liberty, or imprisonment.

10.3.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

Regardless of the above, the tax authorities may also impose a fine on the person responsible for not paying
VAT on time. The penalty, issued by the tax office, cannot be higher than PLN 23,330 (approximately EUR
5,500). The tax authorities’ right to issue fines is limited to minor offences of the tax law. If the case is more
serious and the offence of VAT law leads to avoidance of VAT, the tax office is not competent to issue the fine.
In such a case the tax authorities will direct the case to the competence of the criminal court. The criminal
court may issue different types of penalties such as a financial penalty, restriction of personal liberty, or
imprisonment.

10.3.3.4 Penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If VAT has not been reported correctly, the tax authorities may issue a fine of up to PLN 23,330
{approximately EUR 5,500) to the person responsible. This is because the tax authorities’ right to issue

fines is limited to minor offences of the tax law. However, in the case of more serious offences, such as tax
avoidance, tax evasion or tax fraud, the tax authorities are not competent, and the case is judged by the court.
Depending on the type of the irregularity, the court may issue a monetary fine to the person responsible which
may be very high or even decide on imprisonment.

10.4 Intrastat

Intrastat should be filed in respect to:
+ arrivals of goods to Poland;
+ dispatches of goods from Poland.

Liable to file Intrastat is a natural person or a legal person, as well as an organizational unit without legal
personality, participating in trade in goods within the EU and obliged to provide information for the purposes
of records and statistics on trade in goods within the EU, in accordance with the EU regulations governing
statistics on trade in goods between the Member States of the EU, the provisions on public statistics and the
provisions of the customs Jaw or its tax representative within the meaning of the provisions on the tax on
goods and services.

Intrastat should be filed by the 10" day of the month following the reference month. Intrastat must be filed
electronically via the customs portal called PUESC. The portal PUESC is kept by the Ministry of Finance
on the following web page: https.//tinyurl.com/Sn8wwyef. The Ministry of Finance published a special

--- Page 115 ---
program allowing to generate Intrastat electronically called IST@T. It can be downloaded directly from the
portal PUESC. Each taxable person liable to file the Intrastat returns should register himself in this portal and
subsequently file declarations via this portal.

Intrastat must be signed on behalf of taxable person by an individual person whose authorisation to act on
behalf of given taxable person is registered by the customs authorities as such.

Intrastat must be filed electronically and signed using:

+ aqualified electronic signature;

+ a signature confirmed by the trusted ePUAP profile; or

+ an advanced electronic signature verified by means of a customs certificate.

10.5 Intrastat threshold

The threshold for filing the basic Intrastat for arrivals is PLN 6,000,000 (approximately EUR 1,430,000) and
for dispatches PLN 2,800,000 (approximately EUR 670,000).

The threshold for filing the detailed Intrastat for arrivals is PLN 105,000,000 (approximately EUR
25,0000,000) and for dispatches PLN 158,000,000 (approximately EUR 37,620,000).

Portugal

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Portugal through the “Cd go do Imposto sobre
o Valor Acrescentado” (hereafter referred to as the “VAT Law”), approved by Decree Law 394-B/84, of 26
December, and through the “Regime do IVA nas Transagé s Intracomunitd ias” or “RITP'(VAT Regime on
intra-Community transactions), approved by Decree Law 290/92, of 28 December. VAT is referred to in
Portugal as “Imposto sobre o Valor Acrescentado” or “IVA”.

1. Taxable persons

1 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

As a rule, there is no registration threshold for domestic taxable persons who are required to register for VAT.
However, a person who, on an occasional basis, carries out a taxable transaction below the threshold of
EUR 25,001 does not have to register.

The registration is usually started at any Tax Office or at any other authorised location, prior to the
commencement of the activities. The following information must be provided for the registration:

+ — statement of the Commencement of the Activities duly signed by a director and by the accountant (if
applicable);

+ details of the account number, i.e. the International Bank Account Number (IBAN) of the legal entity:

+ online commercial certificate or certified copy of the entity’s incorporation deed;

* — certified copy of the resolution appointing the corporate bodies (if the documents mentioned above do not
identify the corporate bodies); and

+ copies of passports or identity cards of the company’s representatives and, if applicable, the company’s
accountant (“Técnico Oficial de Contas”).

‘The form of the Statement can be downloaded at the following link: hitps://tinyurl.com/S9beyfav.

111 General exemption criteria for small businesses

Small taxable persons are eligible for VAT exemption if they meet the following conditions:

+ they do not have, nor are they required to have, organized accounting for IRS (Personal Income Tax) or
IRC (Corporate Income Tax) purposes;

+ they do not engage in import, export or related activities:

+ they do not conduct activities involving the supply of goods or services listed in Annex E of the VAT Law
(corresponds to Annex VI of the EU VAT Directive);

+ their annual turnover in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 15,000.

1.1.2 Additional exemption criteria for small businesses

Despite the general criteria, the following taxable persons are also exempt from VAT:

+ those with an annual turnover exceeding EUR 10,000 but less than EUR 15,000, who would qualify for
the small retailers’ regime if taxed; and

+ those who did not exceed an annual turnover of EUR 15,000 in the previous calendar year and the three
preceding years, provided they meet the conditions outlined for the general exemption criteria,

For taxable persons who are starting their activity, the turnover to be considered is based on the forecast for
the current calendar year, subject to the confirmation by the Portuguese Tax Authority.

If the reference period for determining the turnover is less than a full calendar year, the turnover for that
period should be annualized to determine the corresponding annual turnover.

Portugal has not yet implemented rules under which access to the special exemption regime for domestic
SME will be extended (namely, rules under which SMEs established in Portugal will not be eligible for the
special exemption regime if they engage in exports or related activities, or if their annual turnover in the

previous calendar year exceeds EUR 15,000).
Portugal | 477

--- Page 118 ---
