Tel. / Fax: +351 213 401 180/213 401 209

As long as all documents referred to above are duly and timely provided, it is possible to complete the VAT
registration within one week; in most of the cases the VAT registration is concluded in | or 2 business days.
However, the taxable person will only be able to access his private area on the Portuguese Tax Authority’s
website (where VAT returns and other compliance obligations are submitted) after receiving a letter
containing the password for the login into that private area, The amount of time required for the password to
reach the taxable person’s address may vary according to the country of residence.

Portugal has not yet implemented rules under which EU SMEs will be eligible for exemption (namely, rules
under which EU SMEs will be eligible if their annual turnover in the EU does not exceed EUR 100,000 in the
previous calendar year or if this threshold is not exceeded by more than 25% in the current)

13 VAT grouping

VAT grouping is not possible in Portugal.

1.4 Fiscal representative

Appointment of a fiscal representative is voluntary for taxable persons from EU countries and mandatory for
taxable persons from non-EU countries.

The fiscal representative can be a company in Portugal or any other person or entity that is a VAT taxable
person in the Portuguese territory and has a power of attomey (signed by the non-resident entity) granting
enough powers to act as such.

478 | Portugal

--- Page 119 ---
‘The fiscal representative will be liable for all obligations inherent to the application of the Portuguese VAT
Law and is severally liable for the payment of the tax. There is no special tax advantage granted to the non-
resident taxable person for the simple fact of appointing a fiscal representative.

The non-resident taxable person can also grant powers to the fiscal representative to receive in his bank
account amounts arising from any VAT refunds,

‘Taxable persons from EU countries and non-EU countries are waived from the registration and appointment
of a fiscal representative when they perform solely supplies of goods listed in Annex C to the VAT Law
(similar to Annex V of the EU VAT Directive) exempt under the following procedures:

* placed in a temporary storage (after having been presented to the customs authorities);

+ placed in a free zone or a free warehouse;

* placed in a bonded warehouse or in an inward processing arrangement;

+ allocated to be admitted into territorial waters in order to be incorporated into drilling or production
platforms for purposes of the construction, repair, maintenance, alteration or fitting-out of such platforms,
or to connect such drilling or production platforms to the mainland; or

+ placed in a warehouse other than a bonded warehouse (excise warehouses or VAT warehouses as
described in chapter 7.1.3).

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

In principle, no VAT will have to be charged on the transfer of a commercial undertaking of all or part of the
totality of goods and/or services which qualifies as an independent branch of activity. This is in accordance
with Article 3, paragraphs 4 and 5, and Article 4, paragraph 5 of the VAT Law, which implement Articles 19
and 29 of the EU VAT Directive.

‘The Portuguese legislator has made use of the option granted to Member States to take measures necessary to
prevent distortion of competition in cases where the recipient is not wholly liable to tax (e.g. if the recipient is
a mixed taxable person applying the pro rata method), by granting the tax authorities the power to restrict the
right of deduction.

‘The Portuguese tax authorities have exercised this power by issuing a ruling (Oficio-Circulado No. 134850,
dated 21 November 1989). This ruling is a generic guideline to which the tax authorities are bound. The
conformity of this ruling with the EU VAT Directive and with Portuguese legislation is highly debatable. It
provides that, in case the recipient is not wholly liable to tax, the following should apply:

+ ifthe transferee deducts VAT on the basis of the pro rata method, although the transfer of the business as
a going concern will not be subject to VAT, the transferee will have to “adjust” (self-assess) VAT in favor
of the Portuguese State for the difference between: (i) the VAT amount that would have been assessed (by
the transferor) had the transfer been subject to VAT and (ii) the VAT amount resulting from the application
of the transferee’s pro rata to the amount referred in (i); and

* if the transferee deducts VAT on the basis of the direct allocation method, the transfer of the business as
a going concern will, or will not, be subject to VAT depending on whether the business transferred is, or
is not, respectively, allocated to a sector that does not grant the right of deduction or to a sector that does
grant such right,

Based on the above “ruling”, according to the Portuguese tax authorities:
+ if transferor and transferee are wholly liable to tax, there is no VAT on the transaction;
* if transferor and transferee are both exempt from VAT, VAT will need to be charged by the transferor;
+ iftransferor and transferee are mixed taxable persons applying the pro rata method, no VAT needs to be
charged by the transferor, but the transferee will needs to self-assess VAT on the basis of his pro rata; and
*  iftransferor and transferee are mixed taxable persons applying the direct allocation method:
* VAT will need to be charged by the transferor if the business transferred is allocated to a sector of the
transferee’s activity that does not grant the right of deduction; or

Portugal | 479

--- Page 120 ---
+ no VAT needs to be charged by the transferor (or self-assessed by the transferee) if the business
transferred is allocated to a sector of the recipient’s activity that grants the right of deduction.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

According to the general rule, free-of-charge supplies of goods are subject to VAT if the taxable person has
benefited from any deduction of input tax paid on the acquisition of those goods. However, if no input tax was
deducted on the acquisition, the allocation is not regarded as a supply of goods, as stated in Article 3(3)(f) of
the VAT Law.

There are exceptions to this rule. Free-of-charge supplies of samples and low-value business gifts are not
regarded as supplies of goods, as per Article 3(7) of the VAT Law.

Samples are defined as goods not intended for market placement, differing in format or size from the normal
sale unit, presented in substantially smaller quantities, and intended to promote the products. Specific examples
include books and publications supplied free of charge to promoters, educators, critics, cultural organizations, or
press-related entities, as well as CDs, DVDs, and other media supplied free of charge to promoters such as radio/
TV stations, critics, DJs, specialized magazines, and entertainment entities. There are distribution limits for these
samples: up to five copies of the same record for radio/TV stations and one copy of the same work or record per
year to the same beneficiary, as outlined in Article 2(3) of Implementing Order 497/2008,

Low-value business gifts are those made according to trade usage, not exceeding EUR 50 per unit and a
global annual value of 0.5% of the taxable person’s preceding calendar year turnover, as specified in Article
3(7) of the VAT Law. These gifts can include goods produced or marketed by the taxable person or purchased
trom third parties, as mentioned in Article 3(1) of Implementing Order 497/2008.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

‘The importation of goods into Portugal from a third country which, at the time of importation, are consigned

to another EU Member State is exempt from VAT. This exemption shall apply in cases where the importation

of goods is followed by the dispatch or transport of the goods to another EU Member State and the taxable
person provides:

+ his VAT identification number or the VAT identification number of his tax representative, issued in
Portugal;

+ the VAT identification number of the recipient, to whom the goods are supplied, issued in another EU
Member State, or his own VAT identification number issued in the EU Member State in which the
dispatch or transport of the goods ends; and

+ evidence that the imported goods are intended to be transported or dispatched to another EU Member
State.

Non-residents, without a fixed establishment and not registered for VAT purposes in Portugal, which use their
VAT identification number issued by another EU Member State for the import of the goods, may also benefit
from the VAT exemption referred to above, provided the import is made through a representative,

Customs duties and other levies, if any, must be paid on importation into Portugal. A prior license from
the proper tax authority is required. The intra-Community supply must be made within 30 days after the
importation.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Provided a guarantee is granted by the taxable person in favour of the tax authorities (which may vary
between 20 and 100% of the VAT due), it is possible to report the import VAT in the VAT return instead of
separate payment of import VAT to the customs authorities.

This means that VAT is remitted at the same time as when the deduction right is exercised (if the goods are
used for taxable supplies). Thus, in reality, the obligation to remit VAT does not arise at all.

480 | Portugal

--- Page 121 ---
Importers of goods may also self-assess and deduct the VAT due on those importations in the same VAT

return. To benefit from this option, the following conditions must be met by the importers:

* submit VAT returns on a monthly basis;

+ their tax situation must be duly settled (i.e. importers cannot have outstanding tax debts or they must have
challenged any outstanding debts before the Tax Authority or the Tax Courts);

+ they must only carry out transactions which are subject to, and non-exempt from VAT, or exempt
transactions with the full right of deduction (exceptions apply for ancillary real estate and financial
transactions made by the taxable person); and

+ they cannot benefit from the deferral of the payment of VAT related to prior imports.

The procedures and the deadline to opt for this regime are established in Regulation 215/2017, of 20 July 2017.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Regarding domestic supplies of goods under consignment arrangements, VAT becomes chargeable at the
time the consignee supplies the goods to a third party. This applies to both the supply by the consignor to the
consignee and the subsequent supply by the consignee to a third party. The same rule is applicable to supplies
made by a principal to its agent under commission agreements.

In respect of consignment arrangements, if the consignee does not supply the goods to a third party or return
the goods to the consignor within a I-year period from the supply by the consignor to the consignee, VAT
becomes chargeable for the latter supply on the expiry of this period.

In the case of a supply of consigned goods, invoices should be issued within 5 working days after:

+ the moment of the supply of the consigned goods to the consignee; and

+ the moment in which the VAT is due concerning those goods (this invoice should refer to the documents
issued at the time of the supply of the consigned goods).

A simplification measure applies to supplies of goods under consignment arrangements and call-off stocks

in the framework of which goods move from one Member State to another. If the simplification conditions
are met, the supplier does not perform a transfer of own goods followed by a domestic supply, but rather an
intra~Community supply to his customer. This dismisses the need for the consignor to have a VAT registration
in Portugal.

The Portuguese VAT legislation is in line with the EU VAT Directive.

3.2 Installation supplies

‘There is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly” in Portugal.

‘The place of supply of goods which are installed or assembled by or on behalf of the supplier, is the place
where the goods are installed or assembled. If a non-resident supplier installs goods at his customer's
premises in Portugal, then Portuguese VAT is due on the installation work.

The non-resident supplier is not required to register for VAT purposes and in such case the liability to account
for VAT is shifted to the customer, as a consequence of the broad definition of taxable person and broad
application of the local reverse charge adopted in Portugal (see also paragraph 8.1.1).

33 Distance sales

As of | July 2021 the distance selling threshold covers all European TBE services and distance sales of goods
and has been set at EUR 10,000.

Portugal | 481

--- Page 122 ---
Once the threshold is exceeded the supplier may either register in Portugal or in its country of establishment
via OSS to account for Portuguese VAT for its sales to Portugal.

A taxable person of another EU Member State may opt for voluntary registration in Portugal for its distance
sales before the threshold is exceeded. In that case, this option will apply for 2 calendar years.

‘Transfer of goods through the use of an electronic interface

With the entry into force of the VAT e-commerce package, on | July 2021, taxable persons who facilitate the

sale of goods through an electronic interface such as a marketplace, platform, portal or similar means, are

considered to have received and supplied those goods themselves, in the following situations:

+ ina distance sale of imported goods made through an electronic interface, if the intrinsic value of the
goods does not exceed EUR 150; and

+ ina supply of goods within the Community made by a taxable person not established here, through an
electronic interface such as a marketplace, platform, portal or similar means, to a non-taxable person.

In these cases, since two supplies take place (the supply by the supplier of the goods to the facilitator and the
supply by him to the end customer), the supply with transportation/dispatch is the one made by the facilitator
to the end customer.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Portuguese VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented broadly in line
with the EU VAT Directive.

Under the simplification regime, the middleman (party B) may shift his obligation to account for VAT in EU
Member State C to his customer in Member State party C (by way of a reverse charge). This exempts B from
registering or the need to appoint a VAT representative in Member State C.

1f Portugal is the country of arrival of the goods, Portugal does not allow the reverse charge mechanism to
apply to the supply between party B and the person to whom the subsequent supply is rendered party C, if
party B is registered for VAT purposes in Portugal. Consequently, Portuguese VAT must be applied on such
supply by party B. The wording of the Portuguese legislation, however, does not preclude the application of
the simplified triangulation rules if party B is VAT registered in the country of dispatch.

If Portugal is the Member State of arrival of the goods, the relevant domestic provisions on simplified rules

for triangulation provide, among other things, for the following:

+ there is no obligation to register for VAT or appoint a fiscal representative in Portugal if the taxable
person (party B) is established in an EU Member State (other than Portugal) and makes intra-Community
acquisitions of goods for the purpose of making subsequent supplies of these goods in Portugal;

+ if party B is registered in Portugal, the acquisition is taxable as an intra-Community acquisition under the
Portuguese VAT registration number; and

+ his supply to party C is taxable in Portugal.

If Party B is not registered in Portugal and Portugal is the Member State of arrival, party B does not have

to be registered in Portugal for his intra-Commanity acquisition and subsequent supply provided that the

following conditions are met:

* the invoice must contain the VAT identification number of party B in another Member State than the
Member State of dispatch and arrival and party C’s Portuguese VAT identification number since he is
liable for the remittance of the VAT;

+ Party B must make an explicit reference on the invoice that the triangulation rules are applicable (reverse
charge); and

+ party B must periodically report its triangular transactions in his European Sales Listing.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods (excluding new means of transport and goods subject to excise
duties) by taxable persons engaged in exempt activities and non-taxable legal persons (state, regional and

482 | Portugal

--- Page 123 ---
local government authorities and other bodies governed by public law) from suppliers established in another
EU Member State is EUR 10,000 (exclusive of VAT) for Portugal.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Portugal has implemented effective use and enjoyment rules.

‘The place of supply is shifted to Portugal if the effective use and enjoyment of the following services takes

place in Portugal and not in the non-EU country:

* hiring out of movable tangible property, with the exception of means of transport, to a person established
or domiciled outside the EU;

* short-term hiring of a means of transport to a non-taxable person; and

+ hiring out of a means of transport (other than short-term hiring out) by a non-EU company to a non-
taxable person.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied. For goods, this is the date when they are placed at the disposal of the acquirer. If
the supply of goods includes installation or assembly, the VAT becomes chargeable when the installation or
assembly is concluded, A supply of services is deemed to be carried out at the date the service is materially
executed,

‘Whenever the supply of goods or services triggers the need to issue an invoice, the VAT becomes chargeable
on the date of issuance of the invoice, If the invoice is not issued within the S working day legal deadline, the
VAT becomes chargeable on the date when the legal deadline for issuing the invoice expires.

For intra-Community acquisitions of goods, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice or on the
15" day of the month following the acquisition if no invoice has been issued by that time.

If VAT was unduly charged, this VAT becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice.

4.2. Advance payments

Where a payment is to be made on account before the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes
chargeable on receipt of the payment.

43 Continuous supplies

VAT on ongoing services becomes chargeable each time a payment is made. If no payment is made within
12 months or the periodicity for the payment is undefined, the VAT becomes chargeable after each 12-month
period

44 Cash accounting

Upon request, the tax authorities may permit a taxable person to opt to apply a cash accounting scheme for
the payment of the VAT if:

+ the total turnover in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 500,000;

+ he does not develop an exempt activity which does not grant the right to deduct input VAT;

+ he does not apply the SME Scheme;

+ he has been registered for VAT purposes in the last 12 months;

+ he does not have any tax debts to the state; and

+ he has no missed dectarative obligations.

‘This regime is applicable to all transactions made by taxable persons with the exception of:

Portugal | 483

--- Page 124 ---
+ import, export and related activities;

+ intra~Community supplies and acquisitions ;

+ — intra~Community supplies of services;

+ transactions in which a reverse charge mechanism applies; and
+ transactions involving related parties.

Where the scheme applies, output VAT is chargeable when the payment is received. However, even if the
payment is not totally or partially made by the acquirer or recipient of the goods or services, VAT becomes due:
+ in the twelfth month after the date of issue of the invoice, in the period in which such deadline ends;

+ in the period following the communication of deregistration of the regime; or

+ in the period following the communication of cancellation of activity.

However, recently, the Portuguese Government has announced plans to change the cash accounting scheme,
which will encompass companies with an annual turnover of up to EUR 2 million, compared to the current
threshold of EUR 500,000.

5 Taxable amount

51 Bad debt relief

In Portugal, bad debt relief is available to taxable persons, under specified circumstances, if they prove to
the satisfaction of the tax authorities that the amount not yet paid by the customer became a bad debt in the
relevant period

Regardless of the value of the credits owed to the supplier, the payment delay and the debtor VAT status, bad

debt relief is available for credits considered irrecoverable in:

+ anenforcement procedure, following the registration in the enforcement’s database register of the
enforcement’s extinction on the grounds that no seizable goods were found;

+ an insolvency procedure, after the insolvency is declared with a limited nature. The moment the credit
is deemed irrecoverable under an insolvency procedure (and, as a consequence, the taxable person may
benefit from the “bad debt relief”), is the moment when the insolvency is declared following a final
judgment (as res judicata) on the verification, admission and ranking of the credits, as foreseen in the
Insolvency and Corporate Recovery Code (““Cédigo da Insolvéncia e da Recuperagaio de Empresas”), or
whenever there is the ratification of the off-court insolvency plan; and

+ — special procedures which are used to revitalize and to recover financially distressed companies (“Processo
Especial de Revitalizagao” and “Regime Extrajudicial de Recuperagao de Empresas”)

‘The moment as of which a credit due is deemed irrecoverable under an insolvency procedure (and, as a

consequence, the moment at which the taxable person may benefit from the bad debt relief) is as follows:

+ in an insolvency procedure, when the insolvency is declared with a limited nature or when its end is
declared based on the insufficiency of goods according to the Insolvency and Corporate Recovery Code
(“Cédigo da Insolvéncia e da Recuperagao de Empresas”) or after the final goods assessment, from the
latter of which the definite non-payment of the credit is a result; and

+ in an insolvency or special procedure which is used to revitalize and to recover corporations in difficult
economic situations (“Processo Especial de Revitalizagéo”) whenever there is the ratification of the off-
court insolvency plan or whenever the company’s recovery plan anticipates the definite non-payment of
the credit.

For the purposes of the adjustment of VAT initially deducted, the debtor who is a taxable person must be
informed of the total or partial VAT annulment. This information/communication to the debtor must identify
the invoices, the amount of the credit due and the VAT to be adjusted, the process or agreement at stake, as
‘well as the period in which the adjustment is made.

The documents, certificates and information must be included in the taxable person’s tax file.

484 | Portugal

--- Page 125 ---
Bad debt relief is also available for credits owed to the supplier not expressly referred to above, under the

following conditions:

+ credits with a VAT-inclusive value equal to or higher than EUR 750 and unpaid for at least 6 months,
provided that the debtor is a non-taxable person or a taxable person engaged only in exempt activities that
do not grant the right to deduct input VAT;

+ credits with a VAT-inclusive value higher than EUR 750 and lower than EUR 8,000 (regardless of the
payment delay) and provided that the debtor is a non-taxable person or a taxable person engaged only in
exempt activities that do not grant the right to deduct input VAT, when:

+ the debtor is listed in the enforcement’s database register as a debtor against whom a previous
enforcement procedure has been filed which has already been suspended or abandoned on the grounds
that no seizable goods were found;

+ the debtor is listed on the public access list of expired enforcement procedures with partial payment
or on the grounds that no seizable goods were found at the moment of deduction (this will not apply if
the debtor is already listed at the moment in which the transaction takes place);

+ the order of enforcement has been appended in an injunction procedure: or

+ the credits have been recognized by judicial action;

* credits with a VAT-inclusive value lower than EUR 6,000 (regardless of the payment delay) and the
debtor is a taxable person with the right to deduct input VAT, when the credits have been recognized
by judicial action or claimed in an enforcement procedure and the debtor has been serviced by
publication. In this case, the debtor must be informed of the total or partial VAT annulment for the
purposes of the adjustment of VAT initially deducted.

The total amount of these credits, the total amount of the VAT to be deducted, the creditor's efforts to
collect the credits and the total or partial failure of such efforts, must be duly documented and certified by
a registered chartered accountant. This certification must be effected for each of the periods in which an
adjustment was made and until the expiry of the time limit to submit the VAT return, or until its submission
when submitted after that time limit.

The documents, certificates and information must be included in the taxable person’s tax file.

In any case, if, following the adjustment of VAT, the credit is fully or partially recovered, taxable persons
have to deliver the corresponding VAT to the Treasury in the period in which the credit was recovered, The
statute of limitations for the assessment/payment of VAT (4 years) does not apply.

Beside above-mentioned bad debt relief, the bad debt relief is applicable to the following credits:

* credits with a VAT-inclusive value not higher than EUR 750 which are unpaid for more than 6 months,
when the debtor is a private person or a taxpayer that performs exclusively exempt operations without
the right to deduct, The deduction may be made without the prior authorization from the Portuguese tax
authorities within 2 years counting from the following calendar year; and

* credits which are unpaid for more than 12 months for which there is clear evidence of impairment and
that efforts to collect such debts were made. In this case, there is the obligation to present, within a
period of 6 months, a prior authorization request to the Portuguese tax authorities for the bad debt VAT
adjustment, The submission of such request implies that the debtor shall be notified to provide its opinion
or to adjust the VAT in favour of the State. After 4 months, this request is deemed to be tacitly rejected
if the credit value exceeds EUR 150,000 (VAT included) or tacitly accepted, if the credit value does not
exceed EUR 150,000 (VAT included).

Whenever there is an assignment of “bad debts” after the adjustment of the VAT in favour of the assignor, and
if bad debts are subsequently (fully or partially) recovered, the assignor must adjust, in favour of the state, the
VAT previously deducted, In its turn, the assignee is entitled to request from the Portuguese tax authorities the
recovery of such tax upon submission of the appropriate application, The 4-year statute of limitation for the
assessment/payment of VAT does not apply.

--- Page 126 ---
5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods FUR 1,000
23% VAT EUR 230
Total to be paid EUR 1,230

If payment is received by us within the next 4 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

If, after the time limit for the registration of the transaction (i.e. within the period between the moment at
which the transaction takes place and until the moment of submission of the corresponding VAT return or
expiry of the time limit for its submission), its taxable amount is reduced as a consequence of granting of
bonuses or discounts, the supplier may deduct the corresponding tax until the end of the taxable period
following that in which the above-referenced circumstances have occurred.

If the customer (a taxable person) already reported the transaction in respect of which the taxable amount has
been reduced, he may rectify the deduction. He should do so before the end of the taxable period following
that in which he has received the rectifying document.

When the taxable amount has been reduced, the adjustment in favour of the supplier can only be made when
the customer has in his possession evidence that he is aware of the rectification or that the VAT has been
refunded to him, Otherwise the deduction should not be accepted.

Where the taxable amount is reduced, the amount of the reduction should be split between the consideration
and the VAT at the moment the corresponding document is issued if the adjustment of VAT is also intended.

5.3 Open market value

In respect of transactions involving connected parties, the taxable amount should be the open-market value.

This applies to the supply of goods or services where there are family or close personal ties, management,

ownership, membership, financial or legal ties, including relationships between employers and employees or

their families or any other closely connected persons,

‘The actual consideration may be replaced by the open-market value in the following scenarios:

+ lower consideration and limited deduction: When the consideration is lower than the open-market value
and the recipient of the supply is not entitled to a full deduction of input VAT;

+ exempt supply with lower consideration: When the consideration for an exempt supply is lower than the
open-market value and the supplicr is not entitled to a full deduction of input VAT; and

+ higher consideration and limited deduction; When the consideration is higher than the open-market value
and the supplier is not entitled to a full deduction of input VAT.

Deviations from the actual consideration do not apply if it can be proven that the difference between the
consideration and the open-market value is not due to the special relationship between the taxable person and
the acquirer of the goods or services.

Portuguese VAT legislation does not explicitly define “connected parties” or entities with “special
relationships.” Instead, it refers to the definition provided in Article 63(4) of the Corporate Income Tax Code,
Tlowever, the VAT Law specifically includes the relationship between an employer and employee (or the
employee’s family or any closely connected person) as a special relationship.

54 Returnable packaging

Costs associated with packing expenses and containers are excluded from the taxable amount under certain
conditions, namely: firstly, the reimbursement of these costs by the purchaser or customer must not have

been originally agreed upon. secondly, these costs must be separately itemised on the invoice; and lastly, the
invoice must clearly indicate that the packaging is returnable and that its return is required. These rules ensure
that the costs of returnable packing materials are not included in the taxable amount, provided the specified
conditions are met.

486 | Portugal

--- Page 127 ---
6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate

The standard rates in Portugal are 23% for the mainland, 22% for the Autonomous Region of Madeira and
16% for the Autonomous Region of the Azores.

6.2 Reduced rate

In Portugal there are 5 reduced rates:

+ the intermediate rates of 13% for the mainland, 12% for the Autonomous Region of Madeira and 9% for
the Autonomous Region of Azores; and

+ the reduced rates of 6% for the mainland, 4% for the Autonomous Regions of Madeira and 4% for the
Autonomous Region of Azores.

The intermediate rates apply for instance, to:

+ listed foodstuffs, including table wines; and

+ coloured and marked diesel fuel, traded under the conditions and for the purposes legally defined, and
fuel oil and respective blends.

The reduced rates apply, for instance, to:

+ listed foodstuffs, including, among others, cereals, bread, meat, fish, milk, fresh and dehydrated fruit and
vegetables;

+ pharmaceutical products;

* passenger transport; and

* hotel accommodation,

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rate

Portugal has not implemented a super-reduced or a zero rate other than the ones mentioned in chapter 7.1.

Ps Exemptions
TA Exemptions with the right to deductionPortugal has zero rates for the following supplies and
services:

* exports and intra~Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied to travellers resident outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops;

* services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the EU;

* _ services of intermediaries relating to transactions carried out outside the EU;

* sea vessels and aircraft and related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with (diplomatic) tax immunity;

+ supplies of monetary gold to the Portuguese central bank;

+ transport of people and goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

* — international and intra-Community passenger transport by seagoing vessels and airplane;

+ goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ services to goods under customs control;

+ goods and services supplied to exporters; and

+ domestic sales of goods made by suppliers to national exporters of goods established in Portugal, under
certain requirements.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

An intra-Community supply of goods may be zero-rated in the following circumstances:
+ the customer is registered for VAT purposes in another EU Member State and has a valid VAT

identification number;
Portugal | 487

--- Page 128 ---
+ the customer’s VAT identification number is obtained and recorded in the supplicr’s records;

+ the customer’s VAT identification number and the supplier’s VAT identification number are quoted on the
sales invoice;

+ the intra-Community supply is reported in the EC sales listing;

+ the goods are dispatched or transported to another EU Member State; and

+ evidence that the goods have left Portugal is retained

In order to demonstrate that the goods were transported to another EU Member State or exported, the supplier
must keep evidence that the goods have left the country, such as a copy of the sales invoice, proof of payment,
correspondence with the customer (if available) or transport documents. Suppliers who cannot provide
sufficient evidence are held liable for Portuguese VAT at the applicable rate. Proof of intra-Community
transport is presumed in the following cases:
+ supplier possesses two items of non-contradictory evidence, consisting in documents relating to the
dispatch or transport of the goods (such as a signed CMR document or note, a bill of lading, an airfreight
invoice or an invoice from the carrier of the goods); or
* one item of evidence consisting in a document relating to the dispatch or transport of the goods (such
asa signed CMR document or note, a bill of lading, an airfreight invoice or an invoice from the
carrier of the goods) and one of the following documents that is not contradictory:

+ an insurance policy with regard to the dispatch or transport of the goods;

+ bank documents proving payment for the dispatch or transport of the goods;

+ official documents issued by a public authority, such as a notary, confirming the arrival of the goods in
the Member State of destination; and

+ areccipt issued by a warehouse keeper in the Member State of destination, confirming the storage of
the goods in that Member State.

The two items of evidence required shall be issued by two parties independent of each other and of the
supplier and the acquirer (non-related to each other, nor to the supplier or the acquirer). When the goods are
transported or dispatched by the acquirer or by a third party acting on its behalf, the seller must be in the
possession of:
* awritten statement from the acquirer (to be issued until the 10th day of the month following the
date where the goods were placed at its disposal) stating that the goods have been dispatched or
transported by the acquirer. The statement should also contain other elements.

The presumption that the dispatch or transport of the goods has taken place may be refuted by the Tax and
Customs Authority if it has information contradicting the veracity of the evidence presented. In that case, the
taxable person must demonstrate that the conditions for the application of the exemption are met.

7.1.2 Export supplies

Exports are zero-rated, entitling the taxable person to a deduction of the related input tax previously paid. The
export of goods must be evidenced by appropriate customs documents or, if there is no legal intervention of
customs authoritics, a statement issued by the customer of the goods, indicating the destination they will be
given. Suppliers who cannot provide sufficient evidence are held liable for Portuguese VAT at the applicable
rate,

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Exempted frm Portuguese VAT ae:
imports of goods into Portugal which are intended to be placed under the VAT warehousing regime;

+ supplies of goods that are intended to be placed under the VAT warehousing regime, as well as supplies of
services related thereto; and

+ supplies of goods or services carried out under the VAT warehousing regime (and where this regime still
applies).

For the purposes of the above-mentioned exemption which are applicable to supplies of goods that are
intended to be placed under the VAT warehousing regime, VAT warehouses include:

488 | Portugal

--- Page 129 ---
+ authorized locations pursuant to Article 21 of the Excise Duty Code, for excisable goods; and
+ authorized locations pursuant to the applicable legislation, for non-excisable goods,

With respect to non-excisable goods, the authorization can only be granted for goods listed in Annex C to the
VAT Law (similar to Annex V to the EU VAT Directive) that are not intended to be supplied at the retail stage
and provided that these goods already benefit from the customs warehousing regime.

In spite of the above, the VAT warehousing regime may also apply to goods in the following cases:

* where the goods are intended to be supplied, within the airport or maritime terminal, to travellers to
another EU Member State or to a third country;

+ where the goods are intended to be supplied on board of an aircraft or a ship in the course of a flight or
sea crossing where the place of supply is situated in another Member State or outside the EU; and

+ where the goods are intended for taxable persons for the purposes of carrying out exempt supplies under
Article 14 Paragraph 1 (I to m) of the VAT Law (similar to Article 151 Paragraph | (a to c) of the EU VAT
Directive).

Portuguese VAT becomes due when the goods are released from the VAT warehouse and is levied from the
person that releases the goods from the VAT warehousing regime. The taxable amount includes the value
of any exempt transactions that may eventually be carried out while the goods were kept under the VAT
warehouse regime (Article No. 15 paragraph 6 of the VAT Law).

7.1.3.2. Excise warehouse
Portugal has no excise warehouses.

7.1.3.3. Bonded (customs) warehouse
It is possible to put goods into a bonded warehouse in Portugal.

If the goods are brought from outside the EU into a bonded warehouse in Portugal, no importation takes
place. However, the release of the goods from the bonded warehouse into free circulation in Portugal is
considered an import.

Supplies of goods and related services from and in bonded warehouses (as well as customs-free zones) are
treated as zero rated, provided that such goods are not used or consumed within these areas,

If imported goods are under the bonded warehouse procedure, the payment of VAT is effectively deferred
as the entry into free circulation is deferred. However, if imported goods are subject to customs duties,

to agricultural levies or to charges having equivalent effect established under a common policy, and the
chargeable event occurs, then the VAT becomes chargeable when the chargeable event in respect of those
duties occurs.

7.3 Option for taxation

The option to tax is available, namely, for the following exempt supplies:

recognized by the relevant Ministries as having expertise in this domain;

+ medical and sanitary services and strictly related transactions made by hospitals, clinics, health care

Health Service (except services provided by private hospitals which are deemed to be supplied under
social conditions comparable to those of public hospitals - i.e. at prices fixed by public contracts and
whose costs are partially borne by the social security institutions of that Member State), within the scope
of the health system as provided under its basic law;

+ services provided by cooperatives that develop activities for their farming members; and

+ employer-supplied meals and drinks to employees

The supplier may exercise the option to tax in the commencement or (as applicable) amendment of activity
declaration to be presented in any tax office. The option to tax produces effects immediately after the
presentation of that declaration and remains valid for a minimum 5 years period.

‘The supplier of immovable property may also waive the right to the exemption provided that the following

+ the supply refers to the acquisition of the proprietorship of urban immovable property, fractions thereof or
building land;

+ the customer is also a taxable person who will use immovable property, totally or mainly, in activities

the cost of renovation or transformation can still be deducted, in part or in full, as input VAT; or

+ a transaction which was subject to VAT, provided that the 20-year period for adjustment of the initial
deduction of the VAT on the cost of purchase or construction of the property has not yet expired
(Article 12 (5) and (6) of the VAT Law and Circular Letter 30099 of 9 February 2007).

The supplier must request and obtain a certificate in order to be able to waive the exemption. In such a case,
VAT must be assessed by the customer.

The VAT exemption on the letting of immovable property can also be waived, The option for taxation of the
letting of immovable property is subject to the same rules as those referred to for the supply of immovable
property. It is also required that the annual rent be in an amount equal to or higher than 1/25 of the acquisition
or construction value. The lessor must request and obtain a certificate in order to be able to opt for taxation.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse Charges

In Portugal there are several domestic reverse charges asides from the reverse charge for general B2B
supplies of services and domestic supplies of goods provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons
and legal entities established in Portugal. There is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and
electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Portugal (Article 195 EU VAT Directive).

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
In Portugal, the reverse charge mechanism is mandatorily applied to all supplies of goods and/or services

subject to VAT, provided by taxable persons who do not have a head office, fixed establishment, domicile, or
fiscal representative in Portugal, to a taxable person established in Portugal.

According to a literal interpretation of the VAT Law, the reverse charge mechanism only applies if the
recipients are:

+ taxable persons established in Portugal; or

* taxable persons who have appointed a VAT representative in Portugal.

Therefore, the law does not foresee the application of the reverse charge to transactions where a non-resident
supplier provides goods or services to a non-resident taxable person in Portugal (without a tax representative
in Portugal) who only has a VAT registration in Portugal.

In FAQs released on the rules applicable to non-resident taxable persons, the Tax Authorities clarified that the
reverse charge does not apply to domestic supplies carried out by a non-resident taxable person to another
non-resident taxable person. However, these FAQs did not expressly address situations where the non-resident
customer, unlike the non-resident supplier, is already VAT registered in Portugal. Furthermore, the guidance
from the Tax Authorities suggests minimising situations that require VAT registrations in Portugal. Therefore,
it can be argued that in cases where the customer is a non-resident company VAT registered in Portugal

and the supplier is not, the customer should self-assess the VAT due under the reverse charge mechanism.
Consequently, the supplier would not be required to register for VAT in Portugal. However, it is recommended
to seek confirmation from the Tax Authorities to ensure compliance.

On the invoice reference must be made to the application of the reverse charge, as follows: “IVA —
Autoliquidagao” (VAT — self-assessment),

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

On the following supplies and/or services domestic reverse charge rules apply:

+ construction work, including repair, cleaning, maintenance, alteration and demolition services in relation
to immovable property, including the supply of staff engaged in those activities;

+ as of I January 2020, supplies of forestry goods including cork, wood, pine cones and pine nuts with
bark;

* immovable property under the option for taxation;

+ old used materials, scrap, waste and specific services;

* C02 Emission Rights; and

+ investment gold (whenever the taxable person opts for taxation).

A supplier must refer to the reverse charge. The following text should be mentioned on the invoice: “IVA
Autoliquidagao” or (VAT — self-assessment).

8.2 Joint and several liability

Several provisions for joint and several liability ensure the proper remittance of VAT to the tax authorities.
Firstly. a VAT representative appointed by a non-resident entity is jointly liable for the remittance of VAT due
to the tax authorities.

Portugal | 491

--- Page 132 ---
Additionally, the recipient of taxable goods and services, who is a taxable person, may be jointly liable with
the supplier for the remittance of VAT if the mandatory invoice has not been issued or contains incorrect
information, This includes errors in the name and address of the parties, the description and quantity of goods
or services supplied, the price or the amount of VAT duc. However, the recipient can waive this liability by
providing evidence that he has paid the VAT due to the supplier, either in full or partially. The waiver applies
to the amount of tax paid.

Moreover, the recipient may also be jointly liable with the supplier for VAT due in cases of sham transactions.
Furthermore, any taxable person involved in the supply of goods or services with the intention of not
remitting VAT to the state may be held liable for VAT remittance if the person has or should have knowledge
of the situation. There is a legal rebuttable presumption that the taxable person has knowledge that the VAT
due will not be fully remitted to the state if the price paid for the goods or services is lower than the lowest
price reasonably expected in a competitive market.

‘These rules are designed to ensure compliance and prevent VAT evasion by holding various parties
accountable for the proper remittance of VAT.

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

‘The input VAT is not recoverable in case of:

+ vehicles for passenger transport (motor cars, yachts, helicopters, aircraft and motorcycles), including

+ fuel for motor vehicles. However, in respect to diesel oil, liquefied petroleum gas (LPG), natural gas and

used for public transport vehicles (excluding rent-a-car), heavy vehicles for passenger transport and
machines which run on the public roads with a special licence plate but are not treated as vehicles;

+ travel, including toll fees;

* accommodation, food, drinks and tobacco, However (with the exception to tobacco), VAT is recoverable for:
+ 100% if the expenses refer to the provision of lodging, meals and drinks made by the taxable person

to his staff in canteens, dormitories and similar facilities,

+ 50%, when incurred for the purpose of the organisation of congresses, fairs and exhibitions, provided
that they are incurred to meet the participants’ direct needs, that they are directly contracted with the
service supplier(s) or through legally qualified entities and that they clearly contribute to carry out
supplies subject to VAT, or

+ 25%, when incurred for the purpose of the participation in these events, provided that they are directly
contracted with conference organisers and that they clearly contribute to carry out supplies subject to
VAT. However, entities that organize fairs, congresses and other similar events are entitled to a refund
of the amount equal to the VAT incurred and not deductible related to the organization of congresses,
fairs, exhibitions, seminars, conferences and similar events. This refund request must be submitted by
the beneficiary through the website of the Instituto do Turismo de Portugal, .P. (Portuguese Tourism
Institute, Public Institute), and accessible through a link on the ePortugal portal, from the second
month following the issuance of the supporting documents until the end of 1 year from the date of
issuance, and must refer to monthly periods, encompassing all supporting documents. The refund
request will be analysed by the Turismo de Portugal, LP. within a period of 90 days, counting from
the date of submission by the beneficiary. This benefit was initially approved by Decree-Law 54/2020,

ona temporary basis to be in force relating to expenses incurred between 12 August 2020 and 31

Under the Environmental Taxation Reform, approved by Law 82-D/2014 of 31 December 2014, which

92 Excess input VAT

Input VAT is generally deducted in the VAT return of the tax period in which the right of deduction has arisen.
Excess input VAT in a given tax period, must be carried forward to the following periods. However, a refund
may be claimed under the following circumstances:
+ after a 12-month period, if excess input VAT exceeds EUR 250; or
+ before that period if:

* excess input VAT exceeds EUR 3,000; or

* excess input VAT exceeds EUR 25, if the taxable person ceases his activity or leaves the normal

taxation regime.

The Portuguese tax authorities may require that, when the amount to be refunded exceeds EUR 30,000, the
taxable person presents a bank guarantee or any other appropriate security, which should be maintained for a
6-month period.

‘The Minister of Finance may also authorize the tax authorities to give a refund to taxable persons on
conditions other than the ones referred to above (e.g. if the turnover of those taxable persons is mainly
obtained from certain transactions, such as exports).

9.3 Adjustment periods

+ Immovable property - 19 years following the year of first use;
+ Movable property - 4 years following the year of first use

Portugal does not have an adjustment period for services.

o4 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

Permission to obtain a refund of Portuguese VAT is granted to EU taxable persons who, during the period for
which the application for refund is made, satisfy the following conditions:
+ they do not have a head office, a fixed establishment, domicile or usual residence in Portugal from where
services are supplied; and
+ they have not carried out any taxable transactions within the Portuguese territory other than the following:
+ _ transport and services ancillary thereto (including commission, packing and insurance) the value of
which is added to the value of imported goods as incidental expenses incurred up to the first place
of destination of such goods in Portugal or which are related to exports of goods, intra-Community
supplies or transactions covered by special warehousing procedures; or
* supplies of goods and services for which the tax liability is shifted to the resident customer under the
reverse charge mechanism.

The application for a refund must relate to goods or services purchased, acquired or imported in mainland

Portugal or the Autonomous Regions of Madeira and Azores during the period for which the claim is made.
‘Those goods and services must be used for conducting taxable transactions for which VAT is deductible

--- Page 134 ---
‘The VAT refund request must refer lo the previous calendar year as long as the amount refiundable is not
lower than EUR 50, Nevertheless, a refund application regarding an ongoing year can be filed if it refers to at
least 3 consecutive months and the amount refundable exceeds EUR 400.

Arefund of an amount referring lo a period of less than 3 months is also possible as long as the period ends
on 31 December of the previous calendar year and the amount to be refunded exceeds EUR 50. The deadline
for filing the application is 30 September of the ycar following the one in which the tax was due. This
application must be made by electronic means.

After the VAT refund request is presented, it is possible to submit a replacement request (which will imply
the annulment of the former request), provided the latest application is filed before 30 September of the year
following the one in which the tax was due.

‘The Portuguese tax authority has a 4 month term, counting from the time of receipt of the request, to decide
whether to grant the refund (this term can be extended to 6 or 8 months where the Portuguese tax authority
requests additional information). If these deadlines are not respected, the refund claim is deemed to be
(tacitly) rejected.

During the time of evaluation of the request, the Portuguese tax authority may request certain information
from the applicant (or even third parties) which may include the original or copies of the invoices or relevant
import documents.

Refunds must be paid within 10 working days counting from the notification of the decision to grant the
refund (if this deadline is not met, late payment interest is assessed and paid by the Portuguese tax authority).

If the request for refund is denied, the applicant may present a claim, within 120 days after being notified
of the decision, to the Refund Services Director or start a legal action at the first instance tax court, within 3
months after being notified of the decision.

95 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

VAT refund rules have been made available for taxable persons established in third countries under
reciprocity arrangements for Portuguese taxable persons (there is no official list of countries that satisfy the
reciprocity requirement) and under the requirement to appoint a duly empowered fiscal representative who is
a resident of Portugal. The fiscal representative must comply with all VAT procedural formalities and is held
jointly and severally liable for any unduly obtained refunds of VAT.

The request for a refund may be presented by paper or clectronic means within the same periods and
deadlines (which depend on the amount of VAT to be refunded) as mentioned above for applicants from the
EU.

Non-EU applicants are required to present (besides the original invoices and any import documents) an
official certificate issued by the state where they are established, proving that they are subject to a general
tax on turnover in that state and the recognition of a reciprocity treatment for taxable persons established in
Portugal (this requirement is waived if there is an agreement between Portugal and the state concerned).

The refund of the tax must be paid by the end of the 6" month following the presentation of the request (the
deadline is measured from the date on which all the documents required by the Portuguese tax authority are
presented or from the date of the receipt of the application if it is made by electronic means).

If the request for refund is denied, the applicant may present a claim, within 120 days after being notified

of the decision, to the Refund Services Director or start a legal action at the first instance tax court, within 3
months after being notified of the decision.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ a single document code (from 1 January 2022);

+ atwo-dimensional barcode (QR code) (from 1 January 2022);

+ date of issue of the invoice;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ name and address of the supplier or service provider;

+ VAT identification number under which the supply or services is performed;

+ name and address of the customer or recipient;

+ VAT identification number of the customer;

* name, address and VAT identification number of the fiscal representative (if applicable);

+ the country prefix, in accordance with the ISO-3166 alfa 2 code of the customer in the case of intra-
Community supplies (e.g. “PT”, “BE”, “DE”, “ES,”, “FR”, “GB”, etc.);

+ the quantity and a clear description of the goods or services supplied, including in particular, all
characteristics necessary to determine the applicable VAT rate. Packages not included in the transaction
should be referred to separately, with an express reference to the fact that it has been agreed that they will
be returned;

+ — date of supply of goods or the period of supply of services as well as the date(s) on which any advance
payment falls due if different from the date of invoice:

* place where the supply ends, for (zero rated) intra-Community supplies;

* price (VAT exclusive) and all other elements included in the taxable amount;

+ the applied VAT rates;

+ the VAT amount;

+ where an exemption applies (i.e. no VAT is charged), the reason of the exemption;

+ in case of self-billing the following reference is required: “autofaturagao” (self-billing);

+ _ incase of transactions following special scheme regimes, the following references are required (as.
applicable): “Regime da margem de lucro - Agéncias de viagens” (tour operator margin scheme),
“Regime da margem de lucro - bens em segunda mao” (margin scheme - second-hand goods), “Regime
da margem de lucro - objetos de arte” (margin scheme - works of art ), “Regime da margem de lucro
- objetos de colegio ¢ antiguidades” (margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques), “IVA - regime
forfetario” (common flat-rate scheme for farmers); and

+ where the customer/recipient is liable for the payment of the VAT, the reference “IVA — Autoliquidago”
(VAT ~ self-assessment).

If the customer is a private person, his name, address and tax/VAT identification number is only mandatory if

requested by the customer.

10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice must be issued within 5 working days counting from the date on which VAT becomes due.
For continuous supplies of services at least once per year an invoice must be issued.

For (zero rated) intra-Community supplies of goods, invoices must be issued within 15 days counting from
the month following that in which the goods are made available to the customer/recipient.

For intra-Community B2B services taxable in the territory of another EU Member State, invoices must be
issued within 15 days counting from the date in which VAT becomes due.

In relation to payments made before the actual supply of goods or services or to payments made before VAT
becomes due, invoices need to be issued on the date of receipt of such payments and for the amount received.
This obligation does not apply in relation to the payments made before the actual intra~Community supply of
goods takes place. With respect to these supplies, the invoice must be issued within 15 days counting from

Portugal 1 495

--- Page 136 ---
the month following the one in which the goods are made available to the customer, for the full amount of the
intra-Community supply of goods (regardless of the fact whether an advance payment has been made or not),

10.1.3 | Currency

As a general rule, the invoices should be issued in EUR. Despite this, the issuance of invoices in foreign
currency is allowed (see paragraph 10.1.4).

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Where the factors used to determine the taxable amount are expressed in a currency other than EUR, the
exchange rate shall be determined in accordance with the indicative tables released by the European Central
Bank or in accordance with the selling rates recorded by any bank established in Portugal, As an alternative,
the taxable person may also opt to use the exchange rate verified at the time the VAT was due or on the first
business day of the respective month.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Portuguese VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed provided that: (i) there is a written agreement
between the supplier of the goods or services and the customer, (ii) the customer proves that the supplier of
the goods or services acknowledged the issue of the invoices and accepted their content and (iii) the invoices
contain the following reference: “autofaturacao” (self-billing).

If the customer docs not have its head office, fixed establishment or domicile within the EU, self- billing
is subject to the Portuguese tax authorities’ prior authorization. The Portuguese tax authorities may require
certain conditions to be met for the purposes of authorizing self-billing.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

Simplified invoices can be used in relation (i) to supplies of goods made by retailers or peddlers to non-
taxable persons, where the amount of the invoice does not exceed EUR 1,000 and (ii) to other supplies of
goods and services, where the amount of the invoice does not exceed EUR 100.

Simplified invoices must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ name and address of the supplicr or service provider;

+ VAT ID number under which the supply or services is performed;

+ the quantity and description of the nature of the goods supplied or services rendered;

+ the price (VAT exclusive), the applied VAT rates and the VAT amount, or the price (VAT inclusive) and the
applied VAT rates; and

+ where an exemption applies (i.e. no VAT is charged), the reason of the exemption and if the customer or
recipient is a taxable person, the VAT ID number of the customer or recipient; otherwise whenever the
recipient so requires it.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices
Invoices and other documents must be stored for 10 years.

Invoices should, in principle, be stored in the original form, i.e. received paper invoices must be stored in
paper form and electronically received invoices must be stored in electronic form. However, if the taxable
person wishes to keep a digital archive, it is possible to digitalize and electronically store invoices which have
been issued and received in paper form as long as certain requirements are met.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Taxable persons may choose any form for the electronic invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin,
integrity of content and readability are guaranteed through business controls which create reliable audit trails
between the invoices and the supplies of goods or services. A customer is allowed to refuse an electronic
invoice.

Electronic invoicing is mandatory in the context of public tenders and public contracts,

496 | Portugal

--- Page 137 ---
10.1.9 Real-time reporting

Both resident and non-resident companies with a local VAT registration are required to communicate invoices

and other relevant tax documents to the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority, regardless of their value. The

communication process can be accomplished in one of the following three ways:

+ Electronic transmission of data using a standard structured file based on the Standard Audit File for Tax
(SAE-T file): Companies can export the SAF-T file and upload it to the Portuguese Tax and Customs
Authority’s website;

+ Transmission in real time through the webservice channel;

+ Direct insertion of the data on the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority’s website. This option is
available for micro-sized businesses.

It is important to note that failure to comply with this obligation can result in. As such, it is crucial for
companies with a local VAT registration to ensure that they are reporting their invoices and other tax-relevant
documents using one of the three methods provided by the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority.

Given the practical complexity of transmitting in real time through the webservice channel and directly
inserting invoice data into the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority’s website, and considering that most
certified invoicing software includes the capacity.

In Portugal. businesses must submit the SAF-T file on a monthly basis (regardless of VAT return deadlines)
for all months in which invoices and other tax-relevant documents have been issued. Irrespective of market
segments (B2C, B2B, or B2G) or geographical origin and destination (domestic, intra-EU, or extra-EU
transactions), the Portuguese SAF-T system covers all transactions carried out by taxable persons.

Portugal was the first country to implement the OECD's SAF-T file, in 2009. Initially only submitted upon
the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority’s request linked to a tax audit, the obligation to send the SAF-T
oma periodic basis was introduced in 2013. Since then, several adjustments to the data structure of the SAF-T
file have been made, with the latest amendment introduced by Ordinance no. 302/2016 of 2 December 2016.

Starting in 2025, companies will also be required to submit an annual SAF-T file on accounting data which
will then be used for the pre-filling of the IES (Simplified Business Information) return.

In addition to the obligation to submit transactional information digitally to the Portuguese Tax and Customs
Authority, and in line with the European Union’s efforts to harmonize e-invoicing adoption in public
procurement, Portugal has enforced mandatory e-invoicing in the B2G context beginning in 2021. The
Public Administration Shared Services Entity (Entidade de Servigos Partilhados da Administragdo Publica) is
responsible for the implementation and management of B2G e-invoicing in Portugal.

Currently, outside the B2G context, e-invoicing is merely optional and dependent on the acceptance of the
recipient, and there is still no forecast for the transition to mandatory B2B and B2C e-invoicing.

10.2 VAT Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

VAT returns must be submitted:

* ona monthly basis, if the annual turnover in the preceding calendar year was equal to or exceeded EUR
650,000; and

+ ona quarterly basis, if the annual turnover in the preceding calendar year was lower than EUR 650,000.

However, taxable persons whose annual turnover in the preceding calendar year did not exceed EUR 650,000,
may opt to submit VAT returns on a monthly basis (this option is applicable for a minimum of a 3-year
period).

There are no differences for foreign and local businesses regarding the period for VAT returns. Electronic
filing is mandatory.

Portugal | 497

--- Page 138 ---
10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment

Monthly VAT returns must be submitted before the 20" day of the second month following the tax period (month)
to which the transactions refer to, except for the VAT return for June, which is due by 20 September.

Quarterly VAT returns must be submitted before the 20" day of the second month following the tax period
(quarter) to which the transactions refer to, except for the VAT return for the second quarter, which is due by
20 September.

Taxable persons must ensure that the VAT due is paid (i) for monthly VAT returns, before the 25" day of the
second month following the tax period (month) the transactions refer to, except for the VAT due for the period
of June, which must be paid until 25 September; and (ii) for quarterly VAT returns, before the 25" day of

the second month following the tax period (quarter) the transactions refer to, except for the VAT due for the
second quarter, which must be paid before 25 September.

An explanatory note to the VAT return “Declaragfio Periédica” may be found on the site of the Portuguese tax
authorities, through the following link: Declaracao periédica de IVA (portaldasfinancas.gov.pt).

10.2.3 European sales listing
Resident taxable persons making intra-Community supplies of goods or services must file a recapitulative
statement containing the VAT identification numbers of their customers, the total value of the goods or
services supplied to them in the reporting period and any triangular transactions. The recapitulative statements
must be filed electronically:

+ by the 20" day of the first month following the transactions in the case of taxable persons with monthly
accounting periods or in the case of taxable persons with quarterly periods when the total amount of the
relevant operations in that quarter or in any of the 4 preceding quarters exceeds EUR 50,000; or

+ by the 20" day of the first month following the quarter, where the transactions were carried out by taxable
persons with quarterly periods when the limit referred to above is not exceeded.

Electronic filing is mandatory.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

Persons who are subject to Personal Income Tax or Corporate Income Tax who must keep their accounts
organized must file a yearly statement with the relevant tax and accountancy information (“Declaragao
Annual/ IES).

This annual statement must be filed electronically before 15 July of the following year (or until the 15" of the
seventh month after the authorized year end if the taxable person’s tax year is not the calendar year). The annual
lists of suppliers or customers (which are submitted if the total amount of internal transactions with each of them
in the preceding year exceeded EUR 25,000) are included in this annual and accounting tax return.

Electronic filing is mandatory.
An explanatory note to the Annual Statement “Declaragdo Anual” may be found on the site of the Portuguese
tax authorities, through the following link: IES - Declaragdo anual (portaldasfinancas.gov.pt).

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

Asa rule, the VAT assessment period in Portugal is 4 years counted from the beginning of the calendar year
following that in which VAT became chargeable, This means that in the year 2025, the last open year for
assessment is the year 2021.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for Jate registration

Ifa late registration occurs, the Portuguese tax authorities can issue a penalty that may vary between:
+ — for legal persons — EUR 600 (except in case of reduction) and EUR 7,500 (in case of negligence) or EUR
15,000 (in case of deliberate infringement); and

498 | Portugal

--- Page 139 ---
+ for natural persons ~ EUR 300 (except in case of reduction) and EUR 3,750 (in case of negligence) or
EUR 7,500 (in case of deliberate infringement)

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If VAT returns and European sales listing are not filed on time, the penalty may vary between:

+ for legal persons ~ EUR 300 (except in case of reduction) and EUR 3,750 (in case of negligence) or FUR
7,500 (in case of deliberate infringement); and

+ for natural persons - EUR 150 (except in case of reduction) and EUR 1,875 (in case of negligence) or
EUR 3,750 (in case of deliberate infringement).

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time, the Portuguese tax authorities can issue a penalty of 30 to 100% of the VAT

due (for legal persons) or of 15 to 50% (for natural persons), in case of negligence, being:

+ minimum — EUR 50 (or EUR 25, in case of reduction of the penalty); and

+ maximum — EUR 165,000 for legal persons and EUR 82,500 for natural persons, in case of deliberate
infringement; or EUR 45,000 for legal persons and EUR 22,500 for natural persons, in case of negligence.

As of 1 January 2025, a 8.309% (yearly rate counted on a daily basis on the VAT amount that has not been
paid) late payment interest is also payable until the date of payment of the tax debt.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If the VAT is not paid up to the correct amount, the VAT authorities can also issue penalties, as already
mentioned.

A4% (yearly rate, counted on a daily basis on the VAT amount that has not been assessed) compensatory
interest is due for the late assessment of VAT. The maximum accrual period for compensatory interest is 180
days.

If the VAT is paid on the basis of a voluntarily disclosure, the taxable person may benefit from a scale of
reductions from 12.5% (provided that (i) payment of the reduced penalty is requested within 30 days counting
from the infringement and (ii) a tax audit has not been launched) up to 75% (provided that (i) payment of the
reduced penalty due is requested before the end of the tax audit and (ii) the infringement is merely negligent)
of the minimum legal penalty. Under certain circumstances, a waiver of penalties may also be granted.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

As referred to above, generally whenever there is a deliberate infringement, the penalty framework applied to
the transgressors is doubled.

‘The Portuguese tax authorities may also decide to prosecute for a criminal offence, whenever the
transgressive behaviour falls within one of the crimes established by law.

10.4 Intrastat

In Portugal, separate Intrastat returns need to be submitted to the National Statistical Institute (Instituto
Nacional de Estatistica or INE).
10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

The Intrastat threshold for arrivals is EUR 650,000 (if based on the mainland or the Azores). The threshold is
EUR 50,000 if based on Madeira.

The Intrastat threshold for dispatches is EUR 600,000 (if based on the mainland or the Azores). The threshold
is EUR 50,000 if based on Madeira.

10.4.2 Intrastat data te be reported

The information which needs to be reported is the following:

+ nature of the movement of the goods (ie. arrival or dispatch);

+ reference period (i.e. the reporting year and month);

+ VAT ID number of the entity responsible for the information reported;

Portugal | 499

--- Page 140 ---
+ VAT ID number of the customer;

+ VAT LD number of a third party reporting the information;

+ code of the country of dispatch or destination;

+ code of the country of origin;

= code of the region of destination or origin;

+ code of the delivery conditions;

+ code of the nature of the transaction;

+ code of the means of transport;

+ code of the port or airport of loading or unloading;

+ H&S Code (8-digit harmonised commodity code according to the Combined Nomenclature (CN));

+ net weight in kilograms;

+ exact supplementary units according to the HS code;

+ invoice value of the goods;

+ statistical value; and

+ place, date, legible name and signature of the responsible for the information reported or of the third party
reporting the information.

An explanatory note to the Intrastat return may be found on the site of the Instituto Nacional de Estatistica,
through the following link: htips://inyurl.com/yo2xh9fz

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

Portugal has not implemented any additional compliance requirements.
